0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-30.30276pt\raise-31.72221pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})^{\mathrm{gr}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 34.21939pt\raise-26.13692pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.5853pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{gr}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 54.30276pt\raise-31.72221pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 54.30276pt\raise-31.72221pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{E}^{\mathrm{gr}}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ .

(2.25)

We argue that the right vertical arrow is fully faithful. In other words,
the
E
-theory for graded
C^{*}
-algebras extends the
E
-theory for ungraded ones. To this end, for
A,B
in
\mathrm{E}
, we consider the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathtt{map}}_{\mathrm{E}^{\mathrm{gr}}}(\mathrm{e}^{C_{2}}({\mathcal{S}})\stimes\mathrm{triv}(A),\mathrm{e}^{C_{2}}({\mathcal{S}})\stimes\mathrm{triv}(B))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathtt{map}}_{\mathrm{E}}(A,B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathtt{map}}_{\mathrm{E}^{C_{2}}}(\mathrm{Res}_{C_{2}}(A),\mathrm{Res}_{C_{2}}(B))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathtt{map}}_{\mathrm{E}^{C_{2}}}(\mathrm{e}^{C_{2}}({\mathcal{S}})\stimes\mathrm{triv}(A),\mathrm{triv}(B))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathtt{map}}_{\mathrm{E}}(A,B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathtt{map}}_{\mathrm{E}}((\mathrm{e}^{C_{2}}({\mathbb{C}})\rtimes C_{2})\otimes A,B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathtt{map}}_{\mathrm{E}}((\mathrm{e}^{C_{2}}({\mathcal{S}})\rtimes C_{2})\otimes A,B)\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq{,}\eqref{gweroighjweoirfwerfw}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Res}_{C_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\epsilon\rtimes C_{2})^{*}}

where the two unnamed vertical equivalences are given by the dual Green-Julg adjunction
(see e.g.
[
BD24
, Prop. 3.61.2]
).
One can further check using the explicit description of the unit of the dual Green-Julg adjunction that the map maked by
\pi^{*}

is induced by the homomorphism
\pi:{\mathbb{C}}\rtimes C_{2}\to{\mathbb{C}}
induced by the trivial representation of
C_{2}
.
The dotted arrow is an equivalence since by
[
BE17
, Lem. 7.3]

the composition

\mathrm{e}^{C_{2}}({\mathcal{S}})\rtimes C_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\epsilon\rtimes C_{2}}}{{\to}}\mathrm{e}^{C_{2}}({\mathbb{C}})\rtimes C_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi}}{{\to}}\mathrm{e}({\mathbb{C}})

is an equivalence.
We can conclude that the dashed arrow
is an equivalence as desired.

We use the graded
E
-theory in order to define the
K
-theory functor for graded
C^{*}
-algebras.

Definition 2.47
.

We define the lax symmetric monoidal
K
-theory functor for graded
C^{*}
-algebras as the composition

K^{\mathrm{gr}}:(C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})^{\mathrm{gr}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{gr}}}\mathrm{E}^{\mathrm{gr}}\xrightarrow{{\mathtt{map}}_{\mathrm{E}^{\mathrm{gr}}}(\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{gr}}({\mathbb{C}}),-)}{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)\ .

We further define the lax symmetric monoidal
K
-theory functor for
C^{*}
-algebras by

K:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{triv}}(C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})^{\mathrm{gr}}\xrightarrow{K^{\mathrm{gr}}}{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)\ .

Since the right vertical arrow in (
2.25
) is fully faithful
we have
K(-)\simeq\mathrm{E}({\mathbb{C}},-)
which shows that our definition reproduces the classical definition of the
K
-theory functor for
C^{*}
-algebras.

Remark 2.48
.

In this remark we recall the model-free description of the
K
-theory classes associated to a projection or a unitary in an ungraded algebra.

Let
p
be a self-adjoint projection in a
C^{*}
-algebra
Q
.
It gives rise to a homomorphism
\pi:{\mathbb{C}}\to{Q}
sending
1
to
p
.
The morphism
\mathrm{e}(\pi):\mathrm{e}({\mathbb{C}})\to\mathrm{e}(Q)
in
\pi_{0}\mathrm{E}({\mathbb{C}},{Q})\cong K_{0}(Q)
is the
K
-theory class
[p]
associated to
p
.

Let
I
be a
C^{*}
-algebra and
I^{+}
denote its unitalization.
A normalized unitary
u
in
I
is a unitary
u
in
I^{+}
which is sent to
1
by the canonical homomorphism
I^{+}\to{\mathbb{C}}
. If
u
is a normalized unitary in
I
, then
by function calculus it gives rise to a homomorphism

\mu:C(S^{1},\{1\})\to I\ ,\quad f\mapsto f(u)\ ,

where
C(S^{1},\{1\}):={\mathtt{ker}}(C(S^{1})\xrightarrow{f\mapsto f(1)}{\mathbb{C}})
. The composition

\Omega\mathrm{e}({\mathbb{C}})\simeq\mathrm{e}({C}(S^{1},\{1\}))\xrightarrow{{e(\mu)}}\mathrm{e}(I)
represents the class

[u]
in
\pi_{0}\Sigma\mathrm{E}({\mathbb{C}},I)\cong K_{-1}(I)
associated to
u
.

Assume that

0\to I\to A\to Q\to 0

(2.26)

is an exact sequence of
C^{*}
-algebras such that
A\to Q
is a unit-preserving
morphism between unital algebras. Let
p
be a projection in
Q
. Then we can find a lift

\tilde{p}
in
A
which is selfadjoint and has spectrum in the unit interval
[0,1]
. We
get a normalized unitary
u:=e^{2\pi i\tilde{p}}
in
I
where we identify
I^{+}
with the subalgebra
I+1_{A}{\mathbb{C}}
of
A
.
Using the relation of the triangulated structure of
\mathrm{ho}\mathrm{E}
with Puppe sequences one can check that

\partial[p]=[u]\ ,

(2.27)

where
\partial
is the boundary operator

K_{0}(Q)\to K_{-1}(I)
associated to the exact sequence (
2.26
).

\blacksquare

Remark 2.49
.

Recall that

K^{\mathrm{gr}}(A)\simeq\mathrm{E}^{\mathrm{gr}}({\mathbb{C}},A)\simeq\mathrm{E}^{C_{2}}({\mathcal{S}},A)\ .

In particular, every homomorphism
{\mathcal{S}}\to A
of graded
C^{*}
-algebras (e.g., the one in (
2.22
)) represents a class in
K^{\mathrm{gr}}_{0}(A)
.
If this homomorphism is
\Psi_{*}:{\mathcal{S}}\to A
,
\Psi_{*}(f)=f(\Psi)
, for an unbounded multiplier
\Psi
of
A
, then we write
[\Psi]
for the corresponding class in
K^{\mathrm{gr}}_{0}(A)
.

We will use the following variation on this construction:
Let
a
be in
(0,\infty)
.
Then pull-back along the map

{\kappa}:(-a,a)\to{\mathbb{R}}\ ,\quad t\mapsto\frac{t}{\sqrt{a-|t|^{2}}}

(2.28)

induces an isomorphism
{\kappa^{*}}:{\mathcal{S}}\to C_{0}((-a,a))
of graded
C^{*}
-algebras.
It is a homotpy inverse of the extension by zero map
\epsilon:C_{0}((-a,a))\to{\mathcal{S}}
.

Assume that
{j}:B\to A
is the inclusion of a graded subalgebra and
\Psi
is an unbounded multiplier of
A
as above such that

\epsilon(f)(\Psi)\in B
for all
f
in
C_{0}((-a,a))
.
Then we get a class
[\Psi_{B}]\in K^{\mathrm{gr}}_{0}(B)
represented by the homomorphism

\Psi_{B}(f)=(\kappa^{*}f)(\Psi):{\mathcal{S}}\to B\ .

It satisfies
{j}_{*}[\Psi_{B}]=[\Psi]
.

\blacksquare

Remark 2.50
.

In this remark we provide an explicit formula for the cup product of
K
-theory classes.
Let
A,B
be graded
C^{*}
-algebras,

[a]
be a class in
K^{\mathrm{gr}}_{0}(A)
represented by
a:{\mathcal{S}}\to A
, and
[b]
be a class in
K^{\mathrm{gr}}_{0}(B)
represented by

b:{\mathcal{S}}\to B
. Then the product

[a]\cup[b]
in
K^{\mathrm{gr}}_{0}(A\stimes B)
is by definition the image of
([a],[b])
under the structure map

K^{\mathrm{gr}}(A)\times K^{\mathrm{gr}}(B)\to K^{\mathrm{gr}}(A\stimes B)

of the symmetric monoidal structure of
K^{\mathrm{gr}}
.
Explicitly,
[a]\cup[b]
is represented by the composition

{\mathcal{S}}\xrightarrow{\Delta}{\mathcal{S}}\stimes{\mathcal{S}}\xrightarrow{a\stimes b}A\stimes B\ ,

where
\Delta
is the coproduct (
2.23
) of
{\mathcal{S}}
.

\blacksquare

Example 2.51
.

If
V
is a finite-dimensional graded Hilbert space with an action of
{\mathtt{Cl}}^{n}

such that the generators act by anti-selfajoint operators, then

{\mathtt{End}}_{{\mathtt{Cl}}^{n}}(V)
is a graded
C^{*}
-algebra.
The evaluation gives an isomorphism of
{\mathtt{Cl}}^{n}
-modules

{\mathtt{Cl}}^{n}\stimes{\mathtt{Hom}}_{{\mathtt{Cl}}^{n}}({\mathtt{Cl}}^{n},V)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\to}}V\ ,

(2.29)

where
{\mathtt{Cl}}^{n}
acts by right-multiplication in itself. It induces
an isomorphism of graded
C^{*}
-algebras

{\mathtt{Cl}}^{n}\stimes{\mathtt{End}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}({\mathtt{Hom}}_{{\mathtt{Cl}}^{n}}({\mathtt{Cl}}^{n},V))\cong{\mathtt{End}}_{{\mathtt{Cl}}^{n}}(V)\ ,

(2.30)

where the first factor
{\mathtt{Cl}}^{n}
in (
2.30
) acts on the first factor
{\mathtt{Cl}}^{n}
in (
2.29
) by left multiplication.
Since
\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{gr}}({\mathtt{End}}_{{\mathbb{C}}}({\mathtt{Hom}}_{{\mathtt{Cl}}^{n}}({\mathtt{Cl}}^{n},V)))\simeq\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{gr}}({\mathbb{C}})

by Morita invariance we therefore have an equivalence

\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{gr}}({\mathtt{End}}_{{\mathtt{Cl}}^{n}}(V))\simeq\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{gr}}({\mathtt{Cl}}^{n})
.

Let
X:{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\to{\mathtt{Cl}}^{n}
be the canonical linear map which we consider as an unbounded multiplier
on
C_{0}({\mathbb{R}}^{n},{\mathtt{Cl}}^{n})
. Then

(iX)_{*}:{\mathcal{S}}\to C_{0}({\mathbb{R}}^{n},{\mathtt{Cl}}^{n})
represents an invertible class (Kasparov’s Bott element)
\beta
in

\mathrm{E}^{\mathrm{gr}}({\mathbb{C}},C_{0}({\mathbb{R}}^{n},{\mathtt{Cl}}^{n}))
and hence an equivalence

\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{gr}}({\mathbb{C}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\beta}}{{\simeq}}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{gr}}(C_{0}({\mathbb{R}}^{n},{\mathtt{Cl}}^{n}))\simeq\Omega^{n}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{gr}}({\mathtt{Cl}}^{n})\simeq\Omega^{n}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{gr}}({\mathtt{End}}_{{\mathtt{Cl}}^{n}}(V))\ .

Upon tensoring with
\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{gr}}(A)
and applying
\mathrm{K}^{\mathrm{gr}}
its
n
-fold desuspension
\Sigma^{n}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{gr}}({\mathbb{C}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{gr}}({\mathtt{End}}_{{\mathtt{Cl}}^{n}}(V))
induces an equivalence

\Sigma^{n}K^{\mathrm{gr}}(A)\simeq K^{\mathrm{gr}}(A\stimes{\mathtt{End}}_{{\mathtt{Cl}}^{n}}(V))

(2.31)

for any graded
C^{*}
-algebra
A
.
In particular,

K_{*}^{\mathrm{gr}}(A\stimes{\mathtt{End}}_{{\mathtt{Cl}}^{n}}(V))\cong K^{\mathrm{gr}}_{*-n}(A)\ .

(2.32)

\blacksquare

Example 2.52
.

We consider the exact sequence (
2.26
) and a
normalized unitary
u=e^{2\pi i\tilde{p}}
in
I
for some selfadjoint
\tilde{p}
in
A
with spectrum in
[0,1]

whose image in
Q
is a projection.
Taking
V={\mathtt{Cl}}^{1}
as a right
{\mathtt{Cl}}^{1}
-module we have
{\mathtt{End}}_{{\mathtt{Cl}}^{1}}({\mathtt{Cl}}^{1})\cong{\mathtt{Cl}}^{1}
and
(
2.32
) gives an isomorphism

K_{-1}(I)\cong K_{-1}^{\mathrm{gr}}(I)\cong K_{0}^{\mathrm{gr}}(I\stimes{\mathtt{Cl}}^{1})\ .

(2.33)

One can check using the explicit description of Kasparov’s Bott element
\beta
and (
2.28
)
that the image of
[u]
in
K_{0}^{\mathrm{gr}}(I\stimes{\mathtt{Cl}}^{1})
under (
2.33
) is represented by
the map

{\mathcal{S}}\to I\stimes{\mathtt{Cl}}^{1}\ ,\quad f\mapsto f\left(\frac{2\tilde{p}-1}{\sqrt{1-(2\tilde{p}-1)^{2}}}\stimes i\sigma\right)\ .

(2.34)

\blacksquare

We now extend the
E
-theory and
K
-theory functors from
C^{*}
-algebras to
C^{*}
-categories. We start with the characterization of
C^{*}
-categories following
[
Bunb
]
.
A
{\mathbb{C}}
-linear
*
-category
{\mathbf{C}}
is a category enriched in complex vector spaces
with an involution
*:{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{op}}\to{\mathbf{C}}
which fixes objects and acts complex anti-linearly
on morphism vector spaces.
A morphism between
{\mathbb{C}}
-linear
*
-categories is a functor which is compatible with the enrichment in
{\mathbb{C}}
-vector spaces and the involutions.

A
C^{*}
-algebra can be considered as a
{\mathbb{C}}
-linear
*
-category with a single object. We consider a morphism
f
in
{\mathbf{C}}
. We define the maximal norm of
f
by
\|f\|_{\max}:=\sup_{\rho}\|\rho(f)\|
,
where
\rho
runs over all functors
\rho:{\mathbf{C}}\to A
of
{\mathbb{C}}
-linear
*
-categories from
{\mathbf{C}}
to
C^{*}
-algebras
A
. We say that
{\mathbf{C}}
is a pre-
C^{*}
-category if
\|f\|_{\max}<\infty
for every morphism
f
in
{\mathbf{C}}
. Finally,
{\mathbf{C}}
is a
C^{*}
-category if it is a pre-
C^{*}
-category such that
its morphism spaces are complete in the norm
\|-\|_{\max}
.
A morphism between
C^{*}
-categories is just a morphism of
{\mathbb{C}}
-linear
*
-categories.

Remark 2.53
.

A morphism
{\mathbf{C}}\to{\mathbf{D}}
of
C^{*}
-categories is an ideal inclusion if it induces a bijection on objects and
inclusions on the level of morphisms so that the image satisfies the obvious generalization of
the conditions for an ideal to categories. For an ideal inclusion we can form the quotient
{\mathbf{D}}/{\mathbf{C}}

which has the same objects as
{\mathbf{D}}
and whose morphism spaces are the quotients of the morphism spaces of
{\mathbf{D}}

by the images of the corresponding morphism spaces of
{\mathbf{C}}
.

\blacksquare

A
G
-
C^{*}
-category is a
C^{*}
-category with an action of
G
by automorpisms.
We let
GC^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}
denote the category of
G
-
C^{*}
-categories and equivariant morphisms.
By
[
Joa03
]
we have an adjunction

A^{f}:GC^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}\leftrightarrows GC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}:\mathrm{incl}

(2.35)

where the inclusion views a
G
-
C^{*}
-algebra as a
G
-
C^{*}
-category with a single object.

A graded
C^{*}
-category is a
C^{*}
-category with a strict
C_{2}
-action fixing objects.
We let
(C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})^{\mathrm{gr}}
be the full subcategory of
C_{2}C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}
of graded
C^{*}
-categories.
The adjunction
(
2.35
) restricts to an adjunction

A^{f,\mathrm{gr}}:(C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})^{\mathrm{gr}}\leftrightarrows(C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})^{\mathrm{gr}}:\mathrm{incl}\ .

We use this functor in order to extend the
E
-theory to graded
C^{*}
-categories.

Definition 2.54
.

We define the
E
-theory of graded
C^{*}
-categories as the composition

\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{gr}}:(C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})^{\mathrm{gr}}\xrightarrow{A^{f,\mathrm{gr}}}(C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})^{\mathrm{gr}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{gr}}}\mathrm{E}^{\mathrm{gr}}\ .

We further define the
K
-theory of graded
C^{*}
-categories by

K^{\mathrm{gr}}:(C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})^{\mathrm{gr}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{gr}}}\mathrm{E}^{\mathrm{gr}}\xrightarrow{{\mathtt{map}}_{\mathrm{E}^{\mathrm{gr}}}(\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{gr}}({\mathbb{C}}),-)}{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)\ .

The compositions

\mathrm{e}:C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{triv}}(C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})^{\mathrm{gr}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{e}}\mathrm{E}^{\mathrm{gr}}

(2.36)

and

K:C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{triv}}(C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})^{\mathrm{gr}}\xrightarrow{K^{\mathrm{gr}}}{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)

(2.37)

are the usual
E
- and
K
-theory functors for
C^{*}
-categories.

One can check that
\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{gr}}:(C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})^{\mathrm{gr}}\to\mathrm{E}^{\mathrm{gr}}
again has a symmetric monoidal refinement if we equip
(C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})^{\mathrm{gr}}
with the maximal tensor product. We will use that the
E
-theory functor for
C^{*}
-categories from (
2.36
) is a finitary homological functor in the sense of
[
BE23
, Def. 3.24]
. In particular, it preserves filtered colimits, sends unitary equivalences to equivalences, sends exact sequences to fibre sequences, and annihilates flasque
C^{*}
-categories
[
BE
, Def. 11.3]
. The arguments are the same as for the case of
KK
-theory in
[
BELb
, Sec. 6 & 7]
.

2.5
X
-controlled Hilbert spaces and coarse
K
-homology

For the present paper the main example of a coarse homology theory is coarse
K
-theory

K\mathscr{X}:\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)

with values in the stable
\infty
-category of
KU
-modules which we describe in the following.

Let
X
be a set.
An
X
-controlled Hilbert space is a pair
(H,\chi)
of a Hilbert space and finitely additive projection valued measure
\chi:\mathcal{P}(X)\to B(H)
.
We say that
(H,\chi)
is determined on points if
\bigoplus_{x\in X}\chi(\{x\})H\cong H
.
If
X
has a bornology
{\mathcal{B}}
, then we say that
(H,\chi)
is locally finite if
\chi(B)
is finite-dimensional for all
B
in
{\mathcal{B}}
.

Let
(H,\chi)
and
(H^{\prime},\chi^{\prime})
be two
X
-controlled Hilbert spaces. If
U
is an entourage of
X
, then a bounded operator
A:H\to H^{\prime}
is
U
-controlled if
\chi^{\prime}(Z^{\prime})A\chi(Z)=0
for all subsets
Z,Z^{\prime}
of
X
with
Z^{\prime}\cap U[Z]=\emptyset
.
If
X
has a coarse structure
{\mathcal{C}}
, then a controlled operator is an operator which is
U
-controlled from some
U
in
{\mathcal{C}}
.

If
f
in
\ell^{\infty}(X)
is a bounded function on
X
, then we can consider it as a
{\mathtt{diag}}(X)
-controlled operator
\chi(f):H\to H
acting by
f(x)
on the summand
\chi(\{x\})H
of
H
.

We first construct a functor

{\mathbf{C}}:\mathbf{BC}\to C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}

which associates to every bornological coarse space the Roe category
{\mathbf{C}}(X)
.

Definition 2.55
.

1.

Objects of
{\mathbf{C}}(X)
: The objects of
{\mathbf{C}}(X)
are the locally finite
X
-controlled Hilbert spaces
(H,\chi)
which are determined on points.

2.

Morphisms of
{\mathbf{C}}(X)
: The morphisms
A:(H,\chi)\to(H^{\prime},\chi^{\prime})
are bounded operators
which can be approximated in norm by controlled operators.

3.

Involution: The involution on
{\mathbf{C}}(X)
takes the adjoint operator.

4.

{\mathbf{C}}(f)
: For a morphism
X\to Y
of bornological coarse spaces the functor
{\mathbf{C}}(f):{\mathbf{C}}(X)\to{\mathbf{C}}(Y)
sends

(H,\chi)
to
(H,f_{*}\chi)
. It acts by the identity on morphisms.

In the following definition we use the
K
-theory functor for
C^{*}
-categories from (
2.37
).

Definition 2.56
.

We define the coarse
K
-homology functor as the composition

K\mathscr{X}:\mathbf{BC}\xrightarrow{{\mathbf{C}}}C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}\xrightarrow{K}{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)\ .

Theorem 2.57

(
[
BE20b
]
,
[
BE23
]
,
[
BC19
]
)
.

K\mathscr{X}
is a coarse homology theory which is in addition continuous, additive and strong and has a lax symmetric monoidal refinement.

Remark 2.58
.

Coarse
K
-homology as a
{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded group-valued functor for proper metric spaces has been introduced again by Roe, see
[
HR00
]
for a text-book account. Upon taking homotopy groups the above construction extends the classical one to general bornological coarse spaces.
Note that
KU\simeq K\mathscr{X}(*)
.

The idea of using
C^{*}
-categories of
X
-controlled Hilbert spaces appeared already in
[
HR04a
]
.
But note that the details are different. The
C^{*}
-category
{\mathbf{C}}(X)
from
Definition

2.56
is defined for arbitrary bornological coarse spaces and its objects are locally finite, and the control is implemented by a finitely additive projection-valued measure. The

X
-controlled Hilbert spaces in
[
HR04a
]
are defined only for proper metric spaces and usually are not locally finite, and the control
is implemented by an action of the algebra
C_{0}(X)
of continuous functions vanishing at
\infty
.

\blacksquare

Remark 2.59
.

Theorem

2.57
is deduced from
[
BE23
, Thm. 7.3]
and the fact that

K:C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)
is a finitary homological functor in the sense of
[
BE23
, Def. 3.23]
.
Note that the
E
-theory functor
\mathrm{e}:C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to\mathrm{E}
itself is a finitary homological functor with values in the stable
\infty
-category
\mathrm{E}
. Therefore the composition

\mathrm{e}\mathscr{X}:\mathbf{BC}\xrightarrow{{\mathbf{C}}}C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{e}}\mathrm{E}

is an
\mathrm{E}
-valued coarse homology theory. We will in particular use coarse invariance,
excision, vanishing on flasques and the version of (
2.13
) for
\mathrm{e}\mathscr{X}
.
\blacksquare

Let
X
be a bornological coarse space,
Y
be a subset, and
{\mathcal{Y}}
be a big family on
X
.

Definition 2.60
.

We let
{\mathbf{C}}(Y\subseteq X)
be the wide subcategory of the Roe category
{\mathbf{C}}(X)
given by operators of the form
\chi^{\prime}(Y)A\chi(Y)
for
A
in
{\mathbf{C}}(X)
.
We further define
{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq X)
as the ideal in
{\mathbf{C}}(X)
generated by the wide subcategories
{\mathbf{C}}(Y\subseteq X)
for all
members
Y
of
{\mathcal{Y}}
.

Remark 2.61
.

Note that
{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq X)
has the same set of objects as
{\mathbf{C}}(X)

and the inclusion
{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq X)\to{\mathbf{C}}(X)
is an ideal inclusion in the sense explained in
Remark

2.53

allowing to form the quotient
\frac{{\mathbf{C}}(X)}{{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq X)}
. In contrast, the canonical morphism

{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{Y}})\to{\mathbf{C}}(X)
is not an ideal and we can not form the corresponding quotient.
But this morphism factorizes over a morphism

{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{Y}})\to{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq X)
which by
Lemma

2.62
below induces an equivalence in
K
-theory.
\blacksquare

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be a big family in a bornological coarse space
X
.

Lemma 2.62
.

We have a canonical equivalence

K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Y}})\simeq K({\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq X))\ .

(2.38)

Proof.

By definition

K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Y}})\simeq\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{Y\in{\mathcal{Y}}}K\mathscr{X}(Y)\simeq\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{Y\in{\mathcal{Y}}}K({\mathbf{C}}(Y))\ .

By
[
BE23
, Lem. 6.10]

for every
Y
in
{\mathcal{Y}}
we have a unitary equivalence

{\mathbf{C}}(Y)\to{\mathbf{C}}(Y\subseteq X)
. Since
K
sends unitary equivalences of
C^{*}
-categories to equivalences
we get

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{Y\in{\mathcal{Y}}}K({\mathbf{C}}(Y)\simeq\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{Y\in{\mathcal{Y}}}K({\mathbf{C}}(Y\subseteq X))\ .

Since
\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{Y\in{\mathcal{Y}}}{\mathbf{C}}(Y\subseteq X)\cong{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq X)
by definition
and
K
preserves filtered colimits we get

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{Y\in{\mathcal{Y}}}K({\mathbf{C}}(Y\subseteq X))\simeq K({\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq X))\ .

Combining these equivalence
we get the equivalence (
2.38
). ∎

Remark 2.63
.

In the argument for
Lemma

2.62

we can replace
K
by
\mathrm{e}
. We then get a canonical equivalence

\mathrm{e}\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Y}})\simeq\mathrm{e}({\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq\mathscr{X}))\ .

3
Coarse coronas

3.1
Coarse coronas and a commutator estimate

Let
X
be a set,
U
be an entourage on
X
, and
W
be a subset of
X
. For a function
f:X\to{\mathbb{C}}
we define the
U
-variation of
f
on
W
by

\mathrm{Var}_{U}(f,W):=\sup_{(x,y)\in U\cap(W\times W)}|f(x)-f(y)|\ .

For a set
X
we let
\ell^{\infty}(X)
denote the
C^{*}
-algebra of all bounded functions
X\to{\mathbb{C}}

with the supremum norm
\|f\|:=\sup_{x\in X}|f(x)|
.

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be a filtered family of subsets in
X
.

Definition 3.1
.

1.

The
C^{*}
-algebra
\ell^{\infty}({\mathcal{Y}})
of functions vanishing away from
{\mathcal{Y}}
is defined as the sub-
C^{*}
-algebra of
\ell^{\infty}(X)
of functions
f
satisfying

\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{Y\in{\mathcal{Y}}}\|f_{M\setminus Y}\|=0\ .

2.

For a coarse space
X
with coarse structure
{\mathcal{C}}
we define the algebra of bounded functions with vanishing variation away from
{\mathcal{Y}}
as

\ell^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}(X):=\{f\in\ell^{\infty}(X)\mid{\forall U\in{\mathcal{C}}:{\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{Y\in{\mathcal{Y}}}}\mathrm{Var}_{U}(f,X\setminus Y)=0}\}\ .

From now one we assume that
X
is a coarse space.
We have an exact sequence of
C^{*}
-algebras

0\to\ell^{\infty}({\mathcal{Y}})\to\ell^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}(X)\to C(\partial^{{\mathcal{Y}}}X)\to 0

(3.1)

defining the unital quotient
C^{*}
-algebra
C(\partial^{{\mathcal{Y}}}X)
.

Definition 3.2
.

The coarse
{\mathcal{Y}}
-corona of
X
is the compact topological space
\partial^{{\mathcal{Y}}}X
defined as the Gelfand dual of
C(\partial^{{\mathcal{Y}}}X)
.

Remark 3.3
.

We have the following cartoon picture of the coarse
{\mathcal{Y}}
-corona:

{\mathcal{Y}}
\partial^{\mathcal{Y}}X

The grey interior is the space
X
, with the boundary depicting its
{\mathcal{Y}}
-completion, i.e., the Gelfand dual
\smash{\overline{X}^{\mathcal{Y}}}
of the algebra
\ell^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}(X)
.
The boundary
\smash{\overline{X}^{\mathcal{Y}}}\setminus X
of this compactification consists of two parts: The boundary
\partial^{{\mathcal{Y}}}X
(depicted blue in the picture), and its complement in
\smash{\overline{X}^{\mathcal{Y}}}\setminus X
, which comes from the Gelfand dual of
\ell^{\infty}(X)
.

\blacksquare

Example 3.4
.

Let
W
be a subset of
X
.
The coarse boundary of
W
is defined as the big family
\partial W
of
X
consisting of the subsets
Y
of
X
such that there exists a coarse entourage
U
of
X
with
Y\subseteq{U[W]\cap U[X\setminus W]}
.

\partial W
W
\partial^{\partial W}X
f\equiv-1
f\equiv 1

Denote by
f
the function such that
f|_{W}=1
and
f|_{X\setminus W}=-1
.
Then
f\in\ell^{\infty}_{\partial W}(X)
.
Its class
[f]
in
C(\partial^{\partial W}X)
defines a map

\partial^{\partial W}X\to\{-1,1\}
which decomposes this corona into two disjoint components.

\blacksquare

Remark 3.5

(A continuous description of the coarse corona)
.

Assume that
X
is a paracompact topological space and that the coarse structure on
X
is compatible with the topology in the sense that there exists an open coarse entourage. For example, the coarse and topological structures could be both induced by a metric.
We then define the
C^{*}
-algebra

C_{{\mathcal{Y}}}(X):=C(X)\cap\ell^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}(X)

of continuous functions with bounded variation away from
{\mathcal{Y}}
, as well as the algebra

C({\mathcal{Y}}):=\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{Y\in{\mathcal{Y}}}{\mathtt{ker}}(C_{b}(X)\to C_{b}(X\setminus Y))

(3.2)

of continuous functions that vanish away from
{\mathcal{Y}}
.

Lemma 3.6
.

We have

C({\mathcal{Y}})=\ell^{\infty}({\mathcal{Y}})\cap C(X)
and the canonical inclusion is an isomorphism

\frac{C_{{\mathcal{Y}}}(X)}{C({\mathcal{Y}})}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\longrightarrow}}\frac{\ell^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}(X)}{\ell^{\infty}({\mathcal{Y}})}\ .

Proof.

It is clear from the definitions that
C({\mathcal{Y}})\subseteq\ell^{\infty}({\mathcal{Y}})\cap C_{{\mathcal{Y}}}(X)
. We
show the converse inclusion. By assumption we can choose an open coarse entourage
U
on
X
.
For every
Y
in
{\mathcal{Y}}
, we let
\rho_{Y}
be a continuous function on
X
taking values in
[0,1]
, which is supported on
U[Y]
and constant equal to one on
Y
.
Here we use that by paracompactness of
X
, the open covering
(U[Y],X\setminus\bar{Y})
admits a subordinated partition of unity.

Then we have the equality

f=\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{Y\in\mathcal{Y}}\rho_{Y}f
and
\rho_{Y}f\in{\mathtt{ker}}(C_{b}(X)\to C_{b}(X\setminus U[Y]))
. Since

{\mathcal{Y}}
is a big family we have
U[Y]\in{\mathcal{Y}}
for every
U
in
{\mathcal{C}}
and we can conclude from (
3.2
) that

f\in C({\mathcal{Y}})
.

For the second assertion, we have to prove that for any function
f
in
\ell^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}(X)
there exists
\tilde{f}
in
C_{{\mathcal{Y}}}(X)
such that
f-\tilde{f}\in\ell^{\infty}({\mathcal{Y}})
.
We start with the open covering
(U[\{x\}])_{x\in X}
of
X
.
Since the topology of
X
is paracompact, it admits a locally finite open refinement
(O_{j})_{j\in J}

and a subordinate
partition of unity
(\chi_{j})_{j\in J}
. Note that
O_{j}
is
U
-bounded for every
j
in
J
.

For each
j\in J
we pick a point
x_{j}
in
O_{j}
.
Given a function
f
in
\ell^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}(X)
, we define the continuous bounded function

\tilde{f}(y):=\sum_{j\in J}f(x_{j})\chi_{j}(y).

(3.3)

We claim that
f-\tilde{f}\in\ell^{\infty}({\mathcal{Y}})
.
Let
\epsilon
be in
(0,\infty)
.
Then by the variation condition on
f
, there exists a member
Y
of
{\mathcal{Y}}
such that
\mathrm{Var}_{U}(f,X\setminus Y)\leq\epsilon
. This implies for every
y
in
X\setminus U[Y]
that

|f(y)-\tilde{f}(y)|\leq\sum_{{j\in J}}|f(y)-f(x_{j})|{\chi_{j}}(y)\leq\epsilon\sum_{{j\in J}}{\chi_{j}}(y)=\epsilon\ ,

where we used that the sum is actually taken only over those
j
in
J
such that
{y\in U[x_{j}]\neq\emptyset}
which implies that
x_{j},y\in X\setminus Y
.

Since
f\in\ell^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}(X)
and
f-\tilde{f}\in\ell^{\infty}({\mathcal{Y}})
we see that
\tilde{f}\in\ell^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}(X)
. By the first
assertion,
\tilde{f}\in C_{{\mathcal{Y}}}(X)
as desired.
∎

Recall that the coarse
{\mathcal{Y}}
-corona of
X
is by
Definition

3.2
given by

\partial^{{\mathcal{Y}}}X{:=}{\mathtt{spec}}\left(\frac{\ell^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}(X)}{\ell^{\infty}({\mathcal{Y}})}\right)\ .

As a consequence of the second assertion of
Lemma

3.6
, if
X
is a paracompact topological space such that the coarse structure contains an open entourage, then we get the alternative description

\partial^{{\mathcal{Y}}}X\cong{\mathtt{spec}}\left(\frac{C_{{\mathcal{Y}}}(X)}{C({\mathcal{Y}})}\right)

(3.4)

in terms of continuous functions on
X
.

In the case that
X
is a proper metric space and that
{\mathcal{Y}}={\mathcal{B}}
is the collection of bounded subsets of
X
, the
right-hand side of (
3.4
) was considered in
[
Roe93
, §5.1]
and is known as the Higson corona
\partial_{h}X
.

\blacksquare

Example 3.7
.

Let
{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{Z}},{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}
be big families on a coarse space
X
.
Then we have a
homomorphism

\ell^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}({\mathcal{Z}})\otimes\ell^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime})\to\ell^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Y}}\cup{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}}({\mathcal{Z}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime})\ ,\quad(f\otimes g)\mapsto fg\ .

(3.5)

For
{\mathcal{Z}}={\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}=\{X\}
this induces a well-defined homomorphism

C(\partial^{{\mathcal{Y}}}X)\otimes C(\partial^{{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}}X)\to C(\partial^{{\mathcal{Y}}{\cup}{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}}X)\ ,

which corresponds by Gelfand duality to a map

\partial^{{\mathcal{Y}}{\cup}{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}}X\to\partial^{{\mathcal{Y}}}X\times\partial^{{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}}X\ .

\blacksquare

Remark 3.8
.

Let
\varphi:X\to X^{\prime}
be a morphism of coarse spaces.
Then for
f
in
\ell^{\infty}(X^{\prime})
, a subset
Y^{\prime}
of
X^{\prime}
and a coarse entourage
U
of
X
, we have

\mathrm{Var}_{U}(\varphi^{*}f,X\setminus\varphi^{-1}(Y^{\prime}))\leq\mathrm{Var}_{(\varphi\times\varphi)(U)}(f,X^{\prime}\setminus Y^{\prime})\ .

Therefore, if
{\mathcal{Y}}
,
{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}
are big families on
X
, respectively
X^{\prime}
such
\varphi^{-1}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime})\subseteq{\mathcal{Y}}
, we get a
*
-homomorphism
\varphi^{*}:\ell^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}}(X^{\prime})\to\ell^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}(X)
.
Clearly, this homomorphism also sends
\ell^{\infty}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime})
to
\ell^{\infty}({\mathcal{Y}})
.
Therefore, by Gelfand, duality,
\varphi
extends to a continuous map

\varphi:\partial^{{\mathcal{Y}}}X\to\partial^{{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}}X^{\prime}.

In particular, if
X
is a coarse space with two big families
{\mathcal{Y}}
,
{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}
such that
{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}\subseteq{\mathcal{Y}}
, then we may apply the above observation to the identity map of
X
, which yields a continuous surjective map

\partial^{{\mathcal{Y}}}X\to\partial^{{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}}X
.

\blacksquare

Let
X
be a coarse space and
{\mathcal{Y}}
be a big family on
X
.
Let
(H,\chi)
,
(H^{\prime},\chi^{\prime})
be
X
-controlled Hilbert spaces which are determined on points and

A:H\to H^{\prime}
be a bounded operator.
The argument for the following commutator estimate is taken from
[
QR10
]
.

Lemma 3.9
.

If
f
is in
\ell^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}(X)
and
A
is
U
-controlled for some coarse entourage
U
, then

{\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{Y\in{\mathcal{Y}}}\|\chi^{\prime}(X\setminus Y)(\chi^{\prime}(f)A-A\chi(f))\chi(X\setminus Y)\|=0\ .}

Proof.

Let
\epsilon
in
(0,\infty)
be given and set
\eta:=\epsilon/4\|A\|
.
We then choose
Y
in
{\mathcal{Y}}
such that
\mathrm{Var}_{U}(f,X\setminus Y^{\prime})\leq\eta
for each
Y^{\prime}
in
{\mathcal{Y}}
with
Y\subseteq Y^{\prime}
.
We define the partition
(S_{k})_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}
of
X\setminus Y
by

S_{k}:=\{x\in X\setminus Y\mid(k-1)\eta\leq f(x)<k\eta\}\ .

Since
f
is bounded, only finitely many of these sets are non-empty.
If
k,l
are in
{\mathbb{Z}}
, then
x\in S_{k}
and
y\in S_{l}
implies
|f(x)-f(y)|\geq(|k-l|-1)\eta
.
Since the
U
-variation of
f
on
{X\setminus}Y=\bigcup_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}S_{k}
is bounded by
\eta
, the condition
|k-l|\geq 2
implies that
S_{k}\cap U[S_{l}]=U[S_{k}]\cap S_{l}=\emptyset
.
Since
A
is
U
-controlled we can conclude that
\chi^{\prime}(S_{k})A\chi(S_{l})=0

We set

\tilde{f}:=Y\cdot f+\eta\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}k\cdot S_{k},

where for notational simplicity, we identify subsets of
X
with the corresponding indicator function.
Then by construction,
\|\tilde{f}-f\ \|\leq\eta
and hence

\|(\chi^{\prime}(f)A-A\chi(f))-(\chi^{\prime}(\tilde{f})A-A\chi(\tilde{f}))\|\leq 2\eta\|A\|=\frac{\epsilon}{2}\ .

(3.6)

Since
A
is
U
-controlled, we have

\displaystyle\chi^{\prime}(X\setminus U[Y])(\chi^{\prime}(\tilde{f})A-A\chi(\tilde{f}))\chi(X\setminus U[Y])

(3.7)

\displaystyle=\eta\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}k\cdot\chi^{\prime}(X\setminus U[Y])(\chi^{\prime}(S_{k})A-A\chi(S_{k}))\chi(X\setminus U[Y])\ .

Inserting the identities
\chi(X\setminus Y)=\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\chi(S_{k})
and
\chi^{\prime}(X\setminus Y)=\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\chi^{\prime}(S_{k})
and using that
\chi^{\prime}(S_{k})A\chi(S_{l})=0
whenever
|k-l|\geq 2
, we get

\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}k\chi^{\prime}(X\setminus Y)(\chi^{\prime}(S_{k})A-A\chi(S_{k}))\chi(X\setminus Y)=\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}(\chi^{\prime}(S_{k})A\chi(S_{k-1})-\chi^{\prime}(S_{k})A\chi(S_{k+1}))\ .

The right-hand side is an operator with norm bounded by
2\|A\|
.
Using
\chi(X\setminus U[Y])=\chi(X\setminus U[Y])\chi(X\setminus Y)
and plugging the above equality into (
3.7
), we get

\|\chi^{\prime}(X\setminus U[Y])(\chi^{\prime}(\tilde{f})A-A\chi(\tilde{f}))\chi(X\setminus U[Y])\|\leq 2\eta\|A\|=\frac{\epsilon}{2}.

Combining this with (
3.6
), we see that

\|\chi^{\prime}(X\setminus Y^{\prime})(\chi^{\prime}(f)A-A\chi(f))\chi(X\setminus Y^{\prime})\|\leq\varepsilon

for all
Y^{\prime}
in
{\mathcal{Y}}
with
U[Y]\subseteq Y^{\prime}
.
∎

Recall that
X
is a coarse space with a big family
{{\mathcal{Y}}}
and that
(H,\chi)
and
(H^{\prime},\chi^{\prime})
are
X
-controlled Hilbert spaces which are determined on points.
Let now
A:H\to H^{\prime}
be a bounded operator which can be approximated in norm by controlled bounded operators, and
f
be in
\ell^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}(X)
.

Corollary 3.10
.

We have
\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{{Y\in{\mathcal{Y}}}}\|\chi^{\prime}(X\setminus Y)(\chi^{\prime}(f)A-A\chi(f))\chi(X\setminus Y)\|=0\ .

Let
X
be a bornological coarse space,
{\mathcal{Y}}
be a big family in
X
and recall the
Definition

2.60

of the ideal
{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq X)
.

Corollary 3.11
.

For a morphism
A:(H,\chi)\to(H^{\prime},\chi^{\prime})
in
{\mathbf{C}}(X)
and a function
f
in
\ell^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}(X)
we have

\chi^{\prime}(f)A-A\chi(f)\in{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq X)
.

3.2
Uniform coronas and the comparison map

In this section, we define the uniform version of the
{\mathcal{Y}}
-corona introduced in the previous section.
We consider a uniform space
X
with uniform structure
{\mathcal{U}}
.
Recall that a function
f:X\to{\mathbb{C}}
is called uniformly continuous if

\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{U\in{\mathcal{U}}^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathrm{Var}_{U}(f,X)=0\ .

We let
C_{u}(X)
denote the
C^{*}
-algebra of uniformly continuous and bounded functions on
X
with the supremum norm.
Note that
C_{u}(X)
is a closed subalgebra of the
C^{*}
-algebra
C_{b}(X)
of bounded continuous functions.

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be a filtered family of subsets of
X
.

Definition 3.12
.

We define the
C^{*}
-algebra

C_{u}({\mathcal{Y}}):=\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{{Y\in{\mathcal{Y}}}}{\mathtt{ker}}(C_{u}(X)\to C_{u}(X\setminus Y))\ ,

where the colimit is taken in
C^{*}
-algebras.

Remark 3.13
.

C_{u}({\mathcal{Y}})
is a subalgebra of
C_{u}(X)
in a canonical way and consists of uniformly continuous functions which are asymptotically small away from
{\mathcal{Y}}
.
We have an inclusion
C_{u}({\mathcal{Y}})\subseteq\ell^{\infty}({\mathcal{Y}})\cap C_{u}(X)
.
This inclusion is an equality if
{\mathcal{U}}
is induced by a metric on
X
.
However, for general, non-metric, uniform spaces
X
we do not expect that this inclusion is an equality:
If
f
is in
\ell^{\infty}({\mathcal{Y}})\cap C_{u}(X)
, then it is not clear
how to approximate it by uniformly continuous functions supported on a suitable member
Y
of
{\mathcal{Y}}
.

\blacksquare

Example 3.14
.

If
X
is a proper metric space and
{\mathcal{B}}
is the bornology generated by bounded subsets of
X
, then we have an equality
C_{0}(X)=C_{u}({\mathcal{B}})=\ell^{\infty}({\mathcal{B}})\cap C_{u}(X)
.
\blacksquare

Let
X
be a set with compatible coarse and uniform structures
{\mathcal{C}}
and
{\mathcal{U}}
.
Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be a big family on
X
.
Recall the
Definition

3.1
.
2
of
\ell^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}(X)
.

Definition 3.15
.

We define the uniform corona algebra of
X
by

C_{u,{\mathcal{Y}}}(X):=\ell^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}(X)\cap C_{u}(X)\ .

We have an exact sequence of
C^{*}
-algebras

0\to C_{u}({\mathcal{Y}})\to C_{u,{\mathcal{Y}}}(X)\to C(\partial_{u}^{{\mathcal{Y}}}X)\to 0

(3.8)

defining the unital quotient
C^{*}
-algebra
C(\partial_{u}^{{\mathcal{Y}}}X)
.

Definition 3.16
.

The uniform
{\mathcal{Y}}
-corona of
X
is the compact topological space
\partial_{u}^{{\mathcal{Y}}}X
defined as the Gelfand dual of
C(\partial_{u}^{{\mathcal{Y}}}X)
.

Example 3.17
.

If
X
comes from a proper metric space (as in
Example

2.9
) and
{\mathcal{B}}
is the big family of bounded subsets of
X
, then any continuous function with bounded variation away from
{\mathcal{B}}
is automatically uniformly continuous.
In view of
Example

3.14
and
Remark

3.5
the uniform and the coarse corona coincide,

\partial_{u}^{{\mathcal{B}}}X=\partial^{{\mathcal{B}}}X,

and both agree with the Higson corona of
X
.

\blacksquare

Remark 3.18
.

If
{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}
are big families such that
{\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}
, then

C_{u,{\mathcal{Y}}}(X)\subseteq C_{u,{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}}(X)\ .

The algebra
C_{u,\emptyset}(X)
consists of functions which are constant on coarse components of
X
.
Furthermore
C_{u,\{X\}}(X)=C_{u}(X)
and
\smash{\partial^{\{X\}}_{u}}X
contains
X
as a subspace.

\blacksquare

Remark 3.19
.

Consider the cone
{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X)
of
X
from
Definition

2.11
.
One of the main observations that will be exploited below is that pullback along the canonical projection
{\mathtt{pr}}:{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X)\to X
yields a homomorphism

{\mathtt{pr}}^{*}:C_{u}(X)\to\ell^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{-}(X)}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X))\ ,

which follows directly from the definition of the coarse structure of
{\mathcal{O}}^{-}(X)
.
More generally, if
{\mathcal{Z}}
and
{\mathcal{Y}}
are two big families on
X
, then the above homomorphism restricts to a map

C_{u,{\mathcal{Z}}}({\mathcal{Y}}):=C_{u,{\mathcal{Z}}}(X)\cap C_{u}({\mathcal{Y}})\to\ell^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}\cap{\mathcal{Y}}}^{-}(X)}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}({\mathcal{Y}}))\ ,

using the big families in
{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X)
defined in (
2.35
).

\blacksquare

By
Remark

3.13
, the canonical inclusion
C_{u,{\mathcal{Y}}}(X)\hookrightarrow\ell^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}(X)
sends
C_{u}({\mathcal{Y}})
to
\ell^{\infty}({\mathcal{Y}})
.
Hence by Gelfand duality, it induces a comparison map

c:\partial^{{\mathcal{Y}}}X\to\partial^{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{u}X\ .

(3.9)

This map is a homeomorphism in many cases, as
Proposition

3.22
below shows.
We consider the following version of a bounded geometry condition on a set
X
with compatible uniform and coarse structures.

Assumption 3.20
.

We assume that
X
admits a partition of unity
(\chi_{j})_{j\in J}
such that:

1.

The family
(\chi_{j})_{j\in J}
is jointly uniformly continuous in the sense that for every
\epsilon
in
(0,\infty)
there exists a uniform entourage
V
of
X
such that for all
(x,y)
in
V
and
j
in
J
we have
|\chi_{j}(x)-\chi_{j}(y)|<\epsilon
.

2.

The covering
({\mathtt{supp}}(\chi_{j}))_{j\in J}
is uniformly locally finite, i.e,

\sup_{x\in X}|\{j\in J\mid x\in{\mathtt{supp}}\chi_{j}\}|<\infty

3.

The family
({\mathtt{supp}}(\chi_{j}))_{j\in J}
of subsets is coarsely bounded, i.e., there exists a coarse entourage
U
such that
{\mathtt{supp}}(\chi_{j})\times{\mathtt{supp}}(\chi_{j})\subseteq U
of
X
for all
j
in
J
.

\blacksquare

Example 3.21
.

If
(X,d)
is a metric space of bounded geometry, then it satisfies
Assumption

3.20
.
Indeed, we can find a suitable family

(x_{j})_{j\in J}
of points in
X
such that
(U_{2}[\{x_{j}\}])_{j\in J}
is uniformly locally finite and

(U_{1}[\{x_{j}\}])_{j\in J}
is an open covering of
X
.
In order to define the required partition of unity we set

\tilde{\chi}_{j}(x):=\begin{cases}1-d(x,U_{1}[\{x_{j}\}])&d(x,U_{1}[\{x_{j}\}])\leq 1\\
0&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\ ,\qquad\chi_{j}(x):=\frac{\tilde{\chi}_{j}(x)}{\sum_{k\in J}\tilde{\chi}_{k}(x)}

Proposition 3.22
.

If
X
is a set with compatible uniform and coarse structures
satisfying
Assumption

3.20
, then (
3.9
) is a homeomorphism.

Proof.

Without loss of generality we can assume that
\chi_{j}\neq 0
for all
j
in
J
and pick a point
x_{j}
in
{\mathtt{supp}}(\chi_{j})
.

Let
f
be in
\ell^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}(X)
and set

\tilde{f}(x):=\sum_{j\in J}f(x_{j})\chi_{j}(x).

As in
Remark

3.5
and using
Assumption

3.20
.
3
we see that
f
is continuous, has vanishing variation away from
{\mathcal{Y}}
, and satisfies
f-\tilde{f}\in\ell^{\infty}({\mathcal{Y}})
.
It remains to show that
\tilde{f}
is uniformly continuous.
By
Assumption

3.20
.
2
the number
N:=\sup_{x\in X}|\{j\in J\mid x\in{\mathtt{supp}}\chi_{j}\}|
is finite.
Let
\epsilon
be in
(0,\infty)
and choose by
Assumption

3.20
.
1
a uniform entourage
V

such that
|\chi_{j}(x)-\chi_{j}(y)|\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2N\|f\|_{\infty}}
for all
(x,y)
in
V
and
j
in
J
.
Then for all
(x,y)
in
V
we have

\displaystyle|\tilde{f}(x)-\tilde{f}(y)|\leq\sum_{j\in J}|f(x_{j})||\chi_{j}(x)-\chi_{j}(y)|\leq\|f\|_{\infty}\!\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in J\\
x\in{\mathtt{supp}}(\chi_{j})\penalty\ \text{or}\penalty\ y\in{\mathtt{supp}}(\chi_{j})\end{subarray}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\frac{\epsilon}{2N\|f\|_{\infty}}\leq\epsilon\ .

This shows that
\tilde{f}

uniformly continuous.
∎

Recall that
X
is a set with compatible uniform and coarse structures and that
{\mathcal{Y}}
is a big family in
X
.

Definition 3.23
.

We define the
K
-theory of
X
with support in
{\mathcal{Y}}
by

K({\mathcal{Y}}):=K(C_{u}({\mathcal{Y}}))\ .

Remark 3.24
.

Let
X
and
X^{\prime}
be uniform spaces with filtered families
{\mathcal{Y}}
and
{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}
of subsets of
X
, respectively
X^{\prime}
.
If
f:X\to X^{\prime}
is a uniformly continuous map such that
f^{-1}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime})\subseteq{\mathcal{Y}}
, then the pullback homomorphism
f^{*}:C_{u}(X^{\prime})\to C_{u}(X)
sends
C_{u}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime})
to
C_{u}({\mathcal{Y}})
, hence
f
induces a map

f^{*}:K({\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime})\to K({\mathcal{Y}}).

The exact sequence (
3.8
) induces a boundary map

\partial:K(\partial_{u}^{{\mathcal{Y}}}X)\to\Sigma K({\mathcal{Y}})

(3.10)

and the comparison map (
3.9
) induces a pullback map

c^{*}:K(\partial_{u}^{{\mathcal{Y}}}X)\to K(\partial^{{\mathcal{Y}}}X)\ .

These maps involve the topological
K
-theory of the uniform and coarse
{\mathcal{Y}}
-corona of
X
, which is defined as the
K
-theory of the
C^{*}
-algebras of continuous functions on these spaces.

3.3
Analytic locally finite
K
-homology and Paschke duality

Recall
Definition

2.56
of the strong coarse
K
-homology theory
K\mathscr{X}:\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)
(see also
Theorem

2.57
) and the cone functor
{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}:\mathbf{UBC}\to\mathbf{BC}
introduced in
Definition

2.11
.
As explaind in
Section

2.3
, using the cone functor, we can associate to any strong coarse homology theory a local homology theory.

Definition 3.25
.

We define the local
K
-homology functor for uniform bornological coarse spaces as the composition

K^{\mathscr{X}}=\Sigma^{-1}K\mathscr{X}{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}:\mathbf{UBC}\xrightarrow{{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}}\mathbf{BC}\xrightarrow{\Sigma^{-1}K\mathscr{X}}{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)\ .

By specializing (
2.16
)
we get the index map, a natural transformation of functors

a:K^{\mathscr{X}}\to K\mathscr{X}\circ\iota:\mathbf{UBC}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)\ .

(3.11)

Note that if we write the evaluation of
K\mathscr{X}{\circ\iota}
on objects of
\mathbf{UBC}
, then we will omit the symbol
\iota
.

We now specialize the construction from (
2.18
).

Definition 3.26
.

We define the local
K
-homology with support by

K^{\mathscr{X}}_{-}:\mathbf{UBC}^{(2)}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)\ ,\quad(X,{\mathcal{Z}})\mapsto K^{\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(X):=\Sigma^{-1}K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(X))\ .

The index map (
2.20
) specializes to a natural transformation

a_{X,{\mathcal{Z}}}:K^{\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(X)\to K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Z}})\ .

(3.12)

Remark 3.27
.

Note that
K^{\mathscr{X}}_{\{X\}}(X)\simeq K^{\mathscr{X}}(X)
and that we have a fibre sequence

K^{\mathscr{X}}_{{\{\emptyset\}}}(X)\to K^{\mathscr{X}}(X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a_{X}}}{{\longrightarrow}}K\mathscr{X}(X)\ ,

(3.13)

which is a special case of (
2.21
).
Thus the spectrum
\smash{K^{\mathscr{X}}_{{\{\emptyset\}}}(X)}
captures the secondary invariants of the reasons for the vanishing of the index of classes in
K^{\mathscr{X}}(X)
.

The fibre sequence (
3.13
) is our analogue of the Higson-Roe surgery sequence

[
HR04b
, Def. 1.5]
and
\smash{K^{\mathscr{X}}_{{\{\emptyset\}}}}(X)
is the analogue of the
analytic structure set. It follows from
Lemma

2.39
that
\smash{K^{\mathscr{X}}_{{\{\emptyset\}}}(-)}:\mathbf{UBC}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)

is a local homology theory, so it is in particular excisive and homotopy invariant. The analogue of excisiveness for the analytic structure set of Higson-Roe has been
shown in
[
Sie12
]
.

Our order to compare our constructions with
[
HR04b
]
precisely one would have to work with the equivariant generalization

K^{G,\mathscr{X}}_{{\{\emptyset\}}}(X)\to K^{G,\mathscr{X}}(X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a^{G}_{X}}}{{\longrightarrow}}K\mathscr{X}^{G}(X)

(3.14)

for
G
-uniform bornological coarse spaces
X
. If
X
is homotopy equivalent in
G\mathbf{UBC}
to a finite-dimensional
locally finite simplicial complex with a free proper action of
G
, then it follows from the equivariant Paschke duality equivalence
considered in
[
BELa
]
that the long exact sequence obtained from (
3.14
) by taking homotopy groups is equivalent to the sequence defined in
[
HR04b
, Def. 1.5]
.

\blacksquare

We consider a uniform bornological coarse space
X

with bornology
{\mathcal{B}}
and recall the
Definition

3.12
of
C_{u}({\mathcal{B}})
. In this section it is useful to change notation and consider the functor

C_{0}:\mathbf{UBC}\to(C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})^{\mathrm{op}}\ ,\quad X\mapsto C_{0}(X):=C_{u}({\mathcal{B}})

which on morphisms is given by the pull-back.

Remark 3.28
.

If
X
is the uniform bornological coarse space associated to a proper metric space, then

C_{0}(X)
is the usual algebra of continuous functions on
X
vanishing at
\infty
.
This justifies the notation.

\blacksquare

Definition 3.29
.

We define the analytic locally finite
K
-homology as the composition

K^{\mathrm{an}}:\mathbf{UBC}\xrightarrow{C_{0}}(C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})^{\mathrm{op}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{E}(C_{0}(-),{\mathbb{C}})}{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)\ .

The following justifies the name, compare
Definition

2.26
and
Definition

2.29
.

Proposition 3.30
.

The functor
K^{\mathrm{an}}
is a locally finite local homology theory.

Proof.

For any
X
in
\mathbf{UBC}
the
map
C_{0}(X)\to C_{0}([0,1]\otimes X)
is a homotopy equivalence of
C^{*}
-algebras. By homotopy invariance
of the
E
-theory functor
\mathrm{e}:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to\mathrm{E}
we see that
K^{\mathrm{an}}([0,1]\otimes X)\to K^{\mathrm{an}}(X)
is an equivalence. This shows homotopy invariance of
\mathrm{K}^{\mathrm{an}}
.

Assume that
(Y,Z)
is a uniformly and coarsely excisive decomposition of
X
in
\mathbf{UBC}
. Then we have a map of exact sequences of commutative
C^{*}
-algebras

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 5.5pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&\cr&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-5.5pt\raise
