0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{C_{0}(Z\cap Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 203.58951pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 203.58951pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{0}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ ,

where the maps in the right square are the restrictions along the inclusions of subsets and
A,B
are defined as the kernels. We show that the left vertical map is an isomorphism as indicated. Assume that
f
is in
A
and sent to zero in
B
. Then
f_{|Z}=0
and
f_{|Y}=0
so that
f=0
since
Y\cup Z=X
. Assume now that
f
is in
B
.
Then
f_{|Z\cap Y}=0
.
As the
C^{*}
-algebra
C_{0}(Z)
admits an approximate unit, there exists a function
h
in
C_{0}(Z)
such that
\|fh-f\|\leq\epsilon
. By definition of
C_{0}(Z)
we can in addition assume that there exists a bounded subset

B
of
Z
such that
h_{|Y\setminus B}=0
.
Let
\smash{\tilde{f}}
be the extension by zero of
fh
to
X
.
Then
\smash{\tilde{f}_{|X\setminus B}=0}
and
\smash{\tilde{f}}
is uniformly continuous since
(Y,Z)
is uniformly excisive.
Let
\hat{f}
be the extension by zero of
f
to
X
. Then
\|\smash{\hat{f}-\tilde{f}}\|\leq\epsilon
. Since
\epsilon
was arbitrary
we conclude that
\hat{f}\in A
and that it is a preimage of
f
.

We now use the exactness of the
E
-theory functor
\mathrm{E}(-,{\mathbb{C}})
in order to get a map of fibre sequences

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 29.28471pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-29.28471pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{K^{\mathrm{an}}(Z\cap Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 54.0486pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 54.0486pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{K^{\mathrm{an}}(Z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 118.24309pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 73.7639pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 118.24309pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{E}(B,{\mathbb{C}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 137.57819pt\raise-16.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.72855pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\simeq}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 137.57819pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-19.95836pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{K^{\mathrm{an}}(Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 53.28471pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 53.28471pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{K^{\mathrm{an}}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 118.53648pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 118.53648pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{E}(A,{\mathbb{C}})}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ .

Since the right vertical map is an equivalence we conclude that the left square is a push-out.
This shows that
K^{\mathrm{an}}
is excisive.

The algebra
C_{0}([0,\infty)\otimes X)
is contractible by the homotopy

C_{0}([0,\infty)\otimes X)\to C_{0}([0,1]\times[0,\infty)\otimes X)
sending
f(t,x)
to

(s,t,x)\mapsto f(st,x)
. This implies
K^{\mathrm{an}}([0,\infty)\otimes X)\simeq 0
and therefore
K^{\mathrm{an}}
vanishes on flasques.

Since
K^{\mathrm{an}}(X)
does not depend on the coarse structure of
X
at all the functor
K^{\mathrm{an}}
is trivially
u
-continuous.

This completes the verification that
K^{\mathrm{an}}
is a local homology theory. We now show that
K^{\mathrm{an}}
is locally finite.
For
B
in
{\mathcal{B}}
we consider the exact sequence

0\to{C_{B}(X)}\to C_{0}(X)\to C_{0}(X\setminus B)\to 0\ ,

where
{C_{B}(X)}
is defined as the kernel of the restriction map along
X\setminus B\to X
.
By the exactness of the
E
-theory functor
\mathrm{E}(-,{\mathbb{C}})
we conclude that

\mathrm{Cofib}(K^{\mathrm{an}}(X\setminus B)\to K^{\mathrm{an}}(X))\simeq\mathrm{E}({C_{B}(X)},{\mathbb{C}})\ .

Since the
E
-theory functor preserves filtered colimits we get

\displaystyle(K^{\mathrm{an}})^{\mathrm{lf}}(X)\simeq

\displaystyle\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{B\in{\mathcal{B}}^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathrm{Cofib}(K^{\mathrm{an}}(X\setminus B)\to K^{\mathrm{an}}(X))

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{B\in{\mathcal{B}}^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathrm{E}({C_{B}(X)},{\mathbb{C}})\simeq\mathrm{E}(\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{B\in{\mathcal{B}}}{C_{B}(X)},{\mathbb{C}})\ .

Under this equivalence the canonical map
K^{\mathrm{an}}(X)\to(K^{\mathrm{an}})^{\mathrm{lf}}(X)
is induced by the map of
C^{*}
-algebras

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{B\in{\mathcal{B}}}{C_{B}(X)}\to C_{0}(X)\ .

(3.15)

In order to show that
K^{\mathrm{an}}
is locally finite it therefore suffices to show that (
3.15
)
is an isomorphism of
C^{*}
-algebras.
By
Definition

3.12
, the domain of this map is given by

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{B\in{\mathcal{B}}}{\mathtt{ker}}\Big(\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{B^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{B}}}{\mathtt{ker}}\big(C_{u}(X)\to C_{u}(X\setminus B^{\prime})\big)\to\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{B^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{B}}}{\mathtt{ker}}\big(C_{u}(X\setminus B)\to C_{u}(X\setminus(B\cup B^{\prime})\big)\Big)\ .

Since
{\mathcal{B}}
is filtered and
{\mathtt{ker}}
is a finite limit we can interchange
\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{B^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{B}}}
with
{\mathtt{ker}}
and get

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{(B^{\prime},B)\in{\mathcal{B}}\times{\mathcal{B}}}{\mathtt{ker}}\Big({\mathtt{ker}}\big(C_{u}(X)\to C_{u}(X\setminus B^{\prime})\big)\to{\mathtt{ker}}\big(C_{u}(X\setminus B)\to C_{u}(X\setminus(B\cup B^{\prime})\big)\Big)\ .

If
B^{\prime}\subseteq B
, then the map that the outer kernel is taken of is the zero map, so the argument of the colimit is just
{\mathtt{ker}}(C_{u}(X)\to C_{u}(X\setminus B^{\prime})
.
Since the diagonal
{\mathcal{B}}\to{\mathcal{B}}\times{\mathcal{B}}
is cofinal, the domain of (
3.15
) is isomorphic to
\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{B\in{\mathcal{B}}}{\mathtt{ker}}(C_{u}(X)\to C_{u}(X\setminus B))
which is precisely the definition of the codomain.
∎

There is a Paschke duality transformation

p:K^{\mathscr{X}}\to K^{\mathrm{an}}

(3.16)

whose components we will describe following
[
BELa
]
. The essential idea for the construction is taken from
[
QR10
]
.

Recall that the bornology
{\mathcal{B}}
of
X
is a big family.

In the following we use the maximal tensor product of
C^{*}
-categories in the special case that
the first factor is a
C^{*}
-algebra. Then the set of objects of the tensor product is the set of objects of the right factor
{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X))
, and the morphisms of the tensor product are generated by tensor products of algebra elements and morphisms.
Further note that the set of objects of the quotient on the right-hand side of (
3.31
)
is also canonically identified with the set of objects of
{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X))
, see
Remark

2.61
.

Lemma 3.31
.

We have a morphism

\mu:C_{0}(X)\otimes{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X))\to\frac{{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}({\mathcal{B}})\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X))}{{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}^{-}({\mathcal{B}})\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X))}

(3.17)

given as follows:

1.

objects: It is the identity on objects.

2.

morphisms: It sends the morphism
f\otimes A:(H,\chi)\to(H^{\prime},\chi^{\prime})
to
[\chi^{\prime}({\mathtt{pr}}^{*}f)A]:(H,\chi)\to(H^{\prime},\chi^{\prime})
, where
{\mathtt{pr}}:{\mathbb{R}}\times X\to X
is the projection.

Proof.

This is a special case of the more general
Lemma

3.39
below, which reduces to
Lemma

3.31
upon setting
{\mathcal{Y}}={\mathcal{B}}
and
{\mathcal{Z}}=\{X\}
.
∎

The Paschke morphism

p_{X}:K^{\mathscr{X}}(X)\to K^{\mathrm{an}}(X)

(3.18)

is defined as the composition

\displaystyle K^{\mathscr{X}}(X)

\displaystyle\simeq\Sigma^{-1}K({\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X)))\simeq\Sigma^{-1}\mathrm{E}({\mathbb{C}},{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X)))

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{C_{0}(X)\otimes-}\Sigma^{-1}\mathrm{E}(C_{0}(X),C_{0}(X)\otimes{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X)))

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\,\,\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \mu_{*}\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \,}\Sigma^{-1}\mathrm{E}\Big(C_{0}(X),\frac{{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}({\mathcal{B}})\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X))}{{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}^{-}({\mathcal{B}})\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X))}\Big)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{\simeq}}\Sigma^{-1}\mathrm{E}(C_{0}(X),{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}({\mathcal{B}})\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X)))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!!}}{{\to}}\Sigma^{-1}\mathrm{E}(C_{0}(X),{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(*)))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!!!}}{{\simeq}}\mathrm{E}(C_{0}(X),{\mathbb{C}})\simeq K^{\mathrm{an}}(X)\ ,

where for
!
we use that the projection

\mathrm{e}({\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}({\mathcal{B}})\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X)))\to\mathrm{e}\Big(\frac{{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}({\mathcal{B}})\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X))}{{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}^{-}({\mathcal{B}})\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X))}\Big)

induces an equivalence since the functor
\mathrm{e}
is exact and we have
\mathrm{e}({\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}^{-}({\mathcal{B}})\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X)))\simeq 0
.
For the latter equivalence,
we use (see
Lemma

2.62
and
Remark

2.63
) that

\mathrm{e}({\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}^{-}({\mathcal{B}})\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X)))\simeq\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature},B\in{\mathcal{B}}}\mathrm{e}({\mathbf{C}}((-\infty,n]\times B))\ ,

where
(-\infty,n]\times B
has the bornological coarse structure induced from
{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X)
.
These subspaces are flasque and therefore
{\mathbf{C}}((-\infty,n]\times B)
is flasque as a
C^{*}
-category and hence annihilated by
\mathrm{e}
.
The morphism
!!
is induced by the projections
B\to*
for the members
B
of
{\mathcal{B}}
,
and
!!!
employs the equivalence

\mathrm{e}({\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(*)))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{e}({\mathbf{C}}(\partial^{\mathrm{cone}}))}}{{\simeq}}\mathrm{e}({\mathbf{C}}({\mathbb{R}}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\begin{subarray}{c}\eqref{fqwedwedewdqwdwd},\\
\text{\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{wegkowpergrwfrewfrefw}}\end{subarray}}}{{\simeq}}\Sigma\mathrm{e}({\mathbf{C}}(*))\simeq\Sigma\mathrm{e}({\mathbb{C}})\ .

We refer to
[
BELa
]
for more details and a better description of the Paschke morphism which exhibits
p_{X}
as a component of a natural transformation. In
[
BELa
]
it is shown that the Paschke morphism
p_{X}
is an equivalence
under very general conditions on
X
.

Theorem 3.32

(
[
BELa
]
)
.

If
X
is in
\mathbf{UBC}
homotopy equivalent to a countable, finite-dimensional, locally
finite simplicial complex with the spherical path metric, then
p_{X}
in (
3.18
) is an equivalence.

Proof.

In order to convince the reader that the statement is true we sketch the proof and refer to
[
BELa
]
for more details.
We use (an import from
[
BELa
]
) the non-trivial fact that
p_{X}
is the component of a natural transformation
p:K^{\mathscr{X}}\to K^{\mathrm{an}}
.

By the homotopy invariance of
K^{\mathscr{X}}
and
K^{\mathrm{an}}
we can then assume that
X
itself is a locally finite finite-dimensional simplicial complex with the spherical path metric.

We then argue by induction on the dimension. For the induction step we use that gluing the disjoint union of the
n
-simplices into the
n-1
-skeleton
provides a coarsely and uniformly excisive decomposition, and that the disjoint union of
n
-simplices
is homotopy equivalent to a discrete space.
In these two steps it is important that the simplices are equicontinuously parameterized, a fact guaranteed by the choice of the metric.
We then use that both functors
K^{\mathscr{X}}
and
K^{\mathrm{an}}
are excisive and homotopy invariant.

In order to start the induction we must show the assertion for discrete spaces. If
X
is discrete, then

K^{\mathscr{X}}(X)=\prod_{x\in X}KU
since
K\mathscr{X}
is additive, and also
K^{\mathrm{an}}(X)\cong\prod_{x\in X}KU
since

K^{\mathrm{an}}
is locally finite. Under these identifications the map
p_{X}
becomes the identity.
∎

Remark 3.33
.

We note that the Paschke morphism
p_{X}
in (
3.18
) is similar but not the same
as the Paschke isomorphism as discussed, e.g., in
[
HR00
]
. The maps have different domains.

The Paschke morphism from
[
HR00
]
is an equivalence for any locally compact metric space.
We do not know wether we can remove the additional assumption on
X
in
Theorem

3.32

for the Paschke morphism with domain
K^{\mathscr{X}}(X)
. In fact, we do not expect that the functor
K^{\mathscr{X}}
is locally finite.

\blacksquare

3.4
The coarse corona pairing

In this and the next section we construct the coarse corona pairing
\cap^{\mathscr{X}}
and the coarse symbol pairing
\cap^{\mathscr{X}\sigma}
mentioned in the introduction. The principal ideas going into their constructions
are surely well known and variants in different setups have be considered previously, see, e.g.,
[
QR10
,
Wul21
]
.

We consider a bornological coarse space
X
with two big families
{\mathcal{Y}}
and
{\mathcal{Z}}
.

In order to understand the following assertion note that the sets of objects of the
C^{*}
-categories in the domain and target of the functor in (
3.19
)
are canonically identified.

Lemma 3.34
.

We have a morphism of
C^{*}
-categories

\nu:C(\partial^{{\mathcal{Y}}}X)\otimes{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{Z}}\subseteq X)\to\frac{{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{Z}}\subseteq X)}{{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}}\subseteq X)}\ .

(3.19)

given as follows:

1.

objects: It acts as identity on objects.

2.

morphisms: It sends the morphism
[f]{\otimes}A:(H,\chi)\to(H^{\prime},\chi^{\prime})
to
[\chi^{\prime}(f)A]:(H,\chi)\to(H^{\prime},\chi^{\prime})
.

Proof.

Let
f
be in
\ell^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}(X)
and
A
be in
{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{Z}}\subseteq X)
.
First note that if in addition
f\in\ell^{\infty}({\mathcal{Y}})
, then
\chi^{\prime}(f)A\in{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}}\subseteq X)
.
Hence the class
[\chi^{\prime}(f)A]
only depends on the class
[f]
in
\ell^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}(X)/\ell^{\infty}({\mathcal{Y}})=C(\partial^{{\mathcal{Y}}}X)
, so the map
\nu
is well-defined on elementary tensors.
Furthermore, by
Corollary

3.11
we have
\chi^{\prime}(f)A-A\chi(f)\in{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq X)
.
Since the individual terms belong to

{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{Z}}\subseteq X)
we conclude that actually
\chi^{\prime}(f)A-A\chi(f)
belongs to
{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}}\subseteq X)
.

The arguments above show that
\nu
is a well-defined functor (see the proof of
Lemma

3.39
for an analogous argument with more details) defined on the algebraic tensor product in the domain.
Since its target is a
C^{*}
-category,
\nu
uniquely extends to a functor defined on the maximal tensor product, by the universal property of the latter.
∎

Upon applying
K
-theory to
\nu
from
Lemma

3.34
and using the symmetric monoidal structure of
K
and
Lemma

2.62
we get a pairing

K({\partial^{{\mathcal{Y}}}X})\times K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Z}})\to K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Z}},{\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})\ .

(3.20)

Definition 3.35
.

We define the coarse corona pairing

-\cap^{\mathscr{X}}-:K({\partial^{{\mathcal{Y}}}X})\times K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Z}})\to\Sigma K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})

(3.21)

as the composition

K({\partial^{{\mathcal{Y}}}X})\times K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Z}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\nu}}{{\to}}K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Z}},{\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\partial}}{{\to}}\Sigma K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})\ .

(3.22)

of (
3.20
) with the boundary map for the pair
({\mathcal{Z}},{\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})
.

Remark 3.36
.

We now explain the naturality properties of the coarse corona pairing.
To begin with, we consider the category
\mathbf{BC}^{(3)}
, whose objects are triples
(X,{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Z}})
of a space
X
together with two big families
{\mathcal{Y}}
,
{\mathcal{Z}}
on
X
, and whose morphisms
f:(X,{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Z}})\to(X^{\prime},{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime})
are morphisms in
\mathbf{BC}
such that
f({\mathcal{Y}})\subseteq{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}
and
f({\mathcal{Z}})\subseteq{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}
.
We let
\smash{\mathbf{BC}^{(3)}_{\mathrm{tw}}}
be the following variation on the twisted arrow category of
\mathbf{BC}^{(3)}
:
Its objects consist of an object
(X,{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Z}})
of
\mathbf{BC}^{(3)}
, a pair
(\tilde{X},\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}})
of a
coarse space
\tilde{X}
and a big family
\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}
on
X
, as well as a controlled map
f:X\to\tilde{X}
such that
f^{-1}(\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}})\subseteq{\mathcal{Y}}
.
Morphisms of
\smash{\mathbf{BC}^{(3)}_{\mathrm{tw}}}
are diagrams

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\tilde{X},\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X,{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Z}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\tilde{X}^{\prime},\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X^{\prime},{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}}

(3.23)

such that the underlying maps commute, where
h
is a morphism in
\mathbf{BC}^{(3)}
, and where
g
is a controlled map such that
g^{-1}(\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}})\subseteq\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}^{\prime}
.
Here the morphism direction is downward.

There are two functors

L,R:\mathbf{BC}^{(3)}_{\mathrm{tw}}\longrightarrow{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)

whose action on objects is given by

\displaystyle L((\tilde{X},\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\leftarrow}}(X,{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Z}}))

\displaystyle=K(\partial^{\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}}\tilde{X})\times K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Z}})

\displaystyle R((\tilde{X},\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\leftarrow}}(X,{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Z}}))

\displaystyle=K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})\ .

Naturality of the coarse corona pairing is the assertion that there is a
natural transformation
L\to R
whose component at
f:(X,{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Z}})\to(\tilde{X},\tilde{\mathcal{Y}})
is the composition

\cap^{\mathscr{X}}\circ(f^{*}\times{\mathtt{id}}):K(\partial^{\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}}\tilde{X})\times K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Z}})\to\Sigma K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})\ .

This natural transformation arises from applying the
K
-theory functor to a similar (1-categorical) natural transformation at the level of
C^{*}
-categories, involving the morphism
\nu
from
Lemma

3.34
.

In particular, the natural transformation assigns to the morphism (
3.23
) in
\smash{\mathbf{BC}^{(3)}_{\mathrm{tw}}}
the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(\partial^{\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}}\tilde{X})\times K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Z}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(\partial^{{\mathcal{Y}}}X)\times K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Z}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(\partial^{\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}^{\prime}}\tilde{X}^{\prime})\times K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(\partial^{{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}}X^{\prime})\times K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}\cap{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime})\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}\times{\mathtt{id}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{*}\times h_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cap^{\mathscr{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime*}\times{\mathtt{id}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cap^{\mathscr{X}}}

(3.24)

We summarize this by saying that
\cap^{\mathscr{X}}
is an extra-natural transformation.

\blacksquare

Remark 3.37
.

particular case of
Remark

3.36
is the following:
If
f:(X,{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Z}})\to(X^{\prime},{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime})
is a morphism in
{\mathbf{BC}^{(3)}}
satisfying the additional condition that
f^{-1}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime})\subseteq{\mathcal{Y}}
, then we have the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(\partial^{{\mathcal{Y}}}X)\times K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Z}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(\partial^{{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}}X^{\prime})\times K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Z}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(\partial^{{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}}X^{\prime})\times K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}\cap{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime})\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cap^{\mathscr{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}\times{\mathtt{id}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathtt{id}}\times f_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cap^{\mathscr{X}}}

This is the special case of (
3.24
) for
g=f^{\prime}={\mathtt{id}}
and
h=f
.

\blacksquare

Let
X
be a bornological coarse space and let
{\mathcal{W}},{\mathcal{W}}^{\prime}
be two big families on
X
such that
X=W\cup W^{\prime}
for some members
W
of
{\mathcal{W}}
and
W^{\prime}
of
{\mathcal{W}}^{\prime}
.
We then have the following compatibility of the coarse corona pairing with the Mayer-Vietoris boundary map corresponding to this decomposition:

Lemma 3.38
.

The following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(\partial^{{\mathcal{Y}}}X)\times\Sigma^{-1}K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Z}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(\partial^{{\mathcal{Y}}}X)\times K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Z}}\cap{\mathcal{W}}\cap{\mathcal{W}}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}}\cap{\mathcal{W}}\cap{\mathcal{W}}^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cap^{\mathscr{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathtt{id}}\times\partial^{\mathrm{MV}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial^{\mathrm{MV}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cap^{\mathscr{X}}}

(3.25)

Proof.

Consider the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(\partial^{\mathcal{Y}}X)\times K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{W}}\cap{\mathcal{W}}^{\prime}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(\partial^{\mathcal{Y}}X)\times K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{W}}^{\prime}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{W}}\cap{\mathcal{W}}^{\prime}\cap{\mathcal{Z}}\cap{\mathcal{Y}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{W}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}}\cap{\mathcal{Y}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{W}}^{\prime}\cap{\mathcal{Z}}\cap{\mathcal{Y}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Z}}\cap{\mathcal{Y}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(\partial^{\mathcal{Y}}X)\times K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{W}}^{\prime}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(\partial^{\mathcal{Y}}X)\times K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Z}})\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cap^{\mathscr{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cap^{\mathscr{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cap^{\mathscr{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cap^{\mathscr{X}}}

where both the interior and the exterior square is a pushout square, each giving rise to a Mayer-Vietoris boundary map.
The four trapezoids commute by
Remark

3.37
, which in each case is applied to a map of triples that is the identity on
X
.
We therefore obtain a map of pushout diagrams, which yields the desired commutative diagram (
3.25
).
∎

3.5
The coarse symbol pairing

We consider a uniform bornological coarse space
X
with big families
{\mathcal{Y}}
and
{\mathcal{Z}}
and
let
{\mathcal{O}}^{-}({\mathcal{Y}})
and
{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(X)
be the big families in
{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X)
defined in (
2.35
).

Lemma 3.39
.

We have a morphism of
C^{*}
-categories

\mu:{C_{u}({\mathcal{Y}})}\otimes{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\infty}(X)\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X))\to\frac{{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\infty}({\mathcal{Y}})\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X))}{{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}^{-}({\mathcal{Z}}\cap{\mathcal{Y}})\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X))}

(3.26)

given as follows:

1.

objects: It acts as identity on objects.

2.

morphisms: It sends the morphism
f\otimes A:(H,\chi)\to(H^{\prime},\chi^{\prime})
to
[\chi^{\prime}({\mathtt{pr}}^{*}f)A]:(H,\chi)\to(H^{\prime},\chi^{\prime})
, where
{\mathtt{pr}}:{\mathbb{R}}\times X\to X
is the projection.

Proof.

Let
f
be in
{C_{u}({\mathcal{Y}})}
and
A:(H,\chi)\to(H^{\prime},\chi^{\prime})
be a morphism in
{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\infty}(X)\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X))
.
We first show that
\chi^{\prime}({\mathtt{pr}}^{*}f)A
is a morphism of
{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\infty}({\mathcal{Y}})\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X))
.
By assumption,
f
can be approximated by functions which are supported on members of
{\mathcal{Y}}
.
This implies that
{\mathtt{pr}}^{*}f\in\ell^{\infty}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}({\mathcal{Y}}))
.
Similarly,
A
can be approximated by morphisms supported on members of
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\infty}(X)
.
Consequently, the product
\chi^{\prime}({\mathtt{pr}}^{*}f)A
can be approximated by morphisms supported on members of
{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}({\mathcal{Y}})\cap{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(X)={\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{\mathcal{Z}}({\mathcal{Y}})
.
We conclude that

\chi^{\prime}({\mathtt{pr}}^{*}f)A\in{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{{\mathcal{Z}}}}^{\infty}({{\mathcal{Y}}})\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X))
as desired.

We next show that
\mu
is compatible with the composition.
Given another morphism
B:(H^{\prime},\chi^{\prime})\to(H^{\prime\prime},\chi^{\prime\prime})
in
{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{{\mathcal{Z}}}}({{\mathcal{Y}}})\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X))
and a function
g
in
{C_{u}({\mathcal{Y}})}
, we have

\mu(g\otimes B)\mu(f\otimes A)-[\mu(gf\otimes BA)]=[\chi^{\prime\prime}({\mathtt{pr}}^{*}g)(B\chi^{\prime}({\mathtt{pr}}^{*}f)-\chi^{\prime\prime}({\mathtt{pr}}^{*}f)B)A]

We therefore have to show that
B\chi^{\prime}({\mathtt{pr}}^{*}f)-\chi^{\prime\prime}({\mathtt{pr}}^{*}f)B
belongs to the ideal
{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}^{-}({\mathcal{Z}}\cap{\mathcal{Y}})\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X))
.

To this end, we claim that the uniform continuity of
f
implies that
{\mathtt{pr}}^{*}f\in\smash{\ell^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{-}(X)}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X))}
.
Indeed, for each
\varepsilon
in
(0,\infty)
, there exists a uniform entourage
U
of
X
such that
|f(x)-f(y)|\leq\varepsilon
whenever
(x,y)\in U
.
Let
V
be a coarse entourage of
{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X)
.
Then by
Definition

2.11
.
1c
, there exists
r
in
{\mathbb{R}}
such that
((x,t),(x^{\prime},t^{\prime}))\in V
and
\min(t,t^{\prime})\geq r
imply
(x,x^{\prime})\in U
.
Therefore
\mathrm{Var}_{V}({\mathtt{pr}}^{*}f,{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X)\setminus W)\leq\varepsilon
for any member
W:=(-\infty,s]\times X
of
{\mathcal{O}}^{-}(X)
with
s\geq r
.
This shows that
{\mathtt{pr}}^{*}f\in\ell^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{-}(X)}(X)
, as claimed.

In view of the claim
Corollary

3.11
implies
\chi^{\prime\prime}({\mathtt{pr}}^{*}f)B-B\chi^{\prime}({\mathtt{pr}}^{*}f)\in{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}^{-}({X})\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X))
. Furthermore, by the assumptions on
f
and
B
the individual terms belong to
{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}({\mathcal{Y}})\cap{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(X)\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X))
.
In total,
B\chi^{\prime}({\mathtt{pr}}^{*}f)-\chi^{\prime\prime}({\mathtt{pr}}^{*}f)B
belongs to the ideal associated to the big family

{{\mathcal{O}}^{-}(X)}\cap{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}({\mathcal{Y}})\cap{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(X)={\mathcal{O}}^{-}({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})\

as desired.

We thus get a functor
\mu
defined on the algebraic tensor product, which then uniquely extends to the maximal tensor product.
∎

We apply the
K
-theory functor to the
*
-homomorphism
\mu
from
Lemma

3.39
.
With a view on Definitions
3.23
and
3.25
and
Lemma

2.62
, the fact that
K
is symmetric monoidal yields a pairing

\mu:K({\mathcal{Y}})\times K^{\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(X)\to\Sigma^{-1}K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}({\mathcal{Y}}),{\mathcal{O}}^{-}({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}}))\ .

(3.27)

Note that the members of
{\mathcal{O}}^{-}({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})
are flasque and consequently, that

K^{\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}({\mathcal{Y}})\simeq\Sigma^{-1}K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}({\mathcal{Y}}))\to\Sigma^{-1}K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}({\mathcal{Y}}),{\mathcal{O}}^{-}({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}}))

is an equivalence.

Definition 3.40
.

We define the coarse symbol pairing

-\cap^{\mathscr{X}\sigma}-:K({\mathcal{Y}})\times K^{\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(X)\to K_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\mathscr{X}}({\mathcal{Y}})

as the composition

K({\mathcal{Y}})\times K_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\mathscr{X}}(X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mu}}{{\to}}{\Sigma^{-1}}K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}({\mathcal{Y}}),{\mathcal{O}}^{-}({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\leftarrow}}K_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\mathscr{X}}({\mathcal{Y}})\ .

(3.28)

The name of this pairing will be justified in
Section

4.2
where we show in
Theorem

4.32
that
it sends symbols of Dirac type operators to symbols (with support) of suitable Callias type operators.

Remark 3.41
.

The categories
\mathbf{UBC}^{(3)}
and
\mathbf{UBC}^{(3)}_{\mathrm{tw}}
may be defined analogously to the categories
\mathbf{BC}^{(3)}
and
\mathbf{BC}^{(3)}_{\mathrm{tw}}
of
Remark

3.36
, using spaces with compatible coarse and uniform structures instead of coarse spaces.
One may then say that the coarse symbol pairings
\cap^{\mathscr{X}\sigma}
are the components of an extra-natural transformation.

A particular case is the following analog of
Remark

3.37
: For a morphism
f:(X,{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Z}})\to(X^{\prime},{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime})
in
\mathbf{UBC}^{(3)}
satisfying the additional condition that
f^{-1}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime})\subseteq{\mathcal{Y}}
, we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K({\mathcal{Y}})\times K^{\mathscr{X}}_{\mathcal{Z}}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma K^{\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}({\mathcal{Y}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(\partial^{{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}}X^{\prime})\times K^{\mathscr{X}}_{\mathcal{Z}}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K({\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime})\times K^{\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}}(X^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma K^{\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime})\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cap^{\mathscr{X}{\sigma}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}\times{\mathtt{id}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathtt{id}}\times f_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cap^{\mathscr{X}{\sigma}}}

\blacksquare

Remark 3.42
.

If
{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}
is a further big family, then (
3.26
) can be generalized to a paring

C_{u}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime})\ \subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X))\to\frac{{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\infty}({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime})\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X))}{{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}^{-}({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X))}

which yields

-\cap^{\mathscr{X}\sigma}-:K({\mathcal{Y}})\times K^{\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime})\to K^{\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime})\ .

(3.29)

\blacksquare

Let
X
be in
\mathbf{UBC}
and let
{\mathcal{Y}}
,
{\mathcal{Z}}
be big families on
X
.
The following proposition describes the compatibility of the pairings introduced above with the index map (
3.11
).
Note that we drop the forgetful functor
\iota:\mathbf{UBC}\to\mathbf{BC}
.

Proposition 3.43
.

We have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma K({\mathcal{Y}})\times K_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\mathscr{X}}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma K_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\mathscr{X}}({\mathcal{Y}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(\partial^{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{u}X)\times K^{\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(\partial^{{\mathcal{Y}}}X)\times K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Z}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cap^{\mathscr{X}\sigma}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Z}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial\times{\mathtt{id}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{*}\times a_{X,{\mathcal{Z}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cap^{\mathscr{X}}}

Proof.

Consider the diagram of in
C^{*}
-categories with exact rows

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{u}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(X)\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\displaystyle\frac{{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}({\mathcal{Y}})\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X))}{{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}^{-}({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{u,{\mathcal{Y}}}(X)\otimes{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(X)\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\displaystyle\frac{{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(X)\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X))}{{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}^{-}({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C(\partial_{u}^{{\mathcal{Y}}}X)\otimes{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(X)\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\displaystyle\frac{{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(X)\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X))}{{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}({\mathcal{Y}})\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\penalty\ \penalty\ 0\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu}

where the horizontal maps are all given by the description
f\otimes A\mapsto[\chi({\mathtt{pr}}^{*}f)A]
, thus making the diagram commutative.
We now apply the
K
-theory functor to obtain a map of fiber sequences of
KU
-modules.
In particular, inserting
Definition

2.56
, we get the commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma K({\mathcal{Y}})\times K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(X))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}({\mathcal{Y}}),{\mathcal{O}}^{-}({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(\partial_{u}^{{\mathcal{Y}}}X)\times K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(X))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(X),{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}({\mathcal{Y}}))\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial\times{\mathtt{id}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial}

involving the boundary maps for these fiber sequences.
Desuspending this diagram and using
Definition

3.25
, we may expand the resulting diagram to involve the coarse symbol map

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma K({\mathcal{Y}})\times K_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\mathscr{X}}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}({\mathcal{Y}}),{\mathcal{O}}^{-}({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma K_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\mathscr{X}}({\mathcal{Y}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(\partial_{u}^{{\mathcal{Y}}}X)\times K_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\mathscr{X}}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{-1}K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(X),{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}({\mathcal{Y}}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}({\mathcal{Y}}))\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cap^{\mathscr{X}\sigma}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial\times{\mathtt{id}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial}

(3.30)

Here the rightmost square commutes by compatibility of the boundary maps for the map of fiber sequences

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}({\mathcal{Y}}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(X))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(X),{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}({\mathcal{Y}}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}({\mathcal{Y}}),{\mathcal{O}}^{-}({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(X),{\mathcal{O}}^{-}({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(X),{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}({\mathcal{Y}}))\ .}

The projection map
{\mathtt{pr}}:{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X)\to X
is controlled and satisfies
{\mathtt{pr}}^{-1}({\mathcal{Y}})={\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}({\mathcal{Y}})
, hence extends to a map
{\mathtt{pr}}:\partial^{{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}({\mathcal{Y}})}{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X)\to\partial^{{\mathcal{Y}}}X
between the corresponding coarse coronas (see
Remark

3.8
).
This allows to write the second row of (
3.30
) as a coarse corona pairing over the space
{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X)
, for the big families
{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(X)
and
{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}({\mathcal{Y}})
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(\partial_{u}^{{\mathcal{Y}}}X)\times K_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\mathscr{X}}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{-1}K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(X),{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}({\mathcal{Y}}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\infty}({\mathcal{Y}}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(\partial^{{\mathcal{Y}}}X)\times K_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\mathscr{X}}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(\partial^{{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}({\mathcal{Y}})}{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X))\times\Sigma^{-1}K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(X))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{*}\times{\mathtt{id}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathtt{pr}}^{*}\times{\mathtt{id}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cap^{\mathscr{X}}}

(3.31)

Here the top left triangle commutes by definition of the top left horizontal arrow, while the other triangle is the definition of the coarse corona pairing.
The bottom arrow of the diagram (
3.31
) may be extended to the following diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(\partial^{{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}({\mathcal{Y}})}{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X))\times\Sigma^{-1}K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(X))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\infty}({\mathcal{Y}}))=\Sigma K_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\mathscr{X}}({\mathcal{Y}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(\partial^{{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}({\mathcal{Y}})}{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X))\times K\mathscr{X}(\{0\}\otimes{\mathcal{Z}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma K\mathscr{X}(\{0\}\otimes({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(\partial^{{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}({\mathcal{Y}})}{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X))\times K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Z}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(\partial^{{\mathcal{Y}}}X)\times K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Z}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cap^{\mathscr{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathtt{id}}\times\partial^{\mathrm{MV}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial^{\mathrm{MV}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Z}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cap^{\mathscr{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\texttt{i}^{*}\times{\mathtt{id}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathtt{id}}\times{\texttt{{i}}}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cap^{\mathscr{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\texttt{i}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

(3.32)

The upper square is commutative by compatibility of the coarse corona pairing with the Mayer-Vietoris boundary by applying
Lemma

3.38
with

{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X)
,
\{{\mathbb{R}}^{-}\}\times X
,
\{{\mathbb{R}}^{+}\}\times X
,
{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}({\mathcal{Y}})
, and
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\infty}(X)
in place of

X
,
{\mathcal{W}}
,
{\mathcal{W}}^{\prime}
,
{\mathcal{Y}}
and
{\mathcal{Z}}
.
Commutativity of the bottom square is extra-naturality of the coarse corona pairing for the inclusion map
{\texttt{i}}:X\to{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X)
, see
Remark

3.37
.
The triangle on the right is essentially the definition of the index map, see
Remark

2.41
.
Finally, since
{\mathtt{pr}}\circ\texttt{i}={\mathtt{id}}
, it is clear that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(\partial^{{\mathcal{Y}}}X)\times K^{\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(\partial^{{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}({\mathcal{Y}})}{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X))\times\Sigma^{-1}K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(X))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(\partial^{{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}({\mathcal{Y}})}{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X))\times K\mathscr{X}(\{0\}\otimes{\mathcal{Z}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(\partial^{{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}({\mathcal{Y}})}{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(X))\times K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Z}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(\partial^{{\mathcal{Y}}}X)\times K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Z}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathtt{pr}}^{*}\times{\mathtt{id}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathtt{id}}\times a_{X,{\mathcal{Z}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathtt{id}}\times\partial^{\mathrm{MV}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathtt{id}}\times\texttt{i}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\texttt{i}^{*}\times{\mathtt{id}}}

(3.33)

commutes.
The assertion of the proposition follows from pasting the diagrams (
3.30
)–(
3.33
).
∎

Remark 3.44
.

In the case
X
is a coarsifying proper metric space and
{\mathcal{Y}}={\mathcal{B}}
the commutative
diagram (
3.43
) is essentially equivalent to the statement of
[
EM06
, Thm. 36]
.

\blacksquare

Remark 3.45
.

In this remark we explain why in the construction of the coarse index pairing we can not simply replace the local
K
-homology
K^{\mathscr{X}}
by its locally finite version
K^{\mathscr{X},\mathrm{lf}}
(see
Section

2.3
) or the locally finite
K
-homology
K^{\mathrm{an}}
introduced in
Definition

3.29
.
We fix
X
in
\mathbf{UBC}
with a big family
{\mathcal{Y}}
.
For
B
in
{\mathcal{B}}
we have a restriction map

K({\mathcal{Y}})\to K({\mathcal{Y}}\cap(X\setminus B))\ .

By naturality of the coarse symbol pairing we can produce a map of diagrams indexed by
B
in
{\mathcal{B}}^{\mathrm{op}}

K({\mathcal{Y}})\times\mathrm{Cofib}(K^{\mathscr{X}}(X\setminus B)\to K^{\mathscr{X}}(X))\to\mathrm{Cofib}(K^{\mathscr{X}}({\mathcal{Y}}\cap(X\setminus B))\to K^{\mathscr{X}}({\mathcal{Y}}))\ .

But note that taking the limit over
{\mathcal{B}}^{\mathrm{op}}
does
not
produce a map

K({\mathcal{Y}})\times K^{\mathscr{X},\mathrm{lf}}(X)\to K^{\mathscr{X},\mathrm{lf}}({\mathcal{Y}})\ .

(3.34)

The problem is that in the construction above we must first take the colimit over
{\mathcal{Y}}
and and then the limit over
{\mathcal{B}}^{\mathrm{op}}
, while for the target in (
3.34
) we would first take the limit and then the colimit.

Similarly it is not clear whether in general the coarse index pairing has a factorization over the Paschke transformation
p_{X}:K^{\mathscr{X}}(X)\to K^{\mathrm{an}}(X)
from (
3.18
), i.e., whether we can construct a pairing

K({\mathcal{Y}})\times K^{\mathrm{an}}(X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle-\cap^{\mathrm{an}\sigma}-}}{{\to}}K^{\mathrm{an}}({\mathcal{Y}})

rendering the obvious comparison square commutative.
But note that if
{\mathcal{Y}}
consists of bounded subsets, then such a pairing exists.

\blacksquare

Let
X
be in
\mathbf{UBC}
and let
{\mathcal{Y}}
,
{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}
and
{\mathcal{Z}}
be big families on
X
.
Recall the generalization of the coarse symbol pairing discussed in
Remark

3.42
.

Lemma 3.46
.

The following square commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K({\mathcal{Y}})\times K({\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime})\times K^{\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime})\times K\mathscr{X}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K({\mathcal{Y}})\times{K}^{\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{K}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\mathscr{X}}({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathtt{id}}\times\cap^{\mathscr{X}\sigma}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\text{\eqref{WeDoNeedThis!}}}\times{\mathtt{id}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cap^{\mathscr{X}\sigma}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cap^{\mathscr{X}\sigma}}

Proof.

This follows from unfolding definitions, using (
3.29
) and the associativity of the symmetric monoidal structure of
K
.
∎

4
Applications to Dirac operators

4.1
The coarse index of generalized Dirac operators

Let
M
be a complete Riemannian manifold.
Its underlying metric space represents a uniform bornological coarse space which we will also denote by
M
.
We consider a generalized Dirac operator

\not{D}:C^{\infty}(M,E)\to C^{\infty}(M,E)

of degree
n
. Thus
E
is a hermitean vector bundle of graded modules over the Clifford algebra
{\mathtt{Cl}}^{n}
and
\not{D}
is an odd first order formally selfadjoint differential operator commuting with the
{\mathtt{Cl}}^{n}
-action and such that

\not{D}^{2}=\nabla^{*}\nabla+R_{0}

(4.1)

for some hermitean connection
\nabla
on
E
and
R_{0}
in
C^{\infty}(M,{\mathtt{End}}(E))
.
We consider
\not{D}
and its powers as symmetric operators on the Hilbert space
L^{2}(M,E)
with
domain
C_{c}^{\infty}(M,E)
.
By
[
Chernoff_1973
]
the unbounded operator
\not{D}
and its powers are essentially selfadjoint and we will use the same symbols also to denote their unique selfadjoint extensions.

The Dirac operator gives rise to a coarse index class
\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D})
in
K\mathscr{X}_{-n}(M)
whose construction we will describe below in detail. Under additional local positivity assumptions this index class is actually supported on a suitable big family on
M
.

Remark 4.1
.

That local positivity implies a restriction of the support of the coarse index has been observed in
[
Roe16
]
.
In the generality of the present paper (and even equivariantly) for Callias type operators, this has been studied in
[
GHM22
]
.
\blacksquare

The norm in
L^{2}(M,E)
of a section
\phi
will be denoted by
\|\phi\|
.
Let
Y
be a subset of
M
.

Definition 4.2
.

We say that
\not{D}
is positive away from
Y
if there exists a number
a
in
(0,\infty)
such that

\|\not{D}\phi\|^{2}\geq a^{2}\|\phi\|^{2}
for all
\phi
in
C_{c}^{\infty}(M\setminus\bar{Y},E)
.

We will say that
\not{D}
is positive away from a big family
{\mathcal{Y}}
if it is positive away from some member of
{\mathcal{Y}}
.

Example 4.3
.

We consider the summand
R_{0}
from (
4.1
). Let
\sigma(R_{0}(m))
denote the spectrum of the selfadjoint endomorphism
R_{0}(m)
of the fibre of
E
at
m
in
M
.
If
\inf_{m\in M\setminus Y}\min(\sigma(R_{0}(m)))>0
, then
\not{D}
is positive away from
Y
.
\blacksquare

We assume that
\not{D}
is positive away from a big family
{\mathcal{Y}}
. One should expect that
\not{D}
is close to being invertible outside
{\mathcal{Y}}
and that the relevant index theoretic information is supported on this big family. Indeed, following insights of
[
Roe16
]
, one constructs a refined index class
\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D},\mathrm{on}\,{\mathcal{Y}})
in
K\mathscr{X}_{-n}({\mathcal{Y}})
such that

j_{*}\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D},\mathrm{on}\,{\mathcal{Y}})=\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D})\ ,

where
j_{*}:K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Y}})\to K\mathscr{X}(M)
is the canonical morphism. As we need the details of the construction in the subsequent sections
we recall the precise definition of this index class from
[
BE17
]
in
Definition

4.7
.

Let
X
be a bornological coarse space. A graded
X
-controlled Hilbert space determined on points of degree
n
is an
X
-controlled Hilbert space
(H,\chi)
determined on points (see
Section

2.5
) such that
H
is in addition a graded module over
{\mathtt{Cl}}^{n}
, with the generators of
{\mathtt{Cl}}^{n}
acting as anti-selfadjoint operators.
A bounded operator
A
in
B(H)
is called locally compact if

\chi(B)A
and
A\chi(B)
are compact for all bounded subsets
B
of
X
. Note that if
(H,\chi)
is locally finite, then local compactness is automatic.

Definition 4.4
.

The Roe algebra
C^{*}(H,\chi)
associated to
(H,\chi)
is the
C^{*}
-subalgebra of
B(H)
generated by
{\mathtt{Cl}}^{n}
-equivariant controlled and locally compact operators.

Example 4.5
.

If
(H,\chi)
is an object of the Roe category
{\mathbf{C}}(X)
of
X
, then it is of degree
0
and

{\mathtt{End}}_{{\mathbf{C}}(X)}((H,\chi))=C^{*}(H,\chi)
.
\blacksquare

We now come back to the Dirac operator
\not{D}
on
E\to M
.
We consider the graded Hilbert space
L^{2}(M,E)
with its induced
{\mathtt{Cl}}^{n}
-action, where the
L^{2}
-scalar product is defined using the hermitean structure of
E
and the Riemannian volume measure.
In order to turn it into a graded
M
-controlled Hilbert space determined on points of degree
n
, we choose a partition
(B_{i})_{i\in I}
of
M
by regular Borel subsets with uniformly bounded diameter and base points
b_{i}
in
B_{i}
for every
i
in
I
.
We then define the projection valued measure

\chi:=\sum_{i\in I}\chi_{B_{i}}\delta_{b_{i}}\ ,

where
\chi_{B_{i}}
is the multiplication on
L^{2}(M,E)
by the characteristic function of
B_{i}
. Thus for a subset
Z
of
M
we have

\chi(Z)=\sum_{i\in I,b_{i}\in Z}\chi_{B_{i}}
.

By construction, the graded
M
-controlled Hilbert space
(L^{2}(M,E),\chi)

is determined on points, but it is not locally finite if
M
has positive dimension.
We let

C^{*}(M,E):={C^{*}}(L^{2}(M,E),\chi)

denote the associated Roe algebra.
Note that it is independent of the choice of the partition
(B_{i})_{i\in I}
.
We let furthermore
C^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq M,E)
be the subalgebra of
C^{*}(M,E)
generated by operators of the form
\chi(Y)A\chi(Y)
for
A
in
C^{*}(M,E)
and
Y
a member of
{\mathcal{Y}}
.
Note that
C^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq M,E)
is an ideal and again does not depend on the choice of the partition.

The following lemma is
[
Roe16
, Lem. 2.3]
.

Lemma 4.6
.

If
\not{D}
is positive away from
{\mathcal{Y}}
, then there exists
a
in
(0,\infty)
such that for every
f
in
C_{0}((-a,a))
we have
f(\not{D})\in C^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq M,E)
.

Proof.

We repeat the proof
[
Roe16
, Lem. 2.3]
since this lemma is absolutely crucial for our purpose and in the reference, the statement is only proven under the stronger assumptions that
\not{D}
is associated to a Clifford bundle and the term
R_{0}
in (
4.1
) is positive on
M\setminus\bar{Y}
.

Let
Y
be a member of
{\mathcal{Y}}
such that
\not{D}
is positive away from
Y
.
For
s
in
[0,\infty)
we let
U_{s}
be the metric entourage of width
s
as in
Example

2.9
.

For an even function
f
in
{\mathcal{S}}
let
\hat{f}
denote the Fourier transform (in general a distribution) such that

f(x)=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\hat{f}(t)\cos(tx)dt\ ,

(4.2)

where the integral has to be interpreted appropriately. We assume
{\mathtt{supp}}(\hat{f})\subseteq[-r,r]
for some
r
in
(0,\infty)
and show that

\|f(\not{D})\chi(M\setminus U_{b+r}[Y])\|\leq\sup_{|s|\geq|a|}|f(s)|\ ,

(4.3)

where
b
in
(0,\infty)
is a bound on the diameter of the sets
B_{i}
used in the construction of the projection valued measure on
L^{2}(M,E)
.
(This is the analogue of
[
Roe16
, Lem. 2.5]
).

The symmetric operator
\not{D}^{2}
defines a hermitean sesquilinear form
(\phi,\psi)\mapsto\langle\phi,\not{D}^{2}\psi\rangle
on
C^{\infty}_{c}(M\setminus\bar{Y},E)
which by assumption is bounded below by
a^{2}
for some
a
in
(0,\infty)
.
Let
A
denote the square root of the Friedrichs extension of
\not{D}^{2}
on
L^{2}(M\setminus\bar{Y},E)
.
By finite propagation speed of the solutions of the wave equation, for any
h
in
L^{2}(M\setminus\bar{Y},E)
with
{\mathtt{supp}}(h)\in M\setminus U_{r}[Y]
we have

\cos(t\not{D})h=\cos(tA)h
for
t\in[-r,r]
. Replacing
x
by
\not{D}
or
A
in (
4.2
) and since
{\mathtt{supp}}(\hat{f})\subseteq[-r,r]
we get

f(\not{D})h=f(A)h
.
Since
\|f(A)\|\leq\sup_{|s|\geq|a|}|f(s)|
we conclude (
4.3
).

One next derives the analogue of
[
Roe16
, Lem. 2.6]

by the same argument as in the reference: For arbitrary (not necessarily even)
f
in
{\mathcal{S}}
with
{\mathtt{supp}}(f)\in[-r,r]
we have

\|f(\not{D})\chi(M\setminus U_{2r+b}[Y])\|\leq 2\sup_{|s|\geq|a|}|f(s)|\ .

(4.4)

To this end, we observe for odd
f
that

\|f(\not{D})\chi(M\setminus U_{2r+b}[Y])\|^{2}\leq\||f|^{2}(\not{D})\chi(M\setminus U_{2r+b}[Y])\|

and that
{\mathtt{supp}}(\widehat{|f|^{2}})\subseteq[-2r,2r]
.
Then
|f|^{2}
is even so that we can apply the estimate already proven.
A general function
f
can be written as a sum of an even and an odd function which accounts for the factor
2
in the right-hand side of (
4.4
).

Finally, we repeat the argument of
[
Roe16
]
that (
4.4
) implies
Lemma

4.6
.
Assume that
f
is in
C_{0}((-a,a))
.
As functions with compactly supported Fourier transform are dense in
C_{0}({\mathbb{R}})
,
given
\epsilon
in
(0,\infty)
, there exists
g
in
{\mathcal{S}}
with
\hat{g}
compactly supported and
\|f-g\|_{\infty}\leq\epsilon
, where we consider
f
as an element of
{\mathcal{S}}
through extension by zero.
Then
\|f(\not{D})-g(\not{D})\|\leq\epsilon
,
g
has bounded propagation, and by (
4.4
),
\|g(\not{D})\chi(M\setminus U_{r+b}[Y])\|\leq\epsilon
for
r
so large that
{\mathtt{supp}}(\hat{g})\in[-r,r]
.
We can conclude that
f(\not{D})
is in distance
2\epsilon
from an element of
C^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq M,E)
.
Since
\epsilon
is arbitrary we conclude that

f(\not{D})\in C^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq M,E)
as asserted.
∎

It follows from
Lemma

4.6
that if
\not{D}
is positive away from
Y
, then there exists
a
in
(0,\infty)
such that
the homomorphism

{\mathcal{S}}\to C^{*}(M,E)\ ,\quad f\mapsto f(\not{D})

restricts to a homomorphism

(\not{D}_{{\mathcal{Y}}})_{*}:C_{0}((-a,a))\to C^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq M,E)\ .

As explained in
Remark

2.49
, it represents a class

[\not{D}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}]\quad\mbox{in}\quad K^{\mathrm{gr}}_{0}(C^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq M,E))

(4.5)

such that
j_{*}[\not{D}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}]=[\not{D}]
.

In order to identify this class with a class of
\mathrm{K}\mathscr{X}_{-n}(M)
we must incorporate the gradings and and degrees in the definition of Roe categories and compare the graded
K
-theory of Roe algebra
C^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq M,E)
with the graded
K
-theory of a corresponding graded Roe category.

Generalizing the construction of the Roe category
{\mathbf{C}}(X)
in
Section

2.5
, for every bornological coarse space
X
we can consider the graded
C^{*}
-category
{\mathbf{C}}[n](X)
of graded locally finite
X
-controlled Hilbert spaces
(H,\chi)
determined on points of degree
n
and
morphisms which are bounded operators that can be approximated in norm by controlled

{\mathtt{Cl}}^{n}
-equivariant operators. We get a functor

{\mathbf{C}}[n]:\mathbf{BC}\to(C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})^{\mathrm{gr}}\ .

We define the
coarse
K
-homology functor of degree
n
as the composition

K\mathscr{X}[n]:\mathbf{BC}\xrightarrow{{\mathbf{C}}[n]}(C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})^{\mathrm{gr}}\xrightarrow{K^{\mathrm{gr}}}{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)\ .

As observed in
[
BE17
]
(or using
Example

2.51
) we have a canonical equivalence of functors

K\mathscr{X}[n]\simeq\Sigma^{n}K\mathscr{X}:\mathbf{BC}\to{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)

(4.6)

which first of all shows that
K\mathscr{X}[n]
is indeed a coarse homology theory, and that it is equivalent to
K\mathscr{X}
up to a shift.

In order to compare
{\mathbf{C}}[n](M)
and
C^{*}(M,E)
we form the intermediate graded
C^{*}
-category
\tilde{\mathbf{C}}[n](M)
of degree
n
whose objects are triples
(H,\chi,U)
with
(H,\chi)
in
{\mathbf{C}}[n](M)

and
U:H\to L^{2}(M,E)
a
{\mathtt{Cl}}^{n}
-equivariant controlled isometric embedding. The
morphisms
A:(H,\chi,U)\to(H^{\prime},\chi^{\prime},U^{\prime})
in
\tilde{\mathbf{C}}[n](X)
are the morphisms
A:(H,\chi)\to(H^{\prime},\chi^{\prime})
in
{\mathbf{C}}[n](X)
.

We now assume that
M
is not zero-dimensional.
Then the graded
M
-controlled Hilbert space
(L^{2}(M,E),\chi)
is ample which means that any graded locally finite
M
-controlled Hilbert space admits a controlled isometric embedding into it.
Consequently, the forgetful functor

{\mathcal{F}}:\tilde{\mathbf{C}}[n](M)\to{\mathbf{C}}[n](M)\ ,\quad(H,\chi,U)\mapsto(H,\chi)

(4.7)

is surjective on objects. Since it is fully faithful by definition, it is therefore an equivalence of graded
C^{*}
-categories.

We consider the Roe algebra
C^{*}(M,E)
as a graded
C^{*}
-category with a single object.
We then have a functor

{{\mathcal{I}}}:\tilde{\mathbf{C}}[n](M)\to C^{*}(M,E)\ ,\quad(A:(H,\chi,U)\to(H^{\prime},\chi^{\prime},U^{\prime}))\mapsto U^{\prime}AU^{*}\ .

(4.8)

One can show that
C^{*}(M,E)
is generated by the image of
{{\mathcal{I}}}
and it has been observed in
[
BE17
]

that

K^{\mathrm{gr}}({{\mathcal{I}}}):K^{\mathrm{gr}}(\tilde{\mathbf{C}}[n](M))\to K^{\mathrm{gr}}(C^{*}(M,E))

is an equivalence.
The zig-zag of functors

{\mathbf{C}}[n](M)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\mathcal{F}}}}{{\leftarrow}}\tilde{\mathbf{C}}[n](M)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{{\mathcal{I}}}}}{{\rightarrow}}C^{*}(M,E)

(4.9)

thus induces upon applying
K^{\mathrm{gr}}
an equivalence

\kappa_{M}:\Sigma^{n}K\mathscr{X}(M)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{rewgeopgkpwerfwerfwerf}}}{{\simeq}}K\mathscr{X}[n](M)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\leftarrow}}K^{\mathrm{gr}}(\tilde{\mathbf{C}}[n](M))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}K^{\mathrm{gr}}(C^{*}(M,E))\ .

The zig-zag (
4.9
) restricts to a zig-zag of ideals

{\mathbf{C}}[n]({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq M)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\mathcal{F}}}}{{\leftarrow}}\tilde{\mathbf{C}}[n]({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq M)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{{\mathcal{I}}}}}{{\rightarrow}}C^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq M,E)

which in turn induces an equivalence

\kappa_{{\mathcal{Y}}}:\Sigma^{n}K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Y}})\simeq K^{\mathrm{gr}}(C^{*}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq M,E))

(4.10)

such that
we have commutative square

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 26.97168pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-25.32585pt\raise 32.02377pt\raise 4.72855pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.72855pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\simeq}$}}}\kern 37.36057pt\raise-27.27145pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.72855pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\simeq}$}}}\kern
