0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 59.99945pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{K^{\mathrm{gr}}(C^{*}(M,E))}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ ,

(4.11)

where the vertical maps are induced by the inclusions.

Recall the definition of the class
[\not{D}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}]
in (
4.5
).

Definition 4.7
.

If
\not{D}
is positive away from
{\mathcal{Y}}
, then
the coarse index class with support

\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D},\mathrm{on}\,{\mathcal{Y}})\quad\mbox{in}\quad K\mathscr{X}_{-n}({\mathcal{Y}})

is defined uniquely by the condition
\kappa_{{\mathcal{Y}}}(\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D},\mathrm{on}\,{\mathcal{Y}}))\simeq[\not{D}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}]
.

We now formulate the basic properties of the coarse index class.
For
i=0,1
let
\not{D}_{i}
be a generalized Dirac operators of degree
k_{i}
on complete Riemannian manifolds
M_{i}
. If
\not{D}_{1}
is positive away from
{\mathcal{Y}}
, then
\not{D}_{0}\stimes{\mathtt{id}}+{\mathtt{id}}\stimes\not{D}_{1}
is positive away from
M_{0}\times{\mathcal{Y}}
.
Using the explicit description of the cup product from
Remark

2.50
, one can check that the image under the symmetric monoidal structure

K\mathscr{X}(M_{0})\times K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Y}})\to K\mathscr{X}(M_{0}\otimes{\mathcal{Y}})

of
(\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D}_{0}),\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D}_{1},\mathrm{on}\,{\mathcal{Y}}))
is the index
\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D}_{0}\stimes{\mathtt{id}}+{\mathtt{id}}\stimes\not{D}_{1},\mathrm{on}\,M_{0}\times{\mathcal{Y}})
of the product Dirac operator of degree
k_{0}+k_{1}
.

We take
M_{0}={\mathbb{R}}
and
\not{D}_{0}=\sigma\partial_{t}
on the trivial bundle with fibre
{\mathtt{Cl}}^{1}
.
Then

\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\sigma\partial_{t})\in\pi_{-1}K\mathscr{X}({\mathbb{R}})\cong\pi_{-2}K\mathscr{X}(*)\cong KU_{-2}

is the inverse of the Bott element
\beta
.
On the manifold
{\mathbb{R}}\times M
, there is a suspended Dirac operator
\Sigma D
, given by

\Sigma\not{D}:={\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathtt{pr}}^{*}E}\stimes\sigma\partial_{t}+{\mathtt{pr}}^{*}\not{D}\stimes{\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathtt{Cl}}^{1}}

(4.12)

acting on sections of the bundle
{\mathtt{pr}}^{*}E\stimes{\mathtt{Cl}}^{1}
.

Here
\sigma
is the odd generator of the tensor factor
{\mathtt{Cl}}^{1}
(acting by left multiplication) with
\sigma^{*}=-\sigma
and
\sigma^{2}=-1
.
A special case of the above statements on products is then the following.

Recall that the equivalence
\delta^{MV}:{K}\mathscr{X}({{\mathbb{R}}}\otimes{\mathcal{Y}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}\Sigma K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Y}})
(see (
2.13
)) induced by Mayer-Vietoris boundary for the decomposition of
{{\mathbb{R}}}\otimes{\mathcal{Y}}
into
{{\mathbb{R}}^{-}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}
and
{{\mathbb{R}}^{+}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}
.

Proposition 4.8

(Suspension theorem)
.

We have the equality

\beta\cdot\delta^{MV}\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\Sigma\not{D},\mathrm{on}\,{{\mathbb{R}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}})=\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D},\mathrm{on}\,{\mathcal{Y}})\ .

Here on the left, we multiplied using the
KU
-module structure on the values of
K\mathscr{X}
.

For
i=0,1
we consider generalized Dirac operators
\not{D}_{i}
on complete Riemannian manifolds
M_{i}
which are positive away from big families families
{\mathcal{Y}}_{i}
.
Assume there exist subsets
W_{i}
in
M_{i}
such that there exists a Riemannian isometry
e:M_{0}\setminus W_{0}\to M_{1}\setminus W_{1}
which is a morphism of bornological coarse spaces and covered by an isomorphism of all bundle data. Note that
e
is not necessarily an isomorphism of bornological coarse spaces, as
e
is not necessarily distance-preserving.
We let
{\mathcal{W}}
and
{\mathcal{W}}^{\prime}
be the big families generated by
W
and
W^{\prime}
, respectively.
By excision we get a morphism

e_{*}:K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Y}}_{0},{\mathcal{Y}}_{0}\cap{\mathcal{W}}_{0})\to K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1},{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\cap{\mathcal{W}}_{1})\ .

(4.13)

We let

\pi_{i}:K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Y}}_{i})\to K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Y}}_{i},{\mathcal{Y}}_{i}\cap{\mathcal{W}}_{i})

denote the projections.
The following is
[
BE17
, Thm. 10.5]
.

Proposition 4.9

(The relative index theorem)
.

We have the equality

e_{*}\pi_{0}(\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D}_{0},\mathrm{on}\,{\mathcal{Y}}_{0}))=\pi_{1}(\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D}_{1},\mathrm{on}\,{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}))\ .

4.2
Symbols with support

As before, let
M
be a complete Riemannian manifold.
Classically, the symbol of a generalized Dirac operator on
M
is the analytic locally finite
K
-homology class
\sigma^{\mathrm{an}}(\not{D})
in
K^{\mathrm{an}}(M)
.
In the present section, we propose to consider a refined symbol class
\sigma(\not{D})
in
K^{\mathscr{X}}(M)
such that
p_{M}(\sigma(\not{D}))=\sigma^{\mathrm{an}}(\not{D})
, see (
3.16
) for
p
.
If
\not{D}
is positive away from a big family
{\mathcal{Y}}
, then we introduce a further refined symbol class
\sigma_{{\mathcal{Y}}}(\not{D})
in
K^{\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}(M)
which in addition captures the reason for the fact that the
a_{M,{\mathcal{Y}}}(\sigma(\not{D}))=\mathrm{ind}(\not{D},\mathrm{on}\,{\mathcal{Y}})
is supported on
{\mathcal{Y}}
.

Since by definition
K_{{\mathcal{Y}}}^{\mathscr{X}}(M)\simeq\Sigma^{-1}K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}^{\infty}(M))
(see
Definition

2.35
) and
K\mathscr{X}
receives indices of Dirac type operators it is natural to represent
\sigma_{{\mathcal{Y}}}(\not{D})
as a coarse index of a generalized Dirac operator
\smash{\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}}
naturally derived from
\not{D}
on the cone over
M
.

Construction 4.10
.

We assume that
\not{D}
has degree
n
. Then we
consider the manifold
\tilde{M}:={\mathbb{R}}\times M
with the warped product metric

\tilde{g}=dt^{2}+{h(t)^{2}}g

for some choice of a smooth function
h:{\mathbb{R}}\to(0,\infty)
with
h(t)=1
for
t\leq 0
and
h(t)\geq 1
for
t\geq 0
.
We let
\smash{\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}}
be the degree
n+1
Dirac operator on the bundle
\tilde{E}:={\mathtt{pr}}_{M}^{*}E\stimes{\mathtt{Cl}}^{1}\to\tilde{M}
explicitly given by the formula (see
Remark

4.11
for the motivation)

\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}={\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathtt{pr}}^{*}E}\stimes\sigma\left(\partial_{t}+\frac{{n}}{2}\frac{\dot{h}(t)}{h(t)}\right)+\frac{1}{h(t)}{\mathtt{pr}}^{*}\not{D}\stimes{\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathtt{Cl}}^{1}},

(4.14)

where
\sigma
is the odd generator of the tensor factor
{\mathtt{Cl}}^{1}
.

Remark 4.11
.

Abstractly,
\smash{\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}}
is the result obtained by adapting the suspension
\Sigma\not{D}
of
\not{D}
on
{\mathbb{R}}\times M
given in (
4.12
), to the metric
\tilde{g}
, using the adaptation procedure given in
[
Buna
, Sec. 4]
.
The process of adaptation is uniquely fixed by the following requirements: (1) It is a local construction.
(2) When applied to the spin Dirac operator on the product it produces the spin Dirac operator on the warped product.
(3) Finally it is compatible with forming twisted Dirac operators and adding zero order terms.

\blacksquare

We consider the big family

\tilde{M}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}:={{\mathbb{R}}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}\cup\{{\mathbb{R}}^{+}\}\times M\ .

(4.15)

on
\tilde{M}
, see
Example

2.4
for notation.

Lemma 4.12
.

If
\not{D}
is positive away from
{\mathcal{Y}}
, then
\smash{\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}}
is positive away from
\tilde{M}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}
.

Proof.

Let
Y
be a member of
{\mathcal{Y}}
such that
\not{D}
is positive on
M\setminus Y
and choose
a
in
(0,\infty)
as in
Definition

4.2
.
We claim that
\smash{\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}}
is positive away from

({\mathbb{R}}\times Y)\cup({\mathbb{R}}^{+}\times M)=({\mathbb{R}}\times M)\setminus({(-\infty,0)}\times(M\setminus Y))\ .

Indeed, let
\tilde{\phi}
be in
C^{\infty}_{c}({(-\infty,0)}\times(M\setminus Y),\tilde{E})
.
Then since
h\equiv 1
on the support of
\tilde{\phi}
, we have

\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}\tilde{\phi}=({\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathtt{pr}}^{*}E}\stimes\sigma\partial_{t}+\not{D}\stimes{\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathtt{Cl}}^{1}})\tilde{\phi}\ .

As the summands in the bracket commute (in the graded sense), we therefore get

\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}{}^{2}\tilde{\phi}={\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathtt{pr}}^{*}E}\stimes(\sigma\partial_{t})^{2}\tilde{\phi}+\not{D}^{2}\stimes{\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathtt{Cl}}^{1}}\tilde{\phi},

so

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}\tilde{\phi}\|^{2}=\langle\tilde{\phi},\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}{}^{2}\tilde{\phi}\rangle=-\langle\tilde{\phi},\partial_{t}^{2}{\tilde{\phi}}\rangle+\langle\tilde{\phi},\not{D}^{2}\tilde{\phi}\rangle=\|\partial_{t}{\phi}\|^{2}+\|\not{D}\tilde{\phi}\|^{2}\geq a^{2}\|\tilde{\phi}\|^{2}.

Here we used that
\|\not{D}\tilde{\phi}(t)\|_{\{t\}\times M}^{2}\geq a^{2}\|\tilde{\phi}(t)\|^{2}
for each
t
.
∎

In view of
Lemma

4.12
the
Definition

4.7
provides the coarse index class
\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\smash{\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}},\mathrm{on}\,\tilde{M}_{{\mathcal{Y}}})
in
K\mathscr{X}_{-n-1}(\tilde{M}_{{\mathcal{Y}}})
.

For the moment we write
\tilde{M}_{h}
and
\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}_{h}
for the manifold and Dirac operator associated to the warping function
h
.
The set of these functions is partially ordered and the constant function
h\equiv 1

is the minimal element.
If
h^{\prime},h
are two such functions such that

h^{\prime}\leq h\ ,

then the identity map of
{\mathbb{R}}\times M

is a map of bornological coarse spaces
q:\tilde{M}_{h}\to\tilde{M}_{h^{\prime}}
.
We assume that
\not{D}
is positive away from
{\mathcal{Y}}
.
We add a subscript
h
or
h^{\prime}
to the notation of the big family
\tilde{M}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}

indicating from which space its coarse structure is induced.

Proposition 4.13

(
[
Buna
, Prop. 4.12]
)
.

If
\not{D}
is positive away from
{\mathcal{Y}}
, then
we have the equality

q_{*}\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}_{h},\mathrm{on}\,\tilde{M}_{h,{\mathcal{Y}}})=\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}_{h^{\prime}},\mathrm{on}\,\tilde{M}_{h^{\prime},{\mathcal{Y}}})

(4.16)

in
K\mathscr{X}_{-n-1}(\tilde{M}_{h^{\prime},{\mathcal{Y}}})
.

Proof.

We emphasize that the all analysis of Dirac operators used in this proof is hidden in the suspension theorem
Proposition

4.8
and the coarse relative index theorem,
Proposition

4.9
.
Besides these results, the argument below only uses formal properties of coarse homology theories and some simple coarse geometric insights.

We consider the manifold

\hat{M}:={\mathbb{R}}\times{\mathbb{R}}\times M
and let
(s,t)
denote the coordinates on the factor
{\mathbb{R}}\times{\mathbb{R}}
.
We choose a real-valued smooth increasing function

\chi
on
{\mathbb{R}}
such that
\chi(s)=0
for
s\leq 0
and
\chi(s)=1
for
s\geq 1
.
We then consider the function on
\hat{M}
given by

\tilde{h}(s,t,m):=(1-\chi(s))h^{\prime}(t)+\chi(s)h(t)\ .

transition region
[1,\infty)\times([0,\infty)\times M)_{h}
(-\infty,0]\times([0,\infty)\times M)_{h^{\prime}}
{\mathbb{R}}\times(-\infty,0]\times M
s=1
s=0

We view
\hat{M}
as a bornological coarse space with the warped product metric

ds^{2}+dt^{2}+\tilde{h}g
.
We then get following morphisms of bornological coarse spaces

{\mathbb{R}}\otimes\tilde{M}_{h}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hat{q}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\hat{M}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hat{q}^{\prime}}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\mathbb{R}}\otimes\tilde{M}_{h^{\prime}}\ ,

all induced by the identity of the underlying sets.

The families

\hat{M}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}:={\mathbb{R}}\times{\mathbb{R}}\times{\mathcal{Y}},\quad\hat{M}^{\{\leq a\}}:=\{(-\infty,a]\}\times{\mathbb{R}}\times M\ ,\quad\hat{M}^{\{\geq a\}}:=\{[a,\infty)\}\times{\mathbb{R}}\times M

are all big families on
\hat{M}
and so are

\hat{M}^{\{\leq a\}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}:=\hat{M}^{\{\leq a\}}\cap\hat{M}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}\ ,\quad\hat{M}^{\{\geq a\}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}:=\hat{M}^{\{\geq a\}}\cap\smash{\hat{M}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}}\ .

We define
\smash{\hat{\not}{D}}
by a two-dimensional version of
Construction

4.10
.
Notice that
\hat{q}
and
\hat{q}^{\prime}
induce local isomorphisms of the Dirac operators on the subsets
[1,\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}\times M
and
(-\infty,0]\times{\mathbb{R}}\times M
, respectively.

We consider the the projections

\displaystyle\pi_{\leq 1}:K\mathscr{X}({\mathbb{R}}\otimes\tilde{M}_{h,{\mathcal{Y}}})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow K\mathscr{X}({\mathbb{R}}\otimes\tilde{M}_{h,{\mathcal{Y}}},\{(-\infty,1]\}\otimes\tilde{M}_{h,{\mathcal{Y}}})\ ,

\displaystyle\pi_{\geq 0}^{\prime}:K\mathscr{X}({\mathbb{R}}\otimes\tilde{M}_{h^{\prime},{\mathcal{Y}}})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow K\mathscr{X}({\mathbb{R}}\otimes\tilde{M}_{h^{\prime},{\mathcal{Y}}},\{[0,\infty)\}\otimes\tilde{M}_{h^{\prime},{\mathcal{Y}}})\ ,

\displaystyle\tilde{\pi}_{\leq 1}:K\mathscr{X}(\hat{M}_{{\mathcal{Y}}})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow K\mathscr{X}(\hat{M}_{{\mathcal{Y}}},\hat{M}^{\{\leq 1\}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}})\ ,

\displaystyle\tilde{\pi}_{\geq 0}:K\mathscr{X}(\hat{M}_{{\mathcal{Y}}})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow K\mathscr{X}(\hat{M}_{{\mathcal{Y}}},\hat{M}^{\{\geq 0\}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}})

from the absolute to the relative coarse cohomology, and let

\displaystyle\hat{q}^{\mathrm{Rel}}:

\displaystyle K\mathscr{X}({\mathbb{R}}\otimes\tilde{M}_{h,{\mathcal{Y}}},\{(-\infty,1]\}\otimes\tilde{M}_{h,{\mathcal{Y}}})\to K\mathscr{X}(\hat{M}_{{\mathcal{Y}}},\hat{M}^{\{\leq 1\}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}})

\displaystyle\hat{q}^{\prime,\mathrm{\mathrm{Rel}}}:

\displaystyle K\mathscr{X}(\hat{M}_{{\mathcal{Y}}},\hat{M}^{\{\geq 0\}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}})\to K\mathscr{X}({\mathbb{R}}\otimes\tilde{M}_{h^{\prime},{\mathcal{Y}}},\{[0,\infty)\}\otimes\tilde{M}_{h,{\mathcal{Y}}})

the maps in relative coarse
K
-homology.
Then the relative index theorem,
Proposition

4.9
, provides the identities

\displaystyle\hat{q}^{\mathrm{Rel}}_{*}\pi_{\leq 1,*}\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\Sigma\tilde{\not}{D}_{h},\mathrm{on}\,{\mathbb{R}}\times\tilde{M}_{h,{\mathcal{Y}}})

\displaystyle=\tilde{\pi}_{\leq 1,*}\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\hat{\not}{D},\mathrm{on}\,\hat{M}_{{\mathcal{Y}}})\ ,

(4.17)

\displaystyle\pi_{\geq 0,*}^{\prime}\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\Sigma\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}_{h^{\prime}},\mathrm{on}\,{\mathbb{R}}\times\tilde{M}_{h^{\prime},{\mathcal{Y}}})

\displaystyle=\hat{q}^{\prime,\mathrm{Rel}}_{*}\tilde{\pi}_{\geq 0,*}\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\hat{\not}{D},\mathrm{on}\,\hat{M}_{{\mathcal{Y}}})\ .

(4.18)

In the calculations below we use the following relation beween Mayer-Vietoris boundaries and boundaries in pair sequences.
Let
X
be a bornological coarse space with a big family
{\mathcal{Y}}
and let
(W,W^{\prime})
be a coarsely excisive decomposition of
X
.
Then we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Y}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Y}}\cap W\cap W^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Y}}\cap\{W^{\prime}\})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Y}}\cap\{W\},{\mathcal{Y}}\cap\{W\}\cap\{W^{\prime}\})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Y}}\cap\{W\}\cap\{W^{\prime}\})\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial^{MV}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta^{MV}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

(4.19)

where
\pi
is the projection from the absolute to the relative coarse cohomology, and
j
and
\iota
are induced by the canonical inclusions.
The morphism
j
is an equivalence by excision and
\iota
is an equivalence by coarse invariance and the assumption that
(W,W^{\prime})
is coarsely excisive.

Pasting two instances of these diagrams, we get the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K\mathscr{X}({\mathbb{R}}\otimes\tilde{M}_{h,{\mathcal{Y}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma K\mathscr{X}(\tilde{M}_{h,{\mathcal{Y}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K\mathscr{X}({\mathbb{R}}\otimes\tilde{M}_{h,{\mathcal{Y}}},\{(-\infty,1]\}\otimes\tilde{M}_{h,{\mathcal{Y}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\qquad\qquad\qquad K\mathscr{X}(\{[1,\infty)\}\otimes\tilde{M}_{h,{\mathcal{Y}}},\{\{1\}\}\otimes\tilde{M}_{h,{\mathcal{Y}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma K\mathscr{X}(\{\{1\}\}\otimes\tilde{M}_{h,{\mathcal{Y}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K\mathscr{X}(\hat{M}^{\{\geq 1\}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}},\hat{M}^{\{\geq 1\}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}\cap\hat{M}^{\{\leq 1\}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma K\mathscr{X}(\hat{M}^{\{\geq 1\}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}\cap\hat{M}^{\{\leq 1\}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K\mathscr{X}(\hat{M}_{{\mathcal{Y}}},\hat{M}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}^{\{\leq 1\}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K\mathscr{X}(\hat{M}_{{\mathcal{Y}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma K\mathscr{X}(\hat{M}^{\{\leq 1\}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}\cap\hat{M}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}^{\{\geq 1\}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\delta^{MV}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\leq 1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\partial^{MV}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{q}^{\mathrm{Rel}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{q}^{\mathrm{rel}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{q}^{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{j}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\tilde{\partial}^{MV}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\pi}_{\leq 1}}

(4.20)

Here
\delta_{1}^{MV}
is the Mayer-Vietoris map for the coarsely excisive decomposition of
{\mathbb{R}}\otimes\tilde{M}_{h,{\mathcal{Y}}}
into
(-\infty,1]\times\tilde{M}_{h}
and
[1,\infty)\times\tilde{M}_{h})
, while
\tilde{\partial}^{MV}
is the Mayer-Vietoris boundary for the pair of big families
(\hat{M}^{\{\leq 1\}},\hat{M}^{\{\geq 1\}})
in
\hat{M}
.
As above, the maps
\hat{q}^{{?}}
are all induced by
\hat{q}
.
The additional minus signs come from the fact that the order of the partitions is swapped in comparison to (
4.19
).
Consequently we have

\displaystyle-\hat{q}_{*}^{0}\iota_{1,*}\delta_{1,*}^{MV}{\mathtt{Ind}}\mathscr{X}(\Sigma\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}_{h},\text{on}\penalty\ {\mathbb{R}}\times\tilde{M}_{h,{\mathcal{Y}}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{gweiojgoewrgfewrfw}}}{{=}}\partial_{*}\tilde{j}_{1,*}^{-1}\hat{q}^{\mathrm{Rel}}_{*}{\pi}_{\leq 1,*}{\mathtt{Ind}}\mathscr{X}(\Sigma\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}_{h},\text{on}\penalty\ {\mathbb{R}}\times\tilde{M}_{h,{\mathcal{Y}}})

(4.21)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{ApplRelativeIndex1}}}{{=}}\partial_{*}\tilde{j}_{1,*}^{-1}\tilde{\pi}_{\leq 1,*}{\mathtt{Ind}}\mathscr{X}(\hat{\not{D}},\text{on}\penalty\ \hat{M}_{{\mathcal{Y}}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{gweiojgoewrgfewrfw}}}{{=}}-\tilde{\partial}^{MV}_{*}{\mathtt{Ind}}\mathscr{X}(\hat{\not{D}},\text{on}\penalty\ \hat{M}_{{\mathcal{Y}}})\ .

Similarly, we get the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K\mathscr{X}(\hat{M}_{{\mathcal{Y}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma K\mathscr{X}(\hat{M}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}^{\{\leq 0\}}\cap\hat{M}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}^{{\{\geq 0\}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K\mathscr{X}(\hat{M}_{{\mathcal{Y}}},\hat{M}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}^{\{\geq 0\}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K\mathscr{X}(\hat{M}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}^{\{\leq 0\}},\hat{M}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}^{\{\leq 0\}}\cap\hat{M}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}^{\{\geq 0\}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma K\mathscr{X}(\hat{M}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}^{\{\leq 0\}}\cap\hat{M}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}^{\{\geq 0\}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\qquad K\mathscr{X}(\{(-\infty,0]\}\otimes\tilde{M}_{h^{\prime},{\mathcal{Y}}},\{\{0\}\}\otimes\tilde{M}_{h^{\prime},{\mathcal{Y}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma K\mathscr{X}(\{\{0\}\}\otimes\tilde{M}_{h^{\prime},{\mathcal{Y}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K\mathscr{X}({\mathbb{R}}\otimes\tilde{M}_{h^{\prime},{\mathcal{Y}}},\{[0,\infty)\}\otimes\tilde{M}_{h^{\prime},{\mathcal{Y}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K\mathscr{X}({\mathbb{R}}\otimes\tilde{M}_{h^{\prime},{\mathcal{Y}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma K\mathscr{X}(\tilde{M}_{h^{\prime},{\mathcal{Y}}})\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\partial}^{MV}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\pi}_{\geq 0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{q}^{\prime,\mathrm{Rel}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{j}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{q}^{\prime,\mathrm{rel}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{q}^{\prime,0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{0}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta_{0}^{MV}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial_{0}^{MV}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\geq 0}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{\prime}_{0}}

(4.22)

where
\delta_{0}^{MV}
is the Mayer-Vietoris boundary for the coarsely excisive partition of
{\mathbb{R}}\otimes\tilde{M}_{h^{\prime}}
into
(-\infty,0]\times\tilde{M}_{h^{\prime}}
and
[0,\infty)\times\tilde{M}_{h^{\prime}}
.
Notice also that
\smash{\hat{M}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}^{\{\leq 1\}}=\hat{M}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}^{\{\leq 0\}}}
and
\smash{\hat{M}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}^{\{\geq 1\}}=\hat{M}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}^{\{\geq 0\}}}

so that the bottom arrow of (
4.20
) coincides with to top arrow of (
4.22
) up to sign.

We therefore get

\displaystyle\hat{q}^{\prime,0}_{*}\tilde{\partial}^{MV}_{*}{\mathtt{Ind}}\mathscr{X}(\hat{\not}{D},\mathrm{on}\,\hat{M}_{\tilde{h},{\mathcal{Y}}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{gweiojgoewrgfewrfw1}}}{{=}}\partial_{*}j_{0,*}^{\prime,-1}\hat{q}^{\prime,\mathrm{Rel}}_{*}\tilde{\pi}_{\geq 0,*}{\mathtt{Ind}}\mathscr{X}(\hat{\not}{D},\mathrm{on}\,\hat{M}_{\tilde{h},{\mathcal{Y}}})

(4.23)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{ApplRelativeIndex2}}}{{=}}\partial_{*}j_{0,*}^{\prime,-1}{\pi}^{\prime}_{\geq 0,*}{\mathtt{Ind}}\mathscr{X}(\Sigma\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}_{h^{\prime}},\mathrm{on}\,{\mathbb{R}}\times\tilde{M}_{h^{\prime},{\mathcal{Y}}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{gweiojgoewrgfewrfw1}}}{{=}}\iota^{\prime}_{0,*}\delta_{0,*}^{MV}{\mathtt{Ind}}\mathscr{X}(\Sigma\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}_{h^{\prime}},\mathrm{on}\,{\mathbb{R}}\times\tilde{M}_{h^{\prime},{\mathcal{Y}}})\ .

Notice that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{M}_{h}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{M}_{h^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{R}}\otimes\tilde{M}_{h}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{R}}\otimes\tilde{M}_{h^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(t,m)\mapsto(1,t,m)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(t,m)\mapsto(0,t,m)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{q}^{\prime}}

does not strictly commute, but the two compositions are close to each other.
We conclude that

\iota^{\prime}_{0,*}q_{*}=\hat{q}^{\prime,0}_{*}\hat{q}^{0}_{*}\iota_{1,*}:K\mathscr{X}(\tilde{M}_{h,{\mathcal{Y}}})\to K\mathscr{X}(\{\{0\}\}\otimes\tilde{M}_{h^{\prime},{\mathcal{Y}}})\ .

(4.24)

We now calculate

\displaystyle q_{*}{\mathtt{Ind}}(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}_{h},\mathrm{on}\,\tilde{M}_{h,{\mathcal{Y}}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{SuspensionTheorem}}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\beta\cdot q_{*}\delta^{MV}_{1,*}({\mathtt{Ind}}\mathscr{X}(\Sigma\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}_{h},\mathrm{on}\,{\mathbb{R}}\times\tilde{M}_{h,{\mathcal{Y}}}))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{CloseDiagram}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\beta\cdot\iota^{\prime,-1}_{0,*}\hat{q}^{\prime,0}_{*}\hat{q}^{0}_{*}\iota_{1,*}\delta_{1,*}^{MV}{\mathtt{Ind}}\mathscr{X}(\Sigma\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}_{h},\mathrm{on}\,{\mathbb{R}}\times\tilde{M}_{h,{\mathcal{Y}}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{ResultFirstDiagram}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\beta\cdot\iota^{\prime,-1}_{0,*}\hat{q}^{\prime,0}_{*}\tilde{\partial}^{MV}_{*}{\mathtt{Ind}}\mathscr{X}(\hat{\not{D}},\mathrm{on}\,\hat{M}_{{\mathcal{Y}}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{ResultSecondDiagram}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\beta\cdot\delta_{0,*}^{MV}{\mathtt{Ind}}\mathscr{X}(\Sigma\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}_{h^{\prime}},\mathrm{on}\,{\mathbb{R}}\times\tilde{M}_{h^{\prime},{\mathcal{Y}}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{SuspensionTheorem}}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle{\mathtt{Ind}}\mathscr{X}(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}_{h^{\prime}},\mathrm{on}\,\tilde{M}_{h^{\prime},{\mathcal{Y}}})\ ,

as desired.
∎

We now assume that
\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{t\to\infty}h(t)=\infty
.
Then the identity of underlying sets is a map

\iota:\tilde{M}\to{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(M)

(4.25)

in
\mathbf{BC}
. It sends
\tilde{M}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}
to
{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}(X)
.

We assume that
\not{D}
is positive away from
{\mathcal{Y}}
.

Definition 4.14
.

We define the symbol of
\not{D}
with support in
{\mathcal{Y}}
as the class

\sigma_{{\mathcal{Y}}}(\not{D}):=\iota_{*}(\beta\cdot\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}},\mathrm{on}\,\tilde{M}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}))\quad\mbox{in}\quad K_{{\mathcal{Y}},-n}^{\mathscr{X}}(M)\cong K\mathscr{X}_{-n+1}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}^{\infty}(M))\ .

For
{\mathcal{Y}}=\{M\}
we write
\sigma(\not{D}):=\sigma_{\{M\}}(\not{D})
in
K^{\mathscr{X}}_{-n}(M)
.
By
Proposition

4.13
the class
\iota_{*}(\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\smash{\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}},\mathrm{on}\,\tilde{M}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}))
is independent of the choice of the warping function
h
.
Consequently the symbol class
\sigma_{{\mathcal{Y}}}(\not{D})
is well-defined.

Recall the Paschke morphism (
3.18
).

Definition 4.15
.

We define
\sigma^{\mathrm{an}}(\not{D}):=p_{M}(\sigma(\not{D}))
in
K^{\mathrm{an}}_{-n}(M)
.

Remark 4.16
.

In this remark we explain how
\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D})
can be recovered from
\sigma^{\mathrm{an}}(\not{D})
.
Let
g
be the Riemannian metric on
M
.
Then we consider a Riemannian metric
g^{\prime}
with
g\leq g^{\prime}
, which is automatically complete, and let
M^{\prime}
be the uniform bornological coarse space presented by the new metric.
Then the identity map of underlying sets
i:M^{\prime}\to M
is a contraction and therefore a morphism in
\mathbf{UBC}
. Let
\not{D}^{\prime}
be the adaptation of
\not{D}
to the new metric
g^{\prime}
(see
Remark

4.11
).
Using an analogous argument as in
Proposition

4.13
based on
\hat{M}={\mathbb{R}}\times M
with warped product metric metric
dt^{2}+\chi(t)g^{\prime}+(1-\chi(t))g
one can show that
i_{*}\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D}^{\prime})=\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D})
.
We get a commutative diagram

7.99713pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@stopper}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 145.07816pt\raise-16.00891pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.6014pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{a_{M^{\prime}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 145.07816pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-25.61185pt\raise-32.01782pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D})}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 68.83769pt\raise-32.01782pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 118.06354pt\raise-32.01782pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D}^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 118.06354pt\raise-32.01782pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@stopper}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 66.03415pt\raise-26.20186pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.81596pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{i_{*}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 25.61185pt\raise-32.01782pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ .

We now choose the metric
g^{\prime}
adapted to a locally finite triangulation of
M
so that

p_{M^{\prime}}
is an equivalence and get the dotted arrow (see
Example

2.34
).
We then have
\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D})=a^{\mathrm{lf}}_{M^{\prime}}(\sigma^{\mathrm{an}}(M))
.
\blacksquare

Let
a_{M,{\mathcal{Y}}}:K_{{\mathcal{Y}}}^{\mathscr{X}}(M)\to K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Y}})
be the index map (
3.12
).

Lemma 4.17
.

We have the equality

a_{M,{\mathcal{Y}}}(\sigma_{{\mathcal{Y}}}(\not{D}))=\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D},\mathrm{on}\,{\mathcal{Y}})\quad\mbox{in}\quad K\mathscr{X}_{-n}({\mathcal{Y}})\ .

Proof.

The first step in the construction of
a_{M,{\mathcal{Y}}}
is the application of the cone boundary
\partial^{\mathrm{cone}}:{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(M)\to{\mathbb{R}}\otimes M
from (
2.9
).
By
Proposition

4.13
, it sends the class
\iota_{*}(\beta\cdot\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\smash{\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}},\mathrm{on}\,\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}))
to the class
\beta\cdot\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\Sigma\not{D},\mathrm{on}\,\tilde{M}_{{\mathcal{Y}}})
in
K\mathscr{X}_{-n+1}((\tilde{M}_{{\mathcal{Y}}})_{{\mathbb{R}}\otimes M})
, where the subscript inducates that
\tilde{M}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}
has the bornological coarse structures induced from
{\mathbb{R}}\otimes M
and
\iota
is as in (
4.25
).

The second step is the application of the Mayer-Vietoris boundary
for the decomposition
(\{{\mathbb{R}}^{-}\}\times{\mathcal{Y}},\{{\mathbb{R}}^{+}\}\times M)
.
Using that

\Sigma\not{D}
is actually positive away from

{\mathbb{R}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}
and the suspension theorem,
Proposition

4.8
, the image of this class under the Mayer-Vietoris boundary is
\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D},\mathrm{on}\,{\mathcal{Y}})
in
K\mathscr{X}_{-n}({\mathcal{Y}})
.
∎

Remark 4.18
.

Assume that
\not{D}
is positive on
M
. Then we can take
{\mathcal{Y}}=\{\emptyset\}
. We get a class

\sigma_{\{\emptyset\}}(\not{D})
in
K^{\mathscr{X}}_{\{\emptyset\},-n}(M)
which captures the reason for

\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D})=0
, usually called the
\rho
-invariant, see
Remark

3.27
.
\blacksquare

We consider a section

\Psi
in
C^{\infty}(M,{\mathtt{End}}_{{\mathtt{Cl}}^{n}}(E)^{\mathrm{odd}})
.

Definition 4.19
.

We say that
\Psi
is a potential for
\not{D}
if
\Psi
takes values in selfadjoint endomorphisms and the graded commutator
[\not{D},\Psi]
is an operator of order zero.

Note that
[\not{D},\Psi]
is a zero-order operator if
\Psi

commutes in the graded sense with the symbol of
\not{D}
.

If
\Psi
is a potential for
\not{D}
, then we can form the new Dirac type operator
\not{D}+\Psi
of degree
n
.

Definition 4.20
.

We call
\not{D}+\Psi
the Callias type operator associated to
\not{D}
and
\Psi
.

We will consider the condition that
\not{D}+\Psi
is positive outside of a subset
Y
of
M
. But we want the stronger condition that this positivity is caused by
\Psi
alone. To this end we
calculate

(\not{D}+\Psi)^{2}=\not{D}^{2}+[\not{D},\Psi]+\Psi^{2}\ .

(4.26)

By
\sigma(A)
we denote the spectrum of a selfadjoint operator
A
. So
\sigma(\Psi^{2}(m))
below is the spectrum of the finite-dimensional operator
\Psi^{2}(m)
on the fibre
E_{m}
of the bundle
E
at
m
and
\min\>\sigma(\Psi^{2}(m))
is its minimal element.

Definition 4.21
.

We say that
\Psi
is positive away from
Y
if

\inf_{m\in M\setminus Y}\Big({\min}\>\sigma(\Psi^{2}(m))-\|\nabla\Psi(m)\|\Big)>0\ .

(4.27)

If
{\mathcal{Y}}
is a big family then we say that
\Psi
is positive away from
{\mathcal{Y}}
if it is positive away from a member of
{\mathcal{Y}}
.

Lemma 4.22
.

If
\Psi
is positive away from
Y
, then
\not{D}+\Psi
is positive away from
Y
in the sense of
Definition

4.2
.

Proof.

For
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(M\setminus\bar{Y},E)
, we have

\displaystyle\|(\not{D}+\Psi)\phi\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\|\not{D}\phi\|^{2}+\langle\phi,([\not{D},\Psi]+\Psi^{2})\phi\rangle

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{m\in M\setminus Y}\Big(\min\sigma(\Psi^{2}(m))-\|[\not{D},\Psi]\|\Big)\|\phi\|^{2}\ .

As
\|[\not{D},\Psi]\|\leq\|\nabla\Psi\|
pointwise, the result follows from the assumption of (
4.27
).
∎

As a consequence of
Lemma

4.22
, if
\Psi
is positive away from
Y
, then by
Definition

4.7

we get a class

\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D}+\Psi,\mathrm{on}\,{\mathcal{Y}})\quad\mbox{in}\quad K\mathscr{X}_{-n}({\mathcal{Y}})\ .

We abbreviate
\tilde{\Psi}:={\mathtt{pr}}^{*}\Psi\otimes{\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathtt{Cl}}^{1}}
in
C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}\times M,\tilde{E})
and let
\smash{\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}}
as above be the adaptation of
\Sigma\not{D}
to the warped product metric on
{\mathbb{R}}\times M
(see
Construction

4.10
).
Then
\smash{\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}}+\tilde{\Psi}
is the adaptation of
\Sigma(\not{D}+\Psi)
.
Therefore by definition, with
\iota
as in (
4.25
)

\iota_{*}(\beta\cdot\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi}))=\sigma(\not{D}+\Psi)\quad\mbox{in}\quad K_{-n}^{\mathscr{X}}(M)\ .

Lemma 4.23
.

\tilde{\Psi}
is positive away from
{\mathbb{R}}\times Y
.

Proof.

We note that

\|\nabla\tilde{\Psi}(t,m)\|^{2}={h^{-2}(t)}\|\nabla\Psi(m)\|^{2}\ .

(4.28)

Since
h^{-1}(t)\leq 1
for all
t
in
{\mathbb{R}}
we have

\min(\sigma(\tilde{\Psi}(t,m)^{2})-{\|\nabla\tilde{\Psi}(t,m)\|}\geq\min(\sigma(\Psi(m)^{2})-{\|\nabla\Psi(m)\|}

for each
m
in
M\setminus Y
and each
t
in
{\mathbb{R}}
.
Taking the infimum over
{\mathbb{R}}\times(M\setminus Y)
, the result follows from the positivity of
\Psi
away from
Y
.

∎

Assume that
\Psi
is positive away from
Y
.

Definition 4.24
.

We define the symbol with support of the Callias type operator
\not{D}+\Psi
by

\sigma(\not{D}+\Psi,\mathrm{on}\,{\mathcal{Y}}):=\iota_{*}(\beta\cdot\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi},\mathrm{on}\,{\mathcal{Y}}))\quad\mbox{in}\quad K^{\mathscr{X}}_{-n}({\mathcal{Y}})\ .

The symbol is well-defined by
Lemma

4.23
,
Lemma

4.22
and
Definition

4.7
.

Lemma

4.17
implies:

Corollary 4.25
.

We have the equality

a_{{\mathcal{Y}}}(\sigma(\not{D}+\Psi,\mathrm{on}\,{\mathcal{Y}}))=\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D}+\Psi,\mathrm{on}\,{\mathcal{Y}})\quad\mbox{in}\quad K\mathscr{X}_{-n}({\mathcal{Y}})\ .

Remark 4.26
.

Assume that
\not{D}
is positive away from a big family
{\mathcal{Z}}
. Without further assumptions on
\Psi
the construction of the Callias type operator
\not{D}+\Psi
destroys this positivity
since we do not have control over the term
[\not{D},\Psi]
on
(X\setminus Z)\cap Y
.
So we can not conclude that
\not{D}+\Psi
is positive away from
Y\cap Z
.

\blacksquare

In order to improve on the point made in
Remark

4.26
we adopt the following definition.
Let
\Psi
be a potential and
{\mathcal{Z}}
be a big family on
M
. We consider the big family

\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}:=\{{\mathbb{R}}^{-}\}\times({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})\cup\{{\mathbb{R}}^{+}\}\times{\mathcal{Y}}=\tilde{M}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\cap({\mathbb{R}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})

(4.29)

in
\tilde{M}
.

Definition 4.27
.

We say
\Psi
is asymptotically constant away from
{\mathcal{Z}}
if

\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{Z\in{\mathcal{Z}}}\|\nabla\Psi_{|M\setminus Z}\|_{\infty}=0\ .

Lemma 4.28
.

We assume that
\not{D}
is positive away from
{\mathcal{Z}}
, and that
\Psi
is positive away from
{\mathcal{Y}}
and asymptotically constant away from
{\mathcal{Z}}
.
Then
\smash{\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+{\tilde{\Psi}}}
is positive away from
\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}
.

Proof.

By
Lemma

4.22
and
Lemma

4.23
we know that
\smash{\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+{\tilde{\Psi}}}
is positive away from
{\mathbb{R}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}
.
We can therefore find a member
Y
of
{\mathcal{Y}}
and number
b
in
(0,\infty)
such that

\|(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi})\tilde{\phi}\|^{2}\geq b\|\tilde{\phi}\|^{2}

for any compactly supported section
\tilde{\phi}
of
{\mathtt{pr}}^{*}E
with support on
{\mathbb{R}}\times(M\setminus\bar{Y})
.

By (
4.26
),
for any compactly supported smooth section
\tilde{\phi}
of
{\mathtt{pr}}^{*}E
we have

\|(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi})\tilde{\phi}\|^{2}\geq\|\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}\tilde{\phi}\|^{2}-\|\tilde{\nabla}\Psi_{|{\mathtt{supp}}(\tilde{\phi})}\|_{\infty}\|\tilde{\phi}\|^{2}+\|\tilde{\Psi}\tilde{\phi}\|^{2}\ .

(4.30)

Assume that
Z
is a member of
{\mathcal{Z}}
and
a
is a number in
(0,\infty)
such that the quadratic form associated to

\smash{\not{D}^{2}}
is bounded below by
\smash{a^{2}}
on
M\setminus Z
.
Then by (the proof of)
Lemma

4.12
, the quadratic form associated to
\smash{\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}{}^{2}}
is bounded below by
a^{2}
on
{\mathbb{R}}^{-}\times(M\setminus Z)
.
We now enlarge the member
Z
of
{\mathcal{Z}}
such that in addition
\|\nabla\Psi_{|M\setminus Z}\|_{\infty}\leq a^{2}/2
.
Then by (
4.28
) and the fact that
h
is bounded below by
1
we have
\|\smash{\tilde{\nabla}\tilde{\Psi}_{|{\mathbb{R}}\times(M\setminus Z)}}\|_{\infty}\leq a^{2}/2
.
Consequently, if
\tilde{\phi}
is supported on
(-\infty,0)\times M\setminus\bar{Z}
, then the right-hand side of (
4.30
) is larger than
a^{2}/2\cdot\|\tilde{\phi}\|^{2}
.

Since any compactly supported section
\tilde{\phi}
of
{\mathtt{pr}}^{*}E
supported on
{\mathbb{R}}\times(M\setminus\bar{Y})\cup(-\infty,0)\times(M\setminus\bar{Z})
can be written as a sum of two sections, one compactly supported on
{\mathbb{R}}\times(M\setminus\bar{Y})
and one on
(-\infty,0)\times(M\setminus\bar{Z})
, we see that for such sections

\|(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi})\tilde{\phi}\|^{2}\geq\min(b,\frac{a^{2}}{2})\|\tilde{\phi}\|^{2}\ .

This shows that
\smash{\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+{\tilde{\Psi}}}
is positive away from
\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}
, because
{\mathbb{R}}\times(M\setminus\bar{Y})\cup{\mathbb{R}}^{-}\times(M\setminus\bar{Z})
contains the complement of the member
{\mathbb{R}}^{-}\times(U_{1}[Y]\cap U_{1}[Z])\cup{\mathbb{R}}^{+}\times U_{1}[Y]
of
\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{\mathcal{Z}}
, where
U_{1}
is the metric entourage of width
1

∎

Definition 4.29
.

Under the assumptions of
Lemma

4.28
, we define

\sigma_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(\not{D}+\Psi,\mathrm{on}\,{\mathcal{Y}}):=\iota_{*}(\beta\cdot\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi},\mbox{on {$\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{\mathcal{Z}}$}
}))

in
K^{\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathcal{Z}},-n}({\mathcal{Y}})
.

Lemma 4.30
.

Under the assumptions of
Lemma

4.28

we have

a_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Z}}}(\sigma_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(\not{D}+\Psi,\mathrm{on}\,{\mathcal{Y}}))=\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D}+\Psi,\mathrm{on}\,{\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})

(4.31)

in
K\mathscr{X}_{-n}({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})
.

Proof.

We have a diagram

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 5.77779pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-5.77779pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 23.45761pt\raise-11.42361pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\iota}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 37.59378pt\raise-23.86111pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 51.42218pt\raise 5.31596pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.31596pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{i}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 94.59727pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 47.18753pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 99.4523pt\raise-6.33333pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise
