0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ .

in
\mathbf{BC}
all induced by the identity maps of the underlying sets.
In view of the definition of
a_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Z}}}
in terms of the cone boundary and the suspension equivalence
we get

\kappa_{*}a_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Z}}}(\sigma_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(\not{D}+\Psi,\mathrm{on}\,{\mathcal{Y}}))=\partial^{MV}i_{*}(\beta\cdot\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi},\mbox{on {$\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{\mathcal{Z}}$})})\ ,

where
\partial^{MV}
is the Mayer-Vietoris boundary for the decomposition
of
\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{\mathcal{Z}}
into
\{{\mathbb{R}}^{-}\}\times({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})
and
\{{\mathbb{R}}^{+}\}\times{\mathcal{Y}}

and
\kappa:{\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}}=\{0\}\times({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})\to\{{\mathbb{R}}^{-}\}\times({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})\cap\{{\mathbb{R}}^{+}\}\times{\mathcal{Y}}

consist of coarse equivalences and induces an equivalence in coarse
K
-homology.
By
Proposition

4.13

we have

i_{*}\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi},\mbox{on {$\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{\mathcal{Z}}$})}=\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\Sigma(\not{D}+\Psi),\mathrm{on}\,\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{\mathcal{Z}})\ .

The right-hand side actually comes from an index supported on the smaller family
{\mathbb{R}}\times({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})
.
The equality (
4.31
) now follows from the suspension theorem
Proposition

4.8
, as the equivalence
\Sigma^{-1}K\mathscr{X}({\mathbb{R}}\otimes({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}}))\simeq K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})
is induced by the Mayer-Vietoris boundary.
∎

4.3
Callias type operators and pairings

As in the previous section we consider a generalized Dirac operator
\not{D}
of degree
n
on a bundle
E\to M
which is positive away from a big family
{\mathcal{Z}}
(see
Definition

4.2
) and a potential
\Psi
in
C^{\infty}(M,{\mathtt{End}}_{{\mathtt{Cl}}^{n}}(E)^{\mathrm{odd}})
which is very positive away from a second big family
{\mathcal{Y}}
(see
Definition

4.31
below) and asymptotically constant away from
{\mathcal{Z}}
(see
Definition

4.27
).
In this section, we express the coarse index
\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D}+\Psi,\mathrm{on}\,{\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})
in
{K}\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})
and the symbol
\sigma_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(\not{D}+\Psi,\mathrm{on}\,{\mathcal{Y}})
in
{K}^{\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}({\mathcal{Y}})
of the Callias type operator
\not{D}+\Psi
in terms of the coarse corona pairing
\cap^{\mathscr{X}}
and the coarse symbol pairing
\cap^{\mathscr{X}\sigma}
introduced in
Definition

3.35
and
Definition

3.40
, respectively.

We assume that
n=k+l
for integers
k,l
and
E\cong E_{0}\stimes V
. Here
V
is a finite-dimensional graded Hilbert space with a right action of
{\mathtt{Cl}}^{l}
such that the generators of
{\mathtt{Cl}}^{l}
act by anti-selfadjoint operators. Furthermore
E_{0}\to M
is a graded hermitean bundle
of
{\mathtt{Cl}}^{k}
-modules carrying a Dirac operator
\not{D}_{0}
of degree
k
. We assume that
\not{D}=\not{D}_{0}\stimes{\mathtt{id}}_{V}
and that
\Psi={\mathtt{id}}_{E_{0}}\stimes\Psi_{0}
for some map
\Psi_{0}:M\to{\mathtt{End}}_{{\mathtt{Cl}}^{l}}(V)^{\mathrm{odd}}
with selfadjoint values.
In particular, the graded Roe algebra of
{\mathtt{Cl}}^{k+l}
-invariant operators associated to the bundle
E\to M
is given by

C^{*}(M,E)\cong{\mathtt{End}}(V)\stimes C^{*}(M,E_{0})\ ,

where
{\mathtt{End}}(V)
denotes the graded algebra of
{\mathtt{Cl}}^{l}
-equivariant operators on
V
and
C^{*}(M,E_{0})
is the graded Roe algebra of
{\mathtt{Cl}}^{k}
-equivariant operators on
E_{0}\to M
.
We consider a big family
{\mathcal{Y}}
on
M
.
Recall
Definition

4.21
of the notion of local positivity of
\Psi_{0}
.

Definition 4.31
.

We say that
\Psi_{0}
is very positive away from
{\mathcal{Y}}
if it is positive away from
{\mathcal{Y}}
and
e^{-\Psi^{2}}
belongs to
{\mathtt{End}}(V)\stimes C_{u}({\mathcal{Y}})
.

If
\Psi_{0}
is very positive away from
{\mathcal{Y}}
, then it generates a homomorphism

\Psi_{0,*}:{\mathcal{S}}\to{\mathtt{End}}(V)\stimes C_{u}({\mathcal{Y}})

which represents a class

[\Psi_{0}]
in
K^{\mathrm{gr}}_{0}({\mathtt{End}}(V)\stimes C_{u}({\mathcal{Y}}))\cong K^{l}({\mathcal{Y}})
, see
Remark

2.49
.

Recall the coarse symbol pairing
Definition

3.40
.
Let
{\mathcal{Z}}
be a second big family on
M
.

Theorem 4.32
.

Assume:

1.

\not{D}_{0}
is positive away from
{\mathcal{Z}}
.

2.

\Psi_{0}
is asymptotically constant away from
{\mathcal{Z}}
.

3.

\Psi_{0}
is very positive away from
{\mathcal{Y}}
.

4.

\|\nabla\Psi_{0}\|
is uniformly bounded on
M
.

Then we
have the equality

\sigma_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(\not{D}+\Psi,\mathrm{on}\,{\mathcal{Y}})=[\Psi_{0}]\cap^{\mathscr{X}\sigma}\sigma_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(\not{D}_{0})

(4.32)

in
K^{\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathcal{Z}},-n}({\mathcal{Y}})
.

Remark 4.33
.

Note that the right-hand side of (
4.32
) is defined without assuming
2
. But this assumption is necessary in order to get the left-hand side well-defined.
Indeed, the conditions imply that
Definition

4.29
applies and provides the class

\sigma_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(\not{D}+\Psi,\mathrm{on}\,{\mathcal{Y}})\quad\mbox{in}\quad K^{\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathcal{Z}},-n}({\mathcal{Y}})\ .

Both sides of (
4.32
) do not require the technical condition
4
which is only needed in the proof of this equality.

\blacksquare

Proof.

We apply
Construction

4.10
to obtain the operators
\smash{\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}}
and
\smash{\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi}}
on
\tilde{M}
.
Since
\not{D}
is positive away from
{\mathcal{Z}}
,
Lemma

4.12
implies that
\smash{\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}}
is positive away from the big family
\tilde{M}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\subset\tilde{M}
as defined in (
4.15
).
Hence by
Lemma

4.6
, there exists
a
in
(0,\infty)

such that

\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}_{0,*}:C_{0}((-a,a))\to C^{*}(\tilde{M}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\subseteq\tilde{M},\tilde{E}_{0})

is well-defined.
Similarly, as by
Lemma

4.22
,
D+\Psi
is positive away from
{\mathcal{Y}}
, the operator
\smash{\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi}}
on
\tilde{M}
is positive away from the big family
\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}
defined in (
4.29
), and we get a well-defined
*
-homomorphism

(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi})_{*}:C_{0}((-a,a))\to C^{*}(\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\subseteq\tilde{M},\tilde{E})\ .

We also have

\Psi_{0,*}:C_{0}((-a,a))\to{\mathtt{End}}(V)\stimes C_{u}({\mathcal{Y}})\ .

Recall from
Section

2.4
that the coproduct
\Delta:{\mathcal{S}}\to{\mathcal{S}}\stimes{\mathcal{S}}
is the
*
-homomorphism determined uniquely by

f\mapsto f(X\stimes 1+1\stimes X)\ ,

where
X
is the unbounded multiplier of
{\mathcal{S}}
given by
(Xf)(x)=xf(x)
.
The multiplier
X
restricts to a bounded multiplier on each of the subalgebras
C_{0}((-a,a))
.
In particular we get a restriction

\Delta_{a}:C_{0}((-a,a))\to C_{0}((-a,a))\stimes C_{0}((-a,a))

of the coproduct, making the
diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C((-a,a))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C((-a,a))\stimes C((-a,a))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{S}}\stimes{\mathcal{S}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{a}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon\otimes\epsilon}

(4.33)

commute,
where
\epsilon:C_{0}((-a,a))\to{\mathcal{S}}
is the extension-by-zero map.

In view of the above and Remarks
2.49
,
2.50
, the composition

C_{0}((-a,a))\xrightarrow{\Delta_{a}}C_{0}((-a,a))\stimes C_{0}((-a,a))\xrightarrow{\Psi_{0,*}\stimes\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}_{0,*}}{\mathtt{End}}(V)\stimes C_{u}({\mathcal{Y}})\stimes C^{*}({\tilde{M}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}}\subseteq\tilde{M},\tilde{E}_{0})

represents the product

[\Psi_{0}]\cup[\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}_{0}]\quad\mbox{in}\quad K^{\mathrm{gr}}_{0}({\mathtt{End}}(V)\stimes C_{u}({\mathcal{Y}})\stimes C^{*}({\tilde{M}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}}\subseteq\tilde{M},\tilde{E}_{0}))\ .

In analogy to
Lemma

3.39
(with the same arguments and using the analogue of
Corollary

3.11
for
A
in
C^{*}(\tilde{M},\tilde{E})
),
there is a well-defined pairing

\hat{\mu}:{\mathtt{End}}(V)\stimes C_{u}({\mathcal{Y}})\stimes C^{*}(\tilde{M}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\subseteq\tilde{M},\tilde{E}_{0})\to\frac{C^{*}(\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\subseteq\tilde{M},\tilde{E})}{C^{*}(\{{\mathbb{R}}^{-}\}\times({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})\subseteq\tilde{M},\tilde{E})}

(4.34)

defined as the matrix extension of
\hat{\mu}(f\stimes A):=[\chi({\mathtt{pr}}^{*}f\stimes{\mathtt{id}})({\mathtt{id}}\stimes A)]
.

Lemma 4.34
.

The diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C((-a,a))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{*}(\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\subseteq\tilde{M},\tilde{E})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{0}((-a,a))\stimes C((-a,a))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathtt{End}}(V)\stimes C_{u}({\mathcal{Y}})\stimes C^{*}(\tilde{M}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\subseteq\tilde{M},\tilde{E}_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\displaystyle\frac{C^{*}(\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\subseteq\tilde{M},\tilde{E})}{C^{*}(\{{\mathbb{R}}^{-}\}\times({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})\subseteq\tilde{M},\tilde{E})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{a}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi_{0,*}\stimes\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}_{0,*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\mu}}

(4.35)

commutes, where the right vertical map
p_{0}
is the quotient projection.

Proof.

We have a sequence of horizontal ideal inclusions and projections

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{*}(\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\subseteq\tilde{M},\tilde{E})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{*}(\tilde{M},\tilde{E})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}}(C^{*}(\tilde{M},\tilde{E}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\displaystyle\frac{C^{*}(\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\subseteq\tilde{M},\tilde{E})}{C^{*}(\{{\mathbb{R}}^{-}\}\times({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})\subseteq\tilde{M},\tilde{E})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\displaystyle\frac{C^{*}(\tilde{M},\tilde{E})}{C^{*}(\{{\mathbb{R}}^{-}\}\times M\subseteq\tilde{M},\tilde{E})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\displaystyle\frac{{\mathcal{M}}(C^{*}(\tilde{M},\tilde{E}))}{C^{*}(\{{\mathbb{R}}^{-}\}\times M\subseteq\tilde{M},\tilde{E})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces!}

where
{\mathcal{M}}(\cdot)
denotes taking the multiplier algebra.
In order to see that the marked arrow is injective we observe that

C^{*}(\{{\mathbb{R}}^{-}\}\times({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})\subseteq\tilde{M},\tilde{E})=C^{*}(\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\subseteq\tilde{M},\tilde{E})\cap C^{*}(\{{\mathbb{R}}^{-}\}\times M\subseteq\tilde{M},\tilde{E})\ .

The diagram (
4.35
) therefore maps injectively to the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}}(C^{*}(\tilde{M},\tilde{E}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{S}}\stimes{\mathcal{S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathtt{End}}(V)\stimes C_{u}({\mathcal{Y}})\stimes C^{*}(\tilde{M},\tilde{E}_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\displaystyle\frac{{\mathcal{M}}(C^{*}(\tilde{M},\tilde{E}))}{C^{*}(\{{\mathbb{R}}^{-}\}\times M\subseteq\tilde{M},\tilde{E})}\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi_{0,*}\stimes\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}_{0,*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\mu}}

(4.36)

where
\hat{\mu}
is the extension of (
4.34
) defined by the same formula.
We must check the commutativity of (
4.36
). By continuity and a density argument it suffices to check this commutativity on the elements
e^{-tx^{2}}
and
xe^{-tx^{2}}
of
{\mathcal{S}}
for all
t
in
(0,\infty)
.
The coproducts of these elements are given by

\Delta(e^{-tx^{2}})=e^{-tx^{2}}\stimes e^{-tx^{2}}\ ,\quad\Delta(xe^{-tx^{2}})=xe^{-tx^{2}}\stimes e^{-tx^{2}}+e^{-tx^{2}}\stimes xe^{-tx^{2}}\ .

(4.37)

The first relation in (
4.37
) implies the first equality in

\hat{\mu}((\Psi_{0,*}\stimes\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}_{0,*})(\Delta(e^{-tx^{2}})))=\hat{\mu}(e^{-t\Psi_{0}^{2}}\stimes e^{-t\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}{}_{0}^{2}})=p(e^{-t\tilde{\Psi}^{2}})p(e^{-t\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}{}^{2}})\ ,

and the second is a reformuation of the definition
\hat{\mu}
in terms of
p
.

By the usual finite propagation speed argument (use
Lemma

4.6
with
{\mathcal{Y}}=\{\tilde{M}\}
) we have
e^{-t\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}{}^{2}}\in C^{*}(\tilde{M},\tilde{E})
.
In contrast, the operator
\smash{e^{-t\tilde{\Psi}^{2}}}
is not contained in
C^{*}(\tilde{M},\tilde{E})
, but it is fortunately a multiplier for this algebra.
For this reason we passed to multiplier algebras above.

The maps
t\mapsto e^{-tx^{2}}
and
t\mapsto xe^{-tx^{2}}
both are smooth functions
(0,\infty)\to{\mathcal{S}}
.
Consequently, postcomposing these functions with any
*
-homomorphism
{\mathcal{S}}\to A
yields smooth functions
(0,\infty)\to A
.
In particular,

t\mapsto\tilde{\Psi}_{*}(e^{-tx^{2}})=\smash{e^{-t\tilde{\Psi}{}^{2}}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad t\mapsto\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}_{*}(e^{-tx^{2}})=\smash{e^{-t\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}{}^{2}}}

are smooth functions with values in the right upper corner of (
4.36
). We will denote their derivatives suggestively by

-\smash{\tilde{\Psi}{}^{2}e^{-t\tilde{\Psi}{}^{2}}}
and
-\smash{\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}{}^{2}e^{-t\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}{}^{2}}}
, respectively.

Since
\Psi_{0}
is very positive away from
{\mathcal{Y}}
by Assumption
3
, we have
e^{-t\Psi_{0}^{2}}\in{\mathtt{End}}(V)\stimes C_{u}({\mathcal{Y}})
.
Using only boundedness and uniform continuity, the argument given in the proof of
Lemma

3.39
shows that

\smash{e^{-t\tilde{\Psi}_{0}^{2}}}={\mathtt{pr}}^{*}e^{-t\Psi_{0}^{2}}\in{\mathtt{End}}(V)\stimes\smash{\ell^{\infty}_{\{{\mathbb{R}}^{-}\}\times M}{(\tilde{M})}}\ .

By
Corollary

3.11
, for all
s,t
in
(0,\infty)
the commutator of
\smash{e^{-s\tilde{\Psi}^{2}}}
and
\smash{e^{-t\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}{}^{2}}}
is contained in
C^{*}(\{{\mathbb{R}}^{-}\}\times M\subseteq\tilde{M},\tilde{E})
.
Consequently,
\smash{p(e^{-t\tilde{\Psi}^{2}})}
and
\smash{p(e^{-s\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}{}^{2}})}
commute, and so do their derivatives.
For example,
\smash{p(e^{-s\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}{}^{2}})}
commutes with
\smash{p(\tilde{\Psi}^{2}e^{-t\tilde{\Psi}^{2}})}
.

We must show the equality of semigroups

p(e^{-t\tilde{\Psi}^{2}})p(e^{-t\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}{}^{2}})=p(e^{-t(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi})^{2}})\ .

(4.38)

To this end we consider the smooth function

(0,\infty)\times(0,\infty)\ni(s,t)\mapsto p(e^{-s\tilde{\Psi}^{2}})p(e^{-s\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}{}^{2}})p(e^{-t(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi})^{2}})\ \in\frac{{\mathcal{M}}(C^{*}(\tilde{M},\tilde{E}))}{C^{*}(\{{\mathbb{R}}^{-}\}\times M\subseteq\tilde{M},\tilde{E})}.

We calculate

\displaystyle-\frac{\partial}{\partial s}p(e^{-s\tilde{\Psi}^{2}})p(e^{-s\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}{}^{2}})p(e^{-t(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi})^{2}})

(4.39)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle p(\tilde{\Psi}^{2}e^{-s\tilde{\Psi}^{2}})p(e^{-s\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}{}^{2}})p(e^{-t(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi})^{2}})+p(e^{-s\tilde{\Psi}^{2}})p(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}{}^{2}e^{-s\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}{}^{2}})p(e^{-t(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi})^{2}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle p(e^{-s\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}{}^{2}})p(\tilde{\Psi}^{2}e^{-s\tilde{\Psi}^{2}})p(e^{-t(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi})^{2}})+p(e^{-s\tilde{\Psi}^{2}})p(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}{}^{2}e^{-s\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}{}^{2}})p(e^{-t(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi})^{2}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle p(e^{-s\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}{}^{2}})p(\tilde{\Psi}^{2}e^{-s\tilde{\Psi}^{2}}\cdot e^{-t(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi})^{2}})+p(e^{-s\tilde{\Psi}^{2}})p(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}{}^{2}e^{-s\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}{}^{2}}\cdot e^{-t(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi})^{2}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{=}}

\displaystyle p(e^{-s\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}{}^{2}})p(e^{-s\tilde{\Psi}^{2}}\cdot\tilde{\Psi}^{2}e^{-t(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi})^{2}})+p(e^{-s\tilde{\Psi}^{2}})p(e^{-s\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}{}^{2}}\cdot\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}{}^{2}e^{-t(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi})^{2}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle p(e^{-s\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}{}^{2}})p(e^{-s\tilde{\Psi}^{2}})p(\tilde{\Psi}^{2}e^{-t(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi})^{2}})+p(e^{-s\tilde{\Psi}^{2}})p(e^{-s\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}{}^{2}})p(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}{}^{2}e^{-t(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi})^{2}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle p(e^{-s\tilde{\Psi}^{2}})p(e^{-s\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}{}^{2}})p((\tilde{\Psi}^{2}+\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}{}^{2})e^{-t(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi})^{2}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\partial}{\partial t}p(e^{-s\tilde{\Psi}^{2}})p(e^{-s\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}{}^{2}})p(e^{-t(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi})^{2}})

In the marked equality, we used the identities

\displaystyle\tilde{\Psi}^{2}e^{-s\tilde{\Psi}^{2}}\cdot e^{-t(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi})^{2}}

\displaystyle=e^{-s\tilde{\Psi}^{2}}\cdot\tilde{\Psi}^{2}e^{-t(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi})^{2}},

(4.40)

\displaystyle\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}{}^{2}e^{-s\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}{}^{2}}\cdot e^{-t(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi})^{2}}

\displaystyle=e^{-s\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}{}^{2}}\cdot\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}{}^{2}e^{-t(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi})^{2}}

which require justification.
Recall here that we use
\tilde{\Psi}^{2}e^{-s\tilde{\Psi}^{2}}
as a symbol for the operator
{\smash{\tilde{\Psi}_{*}(x^{2}e^{-sx^{2}})=-\partial_{s}e^{-s\tilde{\Psi}^{2}}}}
, and it is not a priori clear that the right hand sides of (
4.40
) are even defined.
Let
{\mathtt{dom}}(\tilde{\Psi}^{2})
denote the domain of the unbounded selfadjoint operator determined by
\smash{\tilde{\Psi}^{2}}
equipped with the
graph norm.
Then
\smash{e^{-s\tilde{\Psi}^{2}}:{\mathtt{dom}}(\tilde{\Psi}^{2})\to H}
is a norm-differentiable family of operators with (negative) derivative

{\mathtt{dom}}(\tilde{\Psi}^{2})\xrightarrow{\tilde{\Psi}^{2}}H\xrightarrow{e^{-s\tilde{\Psi}^{2}}}H\ .

This composition happens to have a continuous extension to a bounded operator on
H
which we denoted by
\tilde{\Psi}^{2}e^{-s\tilde{\Psi}^{2}}
above.
We apply
Lemma

4.38
below with
H=L^{2}(\tilde{M},\tilde{E})
,
\smash{A=t^{1/2}\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}}
,
\smash{B=t^{1/2}\tilde{\Psi}}
,
D=C_{c}^{\infty}(\tilde{M},\tilde{E})
.
Note that
\smash{\{\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}},\tilde{\Psi}\}}
is bounded by Assumption
4
.
Since
\smash{e^{-t(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi})^{2}}:H\to{\mathtt{dom}}((\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi})^{2})}
is continuous,

Lemma

4.38
.
2

implies that
\smash{e^{-t(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi})^{2}}}
corestricts to a bounded map from
H
to
\smash{{\mathtt{dom}}(\tilde{\Psi}^{2})}
.
Hence we can write

\smash{\tilde{\Psi}^{2}e^{-s\tilde{\Psi}^{2}}\cdot e^{-t(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi})^{2}}}
as the composition of bounded operators

H\xrightarrow{e^{-t(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi})^{2}}}{\mathtt{dom}}(\tilde{\Psi}^{2})\xrightarrow{\tilde{\Psi}^{2}}H\xrightarrow{e^{-s\tilde{\Psi}^{2}}}H\ .

This finishes the justification for the first equality in (
4.40
) .
For the second equality, we argue similarly using the claim that
e^{-t(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi})^{2}}
restricts to a bounded map from
H
to
{\mathtt{dom}}(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}{}^{2})
.

By (
4.39
), for every
t
in
(0,\infty)
the function

(0,1)\ni u\mapsto p(e^{-tu\tilde{\Psi}^{2}})p(e^{-tu\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}{}^{2}})p(e^{-(1-u)t(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi})^{2}})

is constant.
This together with
the following two norm limits

\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{u\downarrow 0}e^{-tu\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}{}^{2}}e^{-tu\tilde{\Psi}^{2}}e^{-(1-u)t(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi})^{2}}=e^{-t(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi})^{2}}

(4.41)

and

\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{u\uparrow 1}e^{-tu\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}{}^{2}}e^{-tu\tilde{\Psi}^{2}}e^{-(1-u)t(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi})^{2}}=e^{-t\tilde{\Psi}^{2}}e^{-t\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}{}^{2}}

(4.42)

implies (
4.38
).

We now justify the limits (
4.41
) and (
4.42
).
For the first, we use the decomposition

e^{-tu\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}{}^{2}}e^{-tu\tilde{\Psi}^{2}}e^{-(1-u)t(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi})^{2}}=e^{-tu\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}{}^{2}}e^{-tu\tilde{\Psi}^{2}}e^{-t\frac{1}{2}(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi})^{2}}\cdot e^{-t(\frac{1}{2}-u)(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi})^{2}}\ .

The second factor on the right hand side converges to
e^{-t\frac{1}{2}(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi})^{2}}
and we must show that

\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{u\downarrow 0}e^{-tu\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}{}^{2}}e^{-tu\tilde{\Psi}^{2}}e^{-t\frac{1}{2}(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi})^{2}}=e^{-t\frac{1}{2}(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi})^{2}}\ .

We use the decomposition

H\xrightarrow{e^{-t\frac{1}{2}(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi})^{2}}}{\mathtt{dom}}(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}{}^{2})\cap{\mathtt{dom}}(\tilde{\Psi}^{2})\xrightarrow{e^{-tu\tilde{\Psi}^{2}}}{\mathtt{dom}}(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}})\xrightarrow{e^{-tu\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}{}^{2}}}H\ .

(4.43)

The first map is a bounded operator by
Lemma

4.38
.
2
(applied to

\smash{A=t^{1/2}\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}}
,
\smash{B=t^{1/2}\tilde{\Psi}}
).
The last map is a continuous family of bounded operators which tends to the inclusion
\smash{{\mathtt{dom}}(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}})}\to H
as
u\downarrow 0
.
For the middle map in (
4.43
) we apply
Lemma

4.39
with
\smash{A=\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}}
and
B=\tilde{\Psi}
,
H=L^{2}(\tilde{M},\tilde{E})
and
D=C_{c}^{\infty}(\tilde{M},\tilde{E})
. The assumptions
on the functions of
B
are easily verified.

Lemma

4.39
.
3
states that the middle map is a continuous family of bounded operators which tends to the inclusion if
u\downarrow 0
. This finishes the verification of (
4.41
).

For (
4.42
) we consider the adjoint and use the decomposition

H\xrightarrow{e^{-tu\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}^{2}}}{\mathtt{dom}}(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}})\xrightarrow{e^{-tu\tilde{\Psi}^{2}}}{\mathtt{dom}}(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}})\cap{\mathtt{dom}}(\tilde{\Psi})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{ijgowergwerfwefwef}}.\ref{kotrhperthertgertgetg}}}{{=}}{\mathtt{dom}}(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi})\xrightarrow{e^{-(1-u)t(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi})^{2}}}H\ .

(4.44)

As
u\uparrow 1
the first map converges in norm to

e^{-t\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}^{2}}:H\to{\mathtt{dom}}(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}})
.
The second map is continuous by
Lemma

4.39
.
2
and converges to

\smash{e^{-t\tilde{\Psi}^{2}}:{\mathtt{dom}}(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}})\to{\mathtt{dom}}(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}})\cap{\mathtt{dom}}(\tilde{\Psi})}
as
u\uparrow 1
.
Finally the last map converges in norm to
the inclusion
{\mathtt{dom}}(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi})\to H
.
This gives (
4.42
).

For the odd part we have

\hat{\mu}((\tilde{\Psi}_{0,*}\stimes\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}_{0,*})(\Delta(xe^{-tx^{2}})))=p(\tilde{\Psi}e^{-t\tilde{\Psi}^{2}})p(e^{-t\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}{}^{2}})+p(e^{-t\tilde{\Psi}^{2}})p(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}e^{-t\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}{}^{2}})

and therefore must show that

p(\tilde{\Psi}e^{-t\tilde{\Psi}^{2}})p(e^{-t\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}{}^{2}})+p(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}e^{-t\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}{}^{2}})p(e^{-t\tilde{\Psi}^{2}})=p((\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi})e^{-t(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi})^{2}})\ .

(4.45)

We show

p(\tilde{\Psi}e^{-t\tilde{\Psi}^{2}})p(e^{-t\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}{}^{2}})=p(\tilde{\Psi}e^{-t(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi})^{2}})\ ,

(4.46)

where the right-hand side is defined by
Lemma

4.38
.
1
.
For
\epsilon
in
(0,\infty)
, we observe using the even case that

\displaystyle p(e^{-\epsilon\tilde{\Psi}^{2}}\tilde{\Psi}e^{-t\tilde{\Psi}^{2}})p(e^{-t\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}^{2}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle p(\tilde{\Psi}e^{-\epsilon\tilde{\Psi}^{2}})p(e^{-t\tilde{\Psi}^{2}})p(e^{-t\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}^{2}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle p(\tilde{\Psi}e^{-\epsilon\tilde{\Psi}^{2}})p(e^{-t(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi})^{2}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle p(e^{-\epsilon\tilde{\Psi}^{2}}\tilde{\Psi}e^{-t(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi})^{2}})

Since
\tilde{\Psi}e^{-t(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi})^{2}}
and
\tilde{\Psi}e^{-t\tilde{\Psi}^{2}}
corestrict to bounded maps from
H
to
{\mathtt{dom}}(\tilde{\Psi})
(we use
Lemma

4.38
.
1

for the first case) we can take the limit
\epsilon\downarrow 0
and get (
4.46
).
The idenity

p(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}e^{-t\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}^{2}})p(e^{-t\tilde{\Psi}^{2}})=p(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}e^{-t(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi})^{2}})\

(4.47)

can be shown by an analogous argument.
∎

Corollary 4.35
.

We have the equality

\hat{\mu}_{*}([\Psi_{0}]\cup[\smash{\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}_{0}}])=p_{0,*}([\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi}])\quad\mbox{in}\quad K^{\mathrm{gr}}_{0}\left(\frac{C^{*}(\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\subseteq\tilde{M},\tilde{E})}{C^{*}(\{{\mathbb{R}}^{-}\}\times({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})\subseteq\tilde{M},\tilde{E})}\right)\ .

Recall that on the one hand
\sigma_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(\not{D}+\Psi,\mathrm{on}\,{\mathcal{Y}})
is obtained in
Section

4.2
from the class represented by
(\smash{\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}}+\tilde{\Psi})_{*}
on
\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}
in
K^{\mathrm{gr}}(C^{*}(\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\subseteq\tilde{M},E))
via an identification of the
K
-theory of this Roe algebra with the
K
-theory of the
C^{*}
-category going into the definition of the the coarse
K
-homology functor
K({\mathbf{C}}(\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\subseteq\tilde{M}))\simeq K\mathscr{X}(\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}})
. On the other hand the coarse symbol pairing
-\cap^{\mathscr{X}\sigma}-
is defined in
Definition

3.40
in terms of
the latter
C^{*}
-categories directly. The remaining work in this proof of
Theorem

4.32
consists of transporting the construction of
-\cap^{\mathscr{X}\sigma}-

through these identifications until it can directly be compared with the class represented by
\hat{\mu}(\Psi_{0,*}\stimes\smash{\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}}_{0,*})\Delta_{a}
.

The formula (
3.26
) for
\mu
also defines pairings

{\hat{\mu}_{0}:C_{u}({\mathcal{Y}})\stimes C^{*}(\tilde{M}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\subseteq\tilde{M},\tilde{E}_{0})\to\frac{C^{*}(\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\subseteq\tilde{M},\tilde{E}_{0})}{C^{*}(\{{\mathbb{R}}^{-}\}\times({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})\subseteq\tilde{M},\tilde{E}_{0})}}\ ,

\tilde{\mu}^{\prime}:C_{u}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\tilde{\mathbf{C}}[n](\tilde{M}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\subseteq\tilde{M})\to\frac{\tilde{\mathbf{C}}[n](\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\subseteq\tilde{M})}{\tilde{\mathbf{C}}[n](\{{\mathbb{R}}^{-}\}\times({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})\subseteq\tilde{M})}

and

\mu^{\prime}:C_{u}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes{\mathbf{C}}[n](\tilde{M}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\subseteq\tilde{M})\to\frac{{\mathbf{C}}[n](\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\subseteq\tilde{M})}{{\mathbf{C}}[n](\{{\mathbb{R}}^{-}\}\times({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})\subseteq\tilde{M})}\ ,

where the notation
{\mathbf{C}}[n](-)
and
\tilde{\mathbf{C}}[n](-)
was introduced in
Section

4.1
.

Recall the functor
{\mathcal{I}}
from (
4.8
).

Lemma 4.36
.

The following square commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{u}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\tilde{\mathbf{C}}[n](\tilde{M}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\subseteq\tilde{M})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\displaystyle\frac{\tilde{\mathbf{C}}[n](\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\subseteq\tilde{M})}{\tilde{\mathbf{C}}[n](\{{\mathbb{R}}^{-}\}\times({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})\subseteq\tilde{M})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{u}({\mathcal{Y}})\stimes C^{*}(\tilde{M}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\subseteq\tilde{M},\tilde{E}_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\displaystyle\frac{C^{*}(\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\subseteq\tilde{M},\tilde{E}_{0})}{C^{*}(\{{\mathbb{R}}^{-}\}\times({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})\subseteq\tilde{M},\tilde{E}_{0})}\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\mu}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1\otimes{\mathcal{I}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{I}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\mu}_{0}}

Proof.

Let
A:(H,\chi,U)\to(H^{\prime},\chi^{\prime},U^{\prime})
be a morphism in
\tilde{\mathbf{C}}[n](\tilde{M}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\subseteq\tilde{M})
and
f
be in
{C_{u}({\mathcal{Y}})}
.
Let
\tilde{\chi}
denote the projection-valued measure on
L^{2}(\tilde{M},\tilde{E}_{0})
.
Then we have

\displaystyle(1\otimes{\mathcal{I}})\circ\tilde{\mu}^{\prime}(f\otimes A)

\displaystyle={\mathcal{I}}([\chi^{\prime}({\mathtt{pr}}^{*}f)A])=[U^{\prime}\chi^{\prime}({\mathtt{pr}}^{*}f)AU^{*}]

\displaystyle\hat{\mu}_{0}\circ(1\otimes{\mathcal{I}})(f\otimes A)

\displaystyle=\hat{\mu}_{0}(f\otimes U^{\prime}AU^{*})=[\tilde{\chi}({\mathtt{pr}}^{*}f)U^{\prime}AU^{*}]

The difference of the two representatives is given by

U^{\prime}\chi^{\prime}({\mathtt{pr}}^{*}f)AU^{*}-\tilde{\chi}({\mathtt{pr}}^{*}f){U^{\prime}}AU^{*}=(U^{\prime}\chi^{\prime}({\mathtt{pr}}^{*}f)-\tilde{\chi}({\mathtt{pr}}^{*}f)U^{\prime})AU^{*}\ .

Since
f
is uniformly continuous we have
{\mathtt{pr}}^{*}f\in\ell^{\infty}_{\{{\mathbb{R}}^{-}\}\times M}(\tilde{M})
(see
Remark

3.19
).
Since
U^{\prime}
is controlled for
the coarse structure on
\smash{\tilde{M}}
,
Lemma

3.9
implies that the above difference is in
C^{*}(\{{\mathbb{R}}^{-}\}\times({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})\subseteq\tilde{M},\tilde{E})
.
∎

The morphism
{\mathcal{F}}
from (
4.7
) and the map
\iota
from (
4.25
) induce the respective vertical arrows in the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{u}({\mathcal{Y}})\stimes\tilde{\mathbf{C}}[n](\tilde{M}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\subseteq\tilde{M})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\displaystyle\frac{\tilde{\mathbf{C}}[n](\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\subseteq\tilde{M})}{\tilde{\mathbf{C}}[n](\{{\mathbb{R}}^{-}\}\times({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})\subseteq\tilde{M})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{u}({\mathcal{Y}})\stimes{\mathbf{C}}[n](\tilde{M}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\subseteq\tilde{M})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\displaystyle\frac{{\mathbf{C}}[n](\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\subseteq\tilde{M})}{{\mathbf{C}}[n](\{{\mathbb{R}}^{-}\}\times({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})\subseteq\tilde{M})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{u}({\mathcal{Y}})\stimes{\mathbf{C}}[n]({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\infty}({\mathcal{Y}})\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(M))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\displaystyle\frac{{\mathbf{C}}[n]({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\infty}({\mathcal{Y}})\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(M))}{{\mathbf{C}}[n]({\mathcal{O}}^{-}({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(M))}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\mu}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathtt{id}}\stimes{\mathcal{F}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{F}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathtt{id}}\stimes\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu}

which obviously commutes.
We tensor with
{\mathtt{End}}(V)
, apply
\Sigma^{-l}K^{\mathrm{gr}}
and immediately use the equivalence
\Sigma^{-l}K^{\mathrm{gr}}({\mathtt{End}}(V)\stimes-)\simeq K^{\mathrm{gr}}(-)
in order to save space.
We get a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K^{\mathrm{gr}}(C_{u}({\mathcal{Y}})){\times}K^{\mathrm{gr}}({\mathbf{C}}[n]({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(M)))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K^{\mathrm{gr}}(C_{u}({\mathcal{Y}})\stimes{\mathbf{C}}[n]({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(M))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K^{\mathrm{gr}}(C_{u}({\mathcal{Y}})){\times}K^{\mathrm{gr}}(\tilde{\mathbf{C}}[n](\tilde{M}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\subseteq\tilde{M}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K^{\mathrm{gr}}(\frac{{\mathbf{C}}[n]({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\infty}({\mathcal{Y}})\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}({M}))}{{\mathbf{C}}[n]({\mathcal{O}}^{-}({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}({M}))})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K^{\mathrm{gr}}(C_{u}({\mathcal{Y}})\stimes\tilde{\mathbf{C}}[n](\tilde{M}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\subseteq\tilde{M}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K^{\mathrm{gr}}(C_{u}({\mathcal{Y}})){\times}K^{\mathrm{gr}}(C^{*}(\tilde{M}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\subseteq\tilde{M},\tilde{E}_{0}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K^{\mathrm{gr}}(\frac{\tilde{\mathbf{C}}[n](\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\subseteq\tilde{M})}{\tilde{\mathbf{C}}[n](\{{\mathbb{R}}^{-}\}\times({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})\subseteq\tilde{M})})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Sigma^{-l}}K^{\mathrm{gr}}({\mathtt{End}}(V)\stimes C_{u}({\mathcal{Y}})\stimes C^{*}(\tilde{M}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\subseteq\tilde{M},\tilde{E}_{0}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Sigma^{-l}}K^{\mathrm{gr}}(\frac{C^{*}(\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\subseteq\tilde{M},\tilde{E})}{C^{*}(\{{\mathbb{R}}^{-}\}\times({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})\subseteq\tilde{M},\tilde{E})})\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\boxtimes}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathtt{id}}\times{\mathcal{I}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathtt{id}}\times(\iota\circ{\mathcal{F}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathtt{id}}\stimes{\mathcal{I}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\mu}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathtt{id}}\stimes\iota\circ{\mathcal{F}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{I}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota\circ{\mathcal{F}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\mu}}

We consider the class
([\Psi_{0}],[\smash{\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}_{0}}])
in the lower left corner.
Its image under the up–right composition is by definition
\mu_{*}([\Psi_{0}]\hat{\boxtimes}\beta^{-1}\sigma_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(\not{D}_{0}))
while its image under the right-up composition is
\iota_{*}{\mathcal{F}}_{*}\hat{\mu}_{*}([\Psi_{0}]\cup[\smash{\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}_{0}}])
.
This gives the first equality in the following display, while
Corollary

4.35

implies the second:

\beta^{-1}\mu([\Psi_{0}]\hat{\boxtimes}\sigma_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(\not{D}_{0}))=\iota_{*}{\mathcal{F}}_{*}{\mathcal{I}}^{-1}_{*}\hat{\mu}_{*}([\Psi_{0}]\cup[\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}_{0}])=\iota_{*}{\mathcal{F}}_{*}{\mathcal{I}}^{-1}_{*}([\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi}])\ .

In view of the
Definition

3.40
of the coarse symbol pairing and
Definition

4.7
of the coarse index,
Definition

4.14
of the symbol, and (
4.9
) we can conclude that

[\Psi_{0}]\cap^{\mathscr{X}\sigma}\sigma_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(\not{D}_{0})=\beta\iota_{*}\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi},\mathrm{on}\,\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}})=\sigma_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(\not{D}+\Psi,\mathrm{on}\,{\mathcal{Y}})

as asserted in (
4.32
). ∎

4.4
Technical Results

In this section, we provide some technical results that were used in the proof of the crucial
Lemma

4.34
.

Let
H
be a Hilbert space and
A,B
be two unbounded selfadjoint
operators on
H
.

Assumption 4.37
.

We assume that there is a linear subspace
D
of
{\mathtt{dom}}(A)\cap{\mathtt{dom}}(B)
preserved by both
A
and
B
such that
A^{k}_{|D},B^{k}_{|D}
and
(A_{|D}+B_{|D})^{k}
are essentially selfadjoint for
k=1,2
.
We let
A+B
denote the unique selfadjoint extension of
A_{|D}+B_{|D}
.

If
C
is a selfadjoint operator on
H
with
D\subseteq{\mathtt{dom}}(C)
such that
C_{|D}
is essentially selfadjoint, then

{\mathtt{dom}}(C)
is the closure of
D
with respect to the graph norm
\|x\|_{C}:=\|Cx\|+\|x\|
.
For
k,l
in
{\rm Nature}
we equip
{\mathtt{dom}}(A^{k})\cap{\mathtt{dom}}(B^{l})
with the norm

\|x\|_{A^{k},B^{l}}:=\|x\|_{A^{k}}+\|x\|_{B^{l}}\ .

Lemma 4.38
.

Let
A
and
B
satisfy
Assumption

4.37
and assume in addition
that

\{A,B\}:=AB+BA:D\to H

extends to a bounded operator on
H
.

1.

We have an isomorphism

{\mathtt{dom}}(A+B)={\mathtt{dom}}(A)\cap{\mathtt{dom}}(B)
.

2.

We have an isomorphism

{\mathtt{dom}}((A+B)^{2})={\mathtt{dom}}(A^{2})\cap{\mathtt{dom}}(B^{2})
.

3.

The operator
A:D\to D
extends to a bounded operator

A:{\mathtt{dom}}(A^{2})\cap{\mathtt{dom}}(B^{2})\to{\mathtt{dom}}(A)\cap{\mathtt{dom}}(B)\ .

Proof.

For two norms
\|-\|,\|-\|^{\prime}
on the same domain we write
\|x\|\lesssim\|x\|^{\prime}

for the statement that there exists a constant
C
in
(0,\infty)
such that
\|x\|\leq C\|x\|^{\prime}
for all
x
in the domain.

By the triangle inequality we have

\|-\|_{A+B}\lesssim\|-\|_{A,B}

(4.48)

on
D
.
Using that the restrictions of

A
,
B
and
A+B
are essentially selfadjoint on
D
we can further conclude that

{\mathtt{dom}}(A)\cap{\mathtt{dom}}(B)\subseteq{\mathtt{dom}}(A+B)\ .

This is a non-trivial consequence of our assumptions and does not follow directly
from (
4.48
) which would only imply that the closure of
D
in the norm
\|-\|_{A,B}

is contained in
{\mathtt{dom}}(A+B)
.
Namely, if
\phi
is in
{\mathtt{dom}}(A)\cap{\mathtt{dom}}(B)
, then by the symmetry of
A
and
B

the linear functionals
D\ni\psi\mapsto\langle A\psi,\phi\rangle
and

D\ni\psi\mapsto\langle B\psi,\phi\rangle
are bounded. Then also

D\ni\psi\mapsto\langle(A+B)\psi,\phi\rangle
is bounded which implies
\phi\in{\mathtt{dom}}(A+B)^{*}

and hence
\phi\in{\mathtt{dom}}(A+B)
since
(A+B)_{|D}
is essentially selfadjoint.

For the reverse inclusion

{\mathtt{dom}}(A+B)\subseteq{\mathtt{dom}}(A)\cap{\mathtt{dom}}(B)\ ,

it suffices to show

\|-\|_{A,B}\lesssim\|-\|_{A+B}\ ,

(4.49)

on
D
, as
{\mathtt{dom}}(A+B)
is the closure of
D
with respect to
\|-\|_{A+B}
.
We calculate for
x
in
D

\displaystyle\|(A+B)x\|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle(A+B)x,(A+B)x\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle x,(A+B)^{2}x\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle x,(A^{2}+B^{2}+\{A,B\})x\rangle

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\|Ax\|^{2}+\|Bx\|^{2}-\|\{A,B\}\|\|x\|^{2}

which implies (
4.49
).
This finishes the argument for Assertion
1
.

The argument for Assertion
2
is a more complicated version of the argument above.
It is clear from
(A+B)^{2}=A^{2}+B^{2}+\{A,B\}
and the boundedness of
\{A,B\}
that

\|x\|_{(A+B)^{2}}\lesssim\|x\|_{A^{2},B^{2}}

(4.50)

on
D
.
Using that the restrictions of
A^{2},B^{2}
and
(A+B)^{2}
to
D
are essentially selfadjoint,
we can further conclude

{\mathtt{dom}}(A^{2})\cap{\mathtt{dom}}(B^{2})\subseteq{\mathtt{dom}}((A+B)^{2})\ .

To get the converse inclusion, it suffices to show the reverse estimate

\|x\|_{A^{2},B^{2}}\lesssim\|x\|_{(A+B)^{2}}

(4.51)

on
D
. For
x
in
D

we have

\displaystyle\|(A+B)^{2}x\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\langle x,(A+B)^{4}x\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle x,(A^{2}+B^{2}+\{A,B\})^{2}x\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle x,(A^{4}+B^{4}+A^{2}B^{2}+B^{2}A^{2}+R)x\rangle

\displaystyle=\|A^{2}x\|^{2}+\|B^{2}x\|^{2}+\langle x,(A^{2}B^{2}+B^{2}A^{2})x\rangle+\langle x,Rx\rangle\ ,

where

R:=A^{2}\{A,B\}+\{A,B\}A^{2}+B^{2}\{A,B\}+\{A,B\}B^{2}+\{A,B\}^{2}\ .

Because of

(AB)^{*}AB+(BA)^{*}BA=A^{2}B^{2}+B^{2}A^{2}+\{A,B\}^{2}-A\{A,B\}B-B\{A,B\}A\ ,

we get

\langle x,(A^{2}B^{2}+B^{2}A^{2})x\rangle\geq-\langle x,(\{A,B\}^{2}-A\{A,B\}B-B\{A,B\}A)x\rangle\ .

Therefore

\|(A+B)^{2}x\|^{2}\geq\|A^{2}x\|^{2}+\|B^{2}x\|^{2}-\langle x,(\{A,B\}^{2}-A\{A,B\}B-B\{A,B\}A)x\rangle+\langle x,Rx\rangle\ .

Using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, the boundedness of
\{A,B\}
and estimates of the form

|uv|\leq\epsilon u^{2}+\frac{4}{\epsilon}v^{2}
for every
\epsilon
in
(0,\infty)
several times
we obtain the desired estimate (
4.51
).
This finishes the verification of Assertion
2
.

For Assertion
3
we use the non-trivial consequence of the proof of
Assertion
2
that

{\mathtt{dom}}(A^{2})\cap{\mathtt{dom}}(B^{2})
is the closure of
D
with respect to the norm
\|-\|_{A^{2},B^{2}}
.
It is clear that
A
restricts to a bounded operator
A:{\mathtt{dom}}(A^{2})\cap{\mathtt{dom}}(B^{2})\to{\mathtt{dom}}(A)
.
It suffices to show that

\|BAx\|\lesssim\|x\|_{A^{2},B^{2}}

(4.52)

on
D
.
We calculate

\displaystyle\langle BAx,BAx\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\langle BAx,ABx\rangle+\langle BAx,\{A,B\}x\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\langle ABAx,Bx\rangle+\langle BAx,\{A,B\}x\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle BA^{2}x,Bx\rangle-\langle\{A,B\}Ax,Bx\rangle+\langle BAx,\{A,B\}x\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle A^{2}x,B^{2}x\rangle-\langle\{A,B\}Ax,Bx\rangle+\langle BAx,\{A,B\}x\rangle\ .

We get the estimate

\|BAx\|^{2}\leq\|A^{2}x\|\|B^{2}x\|+\|\{A,B\}\|\|Ax\|\|Bx\|+\|BAx\|\|\{A,B\}\|\|x\|

which implies (
4.52
).
∎

Note that the assumptions for
Lemma

4.38
are invariant under exchanging
A
and
B
.
In the following lemma we break this symmetry.

Lemma 4.39
.

We keep
Assumption

4.37
and assume in addition that

[0,\infty)\ni\epsilon\mapsto e^{-\epsilon B^{2}}x

is a differentiable
{\mathtt{dom}}(A)
-valued function for all
x
in
D
.

1.

The family of commutators

([A,e^{-\epsilon B^{2}}]:D\to H)_{\epsilon\in(0,\infty)}
extends to a norm-continuous family

[0,\infty)\ni\epsilon\mapsto[A,e^{-\epsilon B^{2}}]\in B(H)

vanishing at
\epsilon=0
.

2.

The family of bounded operators

(e^{-\epsilon B^{2}})_{\epsilon\in(0,\infty)}
on
H
restricts to a norm continuous family of bounded operators

(0,\infty)\ni\epsilon\mapsto e^{-\epsilon B^{2}}:{\mathtt{dom}}(A)\to{\mathtt{dom}}(A)\cap{\mathtt{dom}}(B)\ .

3.

The family of bounded operators

(e^{-\epsilon B^{2}})_{\epsilon\in[0,\infty)}
on
H
restricts to a continuous family of bounded operators

[0,\infty)\ni\epsilon\mapsto e^{-\epsilon B^{2}}:{\mathtt{dom}}(A^{2})\cap{\mathtt{dom}}(B^{2})\to{\mathtt{dom}}(A)\ .

Proof.

On
D
we have the relation

[A,B^{2}]=(\{A,B\}B-B\{A,B\})
and therefore

e^{-r\epsilon B^{2}}[A,B^{2}]e^{-(1-r)\epsilon B^{2}}=e^{-r\epsilon B^{2}}(\{A,B\}B-B\{A,B\})e^{-(1-r)\epsilon B^{2}}\ .

Decomposing the right-hand side as

e^{-r\epsilon B^{2}}\cdot\{A,B\}\cdot Be^{-(1-r)\epsilon B^{2}}-e^{-r\epsilon B^{2}}B\cdot\{A,B\}\cdot e^{-(1-r)\epsilon B^{2}}

we see that it extends to a norm-continuous family

(0,\infty)\times(0,1)\ni(\epsilon,r)\mapsto\epsilon\>e^{-r\epsilon B^{2}}[A,B^{2}]e^{-(1-r)\epsilon B^{2}}\in B(H)\ .

We claim the integral of this family over
r
in
[0,1]
exists and defines a norm-continuous family

[0,\infty)\ni\epsilon\mapsto\int_{0}^{1}\epsilon\ e^{-r\epsilon B^{2}}[A,B^{2}]e^{-(1-r)\epsilon B^{2}}dr\in B(H)

vanishing at
\epsilon=0
.
In order to estimate the norm of the intregrand
we split the interval of integration into the halfs
[0,1/2]
and
[1/2,1]
. We further fix
c
in
(0,\infty)
.
We see that there exists a constant
C
in
(0,\infty)
such that for all
r
in
[0,1/2]
and
\epsilon
in
(0,c)

\epsilon\|e^{-r\epsilon B^{2}}\{A,B\}Be^{-(1-r)\epsilon B^{2}}\|\leq\epsilon^{1/2}\|e^{-r\epsilon B^{2}}\|\|\{A,B\}\|\|\epsilon^{1/2}Be^{-(1-r)\epsilon B^{2}}\|\leq C\epsilon^{1/2}

and

\epsilon\|e^{-r\epsilon B^{2}}B\{A,B\}e^{-(1-r)\epsilon B^{2}}\|\leq\epsilon^{1/2}r^{-1/2}\|\epsilon^{1/2}r^{1/2}Be^{-r\epsilon B^{2}}\|\|\{A,B\}\|\|e^{-(1-r)\epsilon B^{2}}\|\leq C\epsilon^{1/2}r^{-1/2}\ .

These estimates imply the claim for the integral over
[0,1/2]
. The other half of the integral is discussed similarly.

For all
x,y
in
D
and
\epsilon
in
(0,\infty)
we have by our assumption that

-\partial_{r}\langle y,[A,e^{-r\epsilon B^{2}}]x\rangle=\langle y,\epsilon\>e^{-rB^{2}}[A,B^{2}]e^{-(1-r)\epsilon B^{2}}x\rangle\ .

Integrating and using that
D
is dense in
H
we get the equality

[A,e^{-\epsilon B^{2}}]=-\epsilon\int_{0}^{1}e^{-r\epsilon B^{2}}[A,B^{2}]e^{-(1-r)\epsilon B^{2}}dr

on
D
. By continuous extension we get a norm-continuous family

[0,\infty)\ni\epsilon\to[A,e^{-\epsilon B^{2}}]\in B(H)

vanishing at
\epsilon=0
. This finishes the proof of Assertion
1
.

We now show Assertion
2
.
We know that

(0,\infty)\ni\epsilon\mapsto e^{-\epsilon B^{2}}:H\to{\mathtt{dom}}(B)

is a norm-continuous family of bounded operators. We must show that

(0,\infty)\ni\epsilon\mapsto e^{-\epsilon B^{2}}:{\mathtt{dom}}(A)\to{\mathtt{dom}}(A)

is a norm-continuous family of bounded operators. To this end we must
see that

(0,\infty)\ni\epsilon\mapsto Ae^{-\epsilon B^{2}}:{\mathtt{dom}}(A)\to H

is a norm-continuous family of bounded operators.
This follows from

Ae^{-\epsilon B^{2}}=[A,e^{-\epsilon B^{2}}]+e^{-\epsilon B^{2}}A\ ,

(4.53)

and Assertion
1
.

We now show Assertion
3
.
We know that

[0,\infty)\ni\epsilon\mapsto e^{-\epsilon B^{2}}:{\mathtt{dom}}(B^{2})\to H

is a continuous family of bounded operators.
We must show that

[0,\infty)\ni\epsilon\mapsto Ae^{-\epsilon B^{2}}:{\mathtt{dom}}(A^{2})\cap{\mathtt{dom}}(B^{2})\to H

is a continuous family of bounded operators.
This follows from (
4.53
) and the decomposition

{\mathtt{dom}}(A^{2})\cap{\mathtt{dom}}(B^{2})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle A}}{{\to}}{\mathtt{dom}}(B)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle e^{-\epsilon B^{2}}}}{{\to}}H\ ,

where the first map is continuous by
Lemma

4.38
.
3

∎

4.5
The Dirac-goes-to-Dirac principle

We consider a complete Riemannian manifold
M
with a uniformly continuous and controlled smooth function
f:M\to{\mathbb{R}}
such that
0
is a regular value. Then
N:=f^{-1}(\{0\})
is an embedded smooth manifold which decomposes

M
into closed subspaces
M_{\pm}:=f^{-1}({\mathbb{R}}^{\pm})
.
The pair of subsets
(M_{+},M_{-})
is a coarsely and uniformly excisive decomposition of
M
.
If
{\mathcal{Y}}
,
{\mathcal{Z}}
are a big families on
M
, then by
Lemma

2.39

this decomposition gives rise to a Mayer-Vietoris fibre sequence

K_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\mathscr{X}}(M_{-}\cap{\mathcal{Y}})\oplus K_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\mathscr{X}}(M_{+}\cap{\mathcal{Y}})\to K_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\mathscr{X}}({\mathcal{Y}})\xrightarrow{\delta^{MV}}\Sigma K_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\mathscr{X}}(N\cap{\mathcal{Y}})\ .

We assume that
\not{D}
is a Dirac operator of degree
n
which is positive away from a big family
{\mathcal{Z}}
(see
Definition

4.2
), and that
\Psi
is a potential which is very positive away from
{\mathcal{Y}}
(see
Definition

4.31
) and asymptotically constant away from
{\mathcal{Z}}
(see
Definition

4.27
).
We then consider the Callias-type operator

\not{D}+\Psi
. We shall assume that
N
has a tubular neighbourhood on which all data has a product structure.
This means that near
N
the operator has the form

\Sigma(\not{D}+\Psi)_{|N}
for some Callias type operator
(\not{D}+\Psi)_{|N}
of degree
n-1
on
N
, see (
4.12
) for notation and
Remark

4.40
below for more details.

Remark 4.40
.

One can reconstruct
(\not{D}+\Psi)_{|N}
as follows.
Assume that
\not{D}+\Psi
acts on the bundle
E\to M
of graded right
{\mathtt{Cl}}^{n}
-modules.
Let
\sigma
in
{\mathtt{End}}_{{\mathtt{Cl}}^{n}}(E_{|N})
be the Clifford multiplication by the normal vector pointing in direction
M_{+}
.
We let
e_{n}
be the
n
th generator of
{\mathtt{Cl}}^{n}
and
z
be the grading of
E
.
Then
iz\sigma e_{n}
is an even selfadjoint involution.
We let
E_{0}
be the
1
-eigensubbundle of
E_{|N}
for
iz\sigma e_{n}
.
The grading
z
induces by restriction a grading
z_{|N}
of
E_{0}
.
The right action of the subalgebra
{\mathtt{Cl}}^{n-1}
generated by remaining generators
e_{1},\dots,e_{n-1}
of
{\mathtt{Cl}}^{n}
preserves the subbundle and therefore induces a right action on
E_{0}
. The left Clifford multiplication by tangent vectors along
N
also preserves
E_{0}
and induces a Clifford bundle structure on
E_{0}\to N
.
Since
\sigma
and hence
iz\sigma e_{n}
are parallel by the product structure assumption, the connection of
E
induces a connection
\nabla_{0}
on
E_{0}
. We let
\not{D}_{|N}
denote the Dirac operator defined by this Dirac bundle structure on
E_{0}
.
The restriction of
\Psi
to
N
commutes with
iz\sigma e_{n}
(it anticommutes with
z
and
\sigma
and commutes with
e_{n}
) and therefore restricts to an endomorphism
\Psi_{|N}
on
E_{0}
.
In order to see that
\not{D}+\Psi
is isomorphic to
\Sigma(\not{D}_{|N}+\Psi_{|N})
near
N

we define an isomorphism

E_{0}\stimes{{\mathtt{Cl}}^{1}}\to E_{|N}\ ,\quad v\otimes(a+be_{n})\mapsto av+bve_{n}\ ,

(4.54)

where we consider
{\mathtt{Cl}}^{1}
as generated by
e_{n}
and
a,b
are in
{\mathbb{C}}
.
This map is compatible with the gradings, the right
{\mathtt{Cl}}^{n}
-actions, the left Clifford multiplication tangent vectors of
M
and the restriction of the connection along
N
.
We define the Clifford multiplication by
\sigma
on
E_{0}\stimes{{\mathtt{Cl}}^{1}}
by
\sigma(v\stimes c)=\pm v\stimes e_{n}c
, where
v
is homogenous of parity
\pm
.
Since we assume a product structure of all data near
N
it is then clear that

\not{D}+\Psi
is isomorphic to
\Sigma(\not{D}\oplus\Psi)_{|N}
in a neighbourhood of
N
.
\blacksquare

The assumptions on
\Psi
imply that
\Psi_{|N}
is very positive away from
N\cap{\mathcal{Y}}
and is asymptotically constant away from
N\cap{\mathcal{Z}}
.
Below in
Theorem

4.44
we will need the condition that
\not{D}_{|N}
is positive away from
N\cap{\mathcal{Z}}
.

Remark 4.41
.

In general we can not deduce the local positivity of
\not{D}_{|N}
from the local positivity of
\not{D}
.
For example, consider the spin Dirac operator on the Riemannian product
M:={\mathbb{R}}\times S^{1}
, where
S^{1}
has the bounding spin structure.
We further consider the decomposition along
N:={\mathbb{R}}\times S^{0}
. Then
\not{D}
is positive everywhere, i.e, away from
\emptyset
, but
\not{D}_{|N}
is not.

On the other hand, if the positivity of
\not{D}
away from
{\mathcal{Z}}
is caused by the zero order term in the Weizenboeck formula (
4.1
), then because of the assumption of a product structure,
\not{D}_{|N}
is positive away from
N\cap{\mathcal{Z}}
.
See
Lemma

4.43
below for a further example.

\blacksquare

Let
U
be a tubular neighbourhood of
N
on which all data has a product structure.
Assume that
\not{D}
is positive away from the big family
{\mathcal{Z}}
.

Definition 4.42
.

We say that the width of
U
goes to
\infty
away from
{\mathcal{Z}}
if for every
R
in
(0,\infty)
there exists a member
Z
of
{\mathcal{Z}}
and an isometric embedding
(-R,R)\times(N\setminus Z)\to U\setminus Z
compatible with the product structure of the bundle data.

N
(-R,R)\times(N\setminus Z)
M
N\setminus Z
Z

Lemma 4.43
.

If the width of
U
goes to
\infty
away from
{\mathcal{Z}}
and
\not{D}
is positive away from
{\mathcal{Z}}
, then
\not{D}_{|N}
is positive away from
N\cap{\mathcal{Z}}
.

Proof.

Since
\not{D}
is positive away from
{\mathcal{Z}}
, according to
Definition

4.2
there exists
a
in
(0,\infty)
and a member
Z
of
{\mathcal{Z}}
such that
\|\not{D}\phi\|^{2}\geq a^{2}\|\phi\|^{2}
for each
\phi
in
C^{\infty}_{c}(M\setminus\bar{Z},E)
.

Using the identification (
4.54
), we may consider sections
\phi
in
C^{\infty}(M,E)
supported on the image of
(-R,R)\times(N\setminus\bar{Z})
in product form
\phi=f\stimes\psi
with
f
in
C_{c}^{\infty}((-R,R))\otimes{\mathtt{Cl}}^{1}
and
\psi
in
C_{c}^{\infty}(N\setminus\bar{Z},E_{0})
.
For such a section
\phi
, we have

\displaystyle\|\not{D}\phi\|^{2}_{L^{2}(M,E)}

\displaystyle=\langle\not{D}^{2}\phi,\phi\rangle_{L^{2}(M,E)}=\langle-f^{\prime\prime}\otimes\psi+f\otimes\not{D}_{|N}^{2}\psi,f\otimes\psi\rangle_{L^{2}(M,E)}

(4.55)

\displaystyle=\|f^{\prime}\|^{2}_{L^{2}((-R,R),{\mathtt{Cl}}^{1})}\|\psi\|^{2}_{L^{2}(N,E_{0})}+\|f\|^{2}_{L^{2}((-R,R),{\mathtt{Cl}}^{1})}\|\not{D}_{|N}\psi\|^{2}_{L^{2}(N,E_{0})}.

For every
R
in
(0,\infty)
, we can find
f
in
C_{c}^{\infty}((-R,R))\otimes{\mathtt{Cl}}^{1}
with
\|f\|^{2}_{L^{2}}=1
and
\|f^{\prime}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\leq\frac{2}{R^{2}}
. Choose
R
such that
\frac{2}{R^{2}}\leq\frac{a^{2}}{2}
and enlarge the member
Z
so that there exists an isometric embedding
(-R,R)\times(N\setminus Z)\to U\setminus Z
and choose a function
f
as above corresponding to this
R
.
Then by (
4.55
), for all
\psi
in
C^{\infty}_{c}(N\setminus\bar{Z},E_{0})
, we have

\begin{aligned} \|\not{D}_{|N}\psi\|^{2}_{L^{2}(N,E_{0})}&=\|\not{D}\phi\|^{2}_{L^{2}(M,E)}-\|f^{\prime}\|^{2}_{L^{2}((-R,R),{\mathtt{Cl}}^{1})}\|\psi\|_{L^{2}(N,E_{0})}^{2}\\
&\geq a^{2}\|\phi\|^{2}_{L^{2}(M,E)}-\frac{2}{R^{2}}\|\psi\|_{L^{2}(N,E_{0})}^{2}\\
&\geq a^{2}\|f\|^{2}_{L^{2}((-R,R),{\mathtt{Cl}}^{1})}\|\psi\|^{2}_{L^{2}(N,E_{0})}-\frac{a^{2}}{2}\|\psi\|_{L^{2}(N,E_{0})}^{2}\\
&=\frac{a^{2}}{2}\|\psi\|_{L^{2}(N,E_{0})}^{2}\end{aligned}\ .

Hence
\not{D}_{|N}
is positive away from
N\cap{\mathcal{Z}}
.
∎

The following theorem is called the Dirac-goes-to-Dirac principle and has been observed in various variations, a first instance
goes back to
[
BDT89
]
.

Theorem 4.44
.

We assume that
\not{D}
is positive away from
{\mathcal{Z}}
, that
\not{D}_{|N}
is positive away from
N\cap{\mathcal{Z}}
, and that
\Psi
is very positive away from
{\mathcal{Y}}
.
If
N
has a tubular neighbourhood with product structure of uniform width, then

\delta^{MV}(\sigma_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(\not{D}+\Psi,\mathrm{on}\,{\mathcal{Y}}))=\beta^{-1}\sigma_{N\cap{\mathcal{Z}}}((\not{D}+\Psi)_{|N},\mathrm{on}\,N\cap{\mathcal{Y}})

in
K_{{\mathcal{Z}},-n-1}^{\mathscr{X}}(N\cap{\mathcal{Y}})
,

Proof.

We apply the relative index theorem (
Proposition

4.9
) to the following data:
We consider

M_{0}:=\tilde{M}\ ,\quad{\mathcal{Y}}_{0}:=\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\ ,\quad\not{D}_{0}:=\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi}\ ,

(4.56)

see (
4.29
).
Furthermore, we let

M_{1}:=({\mathbb{R}}\otimes N)^{\sim}\ ,\quad{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}:=({\mathbb{R}}\times(N\cap{\mathcal{Y}}))^{\sim}_{{\mathbb{R}}\times(N\cap{\mathcal{Z}})}\quad\not{D}_{1}:=(\Sigma(\not{D}+\Psi)_{|N})^{\sim}\ ,

where
(\cdots)^{\sim}
refers to applying the
Construction

4.10
.
Let
U\cong I\times N
be the tubular neighborhood of
N
with uniform width.
By assumption, we have an isomorphism of all data over
{\mathbb{R}}^{+}\times U\cong{\mathbb{R}}^{+}\times I\times N
.
Let
W_{0}\subset M_{0}
and
W_{1}\subset M_{1}
be the complements of these sets and let
{\mathcal{W}}_{i}:=\{W_{i}\}
be the big family generated by
W_{i}
.
One checks that the identification
e:M_{0}\setminus W_{0}\to M_{1}\setminus W_{0}

is a morphism of bornological coarse spaces.

Then by
Proposition

4.9
, we have

e_{*}\pi_{0}(\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D}_{0},\mathrm{on}\,{\mathcal{Y}}_{0}))=\pi_{1}(\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D}_{1},\mathrm{on}\,{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}))\ ,

(4.57)

in
K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1},{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\cap{\mathcal{W}}_{1})
, where
\pi_{i}:K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Y}}_{i})\to K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Y}}_{i},{\mathcal{Y}}_{i}\cap{\mathcal{W}}_{i})
are the projections.

We consider the Mayer-Vietoris boundary for the coarsely excisive compositions of
M_{i}
given by

(M_{0,-},M_{0,+})
with

M_{0,\pm}={\mathbb{R}}\times M_{\pm}\ ,

and
(M_{1,-},M_{1,+})
with

M_{1,\pm}={\mathbb{R}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{\pm}\times N\ .

By naturality of the Mayer-Vietoris boundaries we get the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Y}}_{0},{\mathcal{Y}}_{0}\cap{\mathcal{W}}_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma K\mathscr{X}(\tilde{N}\cap{\mathcal{Y}}_{0},\tilde{N}\cap{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{0}\cap{\mathcal{W}}_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1},{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\cap{\mathcal{W}}_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma K\mathscr{X}(\tilde{N}\cap{\mathcal{Y}}_{1},\tilde{N}\cap{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\cap{\mathcal{W}}_{1})\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta_{0}^{MV}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta_{1}^{MV}}

so by
(
4.57
), we get

\delta_{0}^{MV}(\pi_{0}(\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D}_{0},\mathrm{on}\,{\mathcal{Y}}_{0})))=\delta_{1}^{MV}(\pi_{1}(\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D}_{1},\mathrm{on}\,{\mathcal{Y}}_{1})))

(4.58)

in
K\mathscr{X}_{-n-1}(\tilde{N}\cap{\mathcal{Y}}_{0},\tilde{N}\cap{\mathcal{Y}}_{0}\cap{\mathcal{W}}_{0})\cong K\mathscr{X}_{-n-1}(\tilde{N}\cap{\mathcal{Y}}_{1},\tilde{N}\cap{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\cap{\mathcal{W}}_{1})
.

Lemma 4.45
.

We have

\delta_{1}^{MV}\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D}_{1},\mathrm{on}\,{\mathcal{Y}}_{1})=\beta^{-1}\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(((\not{D}+\Psi)_{|N})^{\sim},\mathrm{on}\,\tilde{N}\cap{\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\ .

(4.59)

in
K\mathscr{X}_{-n-1}(\tilde{N}\cap{\mathcal{Y}}_{1})
.

Proof.

The identity of underlying sets induces a map of bornological coarse spaces

{({\mathbb{R}}\otimes N)^{\sim}}\to{\mathbb{R}}\otimes\tilde{N}
. By
Proposition

4.13
the induced map in coarse
K
-homology sends

\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D}_{1},\text{on }{\mathcal{Y}}_{1})
to
\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\Sigma((\not{D}+\Psi)_{|N})^{\sim},\text{on }{\mathcal{Y}}_{1})
.
By naturality of the Mayer-Vietoris boundaries we get the first equality in

\displaystyle\delta_{1}^{MV}(\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D}_{1},\mathrm{on}\,{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}))

\displaystyle=\delta_{1}^{MV}(\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\Sigma((\not{D}+\Psi)_{|N})^{\sim},\mathrm{on}\,{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}))

\displaystyle=\beta^{-1}\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(((\not{D}+\Psi)_{|N})^{\sim},\mathrm{on}\,\tilde{N}\cap{\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\ ,

while the second one follows from the suspension theorem (
Proposition

4.8
).
∎

Combining (
4.58
) with (
4.59
) and using the definition of
\not{D}_{0}
and
{\mathcal{Y}}_{0}
in (
4.56
), we get

\pi_{0}(\delta_{0}^{MV}(\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\tilde{\smash{\not{D}}\vphantom{D}}+\tilde{\Psi},\mathrm{on}\,\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}})))=\beta^{-1}\pi_{1}(\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(((\not{D}+\Psi)_{|N})^{\sim},\mathrm{on}\,\tilde{N}\cap{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}))

Here we implicitly employed the fact that
\pi_{i}
intertwines the relative with the absolute Mayer-Vietoris boundary. Applying the map of coarse spaces
\iota:\tilde{M}\to{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(M)
to this equation and using naturality of the Mayer-Vietoris boundary,
Definition

4.29
yields the following result.

Corollary 4.46
.

We have

\pi_{0}(\delta^{MV}(\sigma_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(\not{D}+\Psi,\mathrm{on}\,{\mathcal{Y}})))=\pi_{0}(\beta^{-1}\sigma_{N\cap{\mathcal{Z}}}((\not{D}+\Psi)_{|N},\mathrm{on}\,N\cap{\mathcal{Y}}))\ .

(4.60)

in
K\mathscr{X}_{-n-1}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\infty}(N\cap{\mathcal{Y}}),{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(N\cap{\mathcal{Y}})\cap{\mathcal{W}}_{0})
.

We now observe that every member of
{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(N\cap{\mathcal{Y}})\cap{\mathcal{W}}_{0}
is contained in a member of
{\mathcal{O}}^{-}(N\cap{\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})
. Since these subsets are flasque the map

K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(N\cap{\mathcal{Y}})\cap{\mathcal{W}}_{0})\to K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\infty}(N\cap{\mathcal{Y}}))

vanishes and
the projection
\pi_{0}
is a split injection.
We can therefore omit
\pi_{0}
in (
4.60
) and get the desired equality.
∎

Remark 4.47
.

Even if we do not know that
\not{D}_{|N}
is positive away from
N\cap{\mathcal{Z}}

we have a class

\delta^{MV}\sigma_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(\not{D}+\Psi,\mathrm{on}\,{\mathcal{Y}})
in
K_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\mathscr{X}}(N\cap{\mathcal{Y}})

whose image under
K_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\mathscr{X}}(N\cap{\mathcal{Y}})\to K^{\mathscr{X}}(N\cap{\mathcal{Y}})
is

\beta^{-1}\sigma((\not{D}+\Psi)_{|N},\mathrm{on}\,N\cap{\mathcal{Y}})
.
\blacksquare

4.6
Decompositions and Mayer-Vietoris

We consider a bornological coarse space
M
(we use the symbol
M
since it will later be the bornological coarse space associated to a complete Riemannian manifold).
Let
\tilde{f}:M\to{\mathbb{R}}
be a controlled function.
Then we define the big families

{\mathcal{M}}_{\pm}:=\tilde{f}^{-1}(\{{\mathbb{R}}^{\pm}\})

on
M
and set
{\mathcal{Y}}:={\mathcal{M}}_{-}\cap{\mathcal{M}}_{+}
.
We consider the function

f:=\frac{\tilde{f}}{\sqrt{1+\tilde{f}^{2}}}\quad\mbox{in}\quad\ell^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}(M)

(4.61)

and observe that

(\frac{f+1}{2})^{2}-\frac{f+1}{2}\in\ell^{\infty}({\mathcal{Y}})\ ,

see
Definition

3.1
for notation.
The class of
\frac{f+1}{2}
in
C(\partial^{{\mathcal{Y}}}X)=\smash{\frac{\ell^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}(M)}{\ell^{\infty}({\mathcal{Y}})}}
is a projection
and represents a
K
-theory class

p:=\left[\frac{f+1}{2}\right]

(4.62)

in
K_{0}(C(\partial^{{\mathcal{Y}}}X))
, see
Remark

2.48
.

We have the pairing (
3.21
)

p\cap^{\mathscr{X}}-:K\mathscr{X}(M)\to\Sigma K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Y}})\ .

On the other hand, the decomposition of
M
into

({\mathcal{M}}_{-},{\mathcal{M}}_{+})
induces in view of (
2.11
) a Mayer-Vietoris fibre sequence

K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{M}}_{-})\oplus K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{M}}_{+})\to K\mathscr{X}(M)\xrightarrow{\partial^{MV}}\Sigma K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Y}})\ .

Lemma 4.48
.

We have an equivalence
p\cap^{\mathscr{X}}-\simeq\partial^{MV}:K\mathscr{X}(M)\to\Sigma K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Y}})
.

Proof.

The origin of the Mayer-Vietoris boundary map for coarse
K
-homology
K\mathscr{X}
is the excisiveness of the
{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)
-valued functor
K\mathscr{X}
on
\mathbf{BC}
. It sends the square

\textstyle{{\mathcal{Y}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{M}}_{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{M}}_{-}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M}

(4.63)

of big families or objects in
\mathbf{BC}
to the left square in

\textstyle{K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Y}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{M}}_{+})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{M}}_{+},{\mathcal{Y}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{M}}_{-})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K\mathscr{X}(M)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K\mathscr{X}(M,{\mathcal{M}}_{-})}

(4.64)

in
{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)
which has the property of being cocartesian. It extends to a map of fibre sequences as indicated so that the right vertical map is an equivalence.
The Mayer-Vietoris boundary map is by definition

\partial^{MV}:K\mathscr{X}(M)\to K\mathscr{X}(M,{\mathcal{M}}_{-})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\leftarrow}}K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{M}}_{+},{\mathcal{Y}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\partial}}{{\to}}\Sigma K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Y}})\ ,

(4.65)

where
\partial
is the boundary map associated to the upper fibre sequence.
Since we want to compare this map with a construction of
p\cap^{\mathscr{X}}-
defined on the level of
C^{*}
-categories
we must also express the
\partial^{MV}
in terms of
C^{*}
-categorical constructions.

The square (
4.63
)
yields an excisive square of
C^{*}
-categories
[
BE23
, Lemma 6.10]

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 27.89581pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-25.46524pt\raise (4.66)

Applying the
K
-theory functor for
C^{*}
-categories we get a square

\textstyle{K({\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq M))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K({\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{M}}_{+}\subseteq M))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K({\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{M}}_{-}\subseteq M))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K({\mathbf{C}}(M))}

which corresponds to the left push-out square in (
4.64
).
Unfolding the definition of
\nu
in (
3.19
) and (
3.22
) the map

p\cap^{\mathscr{X}}-
is given by the following composition

\displaystyle K\mathscr{X}(M)

\displaystyle\simeq K({\mathbf{C}}(M))\xrightarrow{p\otimes{\mathtt{id}}}K\left(\frac{\ell^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}(M)}{\ell^{\infty}({\mathcal{Y}})}\otimes{\mathbf{C}}(M)\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\nu}}{{\to}}K\left(\frac{{\mathbf{C}}(M)}{{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq M)}\right)\xrightarrow{\partial}\Sigma K({\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq M))\simeq\Sigma K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Y}})

We now argue with the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K({\mathbf{C}}(M))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K\Big(\frac{{\mathbf{C}}(M)}{{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{M}}_{-}\subseteq M)}\Big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K\Big(\frac{{\mathbf{C}}(M)}{{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq M)}\Big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K\Big(\frac{{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{M}}_{+}\subseteq M)}{{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq M)}\Big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Sigma}K({\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq M))}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu\circ(p\otimes{\mathtt{id}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

where the horizontal arrows are induced by the obvious projections and inclusions.
The lower triangle commutes by the naturality of the boundary operator for
C^{*}
-algebra
K
-theory for maps of exact sequences of
C^{*}
-categories. The upper right vertical morphism is the left vertical equivalence in (
4.64
). The
composition along the left part reflects the definition of
p\cap^{\mathscr{X}}-
, and the other composition is the definition (
4.65
) of
\partial^{MV}
.
∎

We now assume that
M
is a uniform bornological coarse space and that
\tilde{f}
is in addition uniformly continuous.
We define the closed subspaces

M_{\pm}:=f^{-1}({\mathbb{R}}^{\pm})
and set
N:=M_{+}\cap M_{+}
.

Then we have
p\in K_{0}(C(\partial^{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{u}M))
and

\partial p\in K^{1}({\mathcal{Y}})
, where
\partial
is the boundary map in
K
-theory associated to the exact sequence of
C^{*}
-algebras (
3.8
) and we use the grading convention
\pi_{-*}K(C_{u}({\mathcal{Y}}))\simeq K^{*}({\mathcal{Y}})
.

Let
{\mathcal{Z}}
be a second big family.
We have the coarse symbol pairing

\partial p\cap^{\mathscr{X}\sigma}-:K^{\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(M)\to\Sigma K^{\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}({\mathcal{Y}})

introduced in
Definition

3.40
.
By
Lemma

2.39
.
2
the decomposition
(M_{+},M_{-})
(which is coarsely and uniformly excisive) of
M
induces a Mayer-Vietoris fibre sequence

K^{\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(M_{+})\oplus K^{\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(M_{-})\to K^{\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(M)\xrightarrow{\delta^{MV}}\Sigma K_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\mathscr{X}}(N)\ .

We let

i^{\mathscr{X}}:K_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\mathscr{X}}(N)\to K_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\mathscr{X}}({\mathcal{Y}})\quad\mbox{and}\quad i:K\mathscr{X}(N\cap{\mathcal{Z}})\to K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})

(4.67)

denote the maps induced by the obvious inclusions. The square

\textstyle{K_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\mathscr{X}}(N)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{\mathscr{X}}}
\scriptstyle{a_{N,N\cap{\mathcal{Z}}}}
\textstyle{K_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\mathscr{X}}({\mathcal{Y}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}}}}
\textstyle{K\mathscr{X}(N\cap{\mathcal{Z}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i}
\textstyle{K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})}

(4.68)

commutes by the naturality of the index map
Definition

2.40
.

Lemma 4.49
.

We have an equivalence

\partial p\cap^{\mathscr{X}\sigma}-\simeq i^{\mathscr{X}}\circ\delta^{MV}:K^{\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(M)\to\Sigma K^{\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}({\mathcal{Y}})\ .

Proof.

We consider the following morphism of exact sequences of
C^{*}
-categories

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{u}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\infty}(M))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\infty}({\mathcal{Y}})\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(M))}{{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}^{-}({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(M))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\infty}({\mathcal{Y}})\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(M))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{u,{\mathcal{Y}}}(X)\otimes{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\infty}(M))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\infty}(M)\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(M))}{{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}^{-}({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(M))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\infty}(M)\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(M))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{u}(\partial_{u}^{{\mathcal{Y}}}M)\otimes{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\infty}(M))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\infty}(M)\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(M))}{{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\infty}({\mathcal{Y}})\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(M))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\infty}(M)\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(M))}{{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\infty}({\mathcal{Y}})\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(M))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces!}

where the left vertical sequence is obtained by tensoring (
3.8
) with
{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\infty}(M))
.
To explain the arrow marked “!”, notice that we have a map

\partial^{{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}({\mathcal{Y}})}{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(M)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\mathtt{pr}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\partial^{{\mathcal{Y}}}M\stackrel{{\scriptstyle c}}{{\longrightarrow}}\partial^{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{u}M\ ,

where the first map is the extension of the controlled map
{\mathtt{pr}}:{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(M)\to M
to a continuous map between the coronas (see
Remark

3.8
) and the second map is the comparison map (
3.9
).
The arrow marked “!” is then the composition
\nu\circ((c\circ{\mathtt{pr}})^{*}\otimes{\mathtt{id}})
, where
\nu
is the appropriate version of (
3.19
).

Applying
K
-theory and using the naturality of the boundary map in
K
-theory for morphisms of exact sequences of
C^{*}
-categories we get the two lower right commutative squares in

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K({\partial^{{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}({\mathcal{Y}})}{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(M)})\times K_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\mathscr{X}}(M)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K\left({C(\partial^{{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}({\mathcal{Y}})}{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(M))}\otimes{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\infty}(M))\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(\partial_{u}^{{\mathcal{Y}}}M)\times K^{\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(M)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K\left(\frac{{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\infty}(M))}{{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}({\mathcal{Y}})\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(M))}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(C(\partial_{u}^{{\mathcal{Y}}}M)\otimes{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\infty}(M)))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma K({\mathcal{Y}})\times K_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\mathscr{X}}(M)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K\left(\frac{{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\infty}(M)\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(M))}{{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}({\mathcal{Y}})\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(M))}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma K(C_{u}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\infty}(M)))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K\left(\frac{{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\infty}(M)\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(M))}{{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}({\mathcal{Y}})\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(M)}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma K\left(\frac{{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\infty}({\mathcal{Y}})\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(M))}{{\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}^{-}({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(M))}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma K({\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}({\mathcal{Y}})\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(M)))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(\nu)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K((c\circ{\mathtt{pr}})^{*})\times{\mathtt{id}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial\times{\mathtt{id}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(!)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K({(c\circ{\mathtt{pr}})^{*}}\times{\mathtt{id}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

The left horizontal maps are given by the symmetric monoidal structure of the
K
-theory functor for
C^{*}
-categories and the left squares commute by bi-exactness of this structure.

Finally, the top right square commutes by functoriality of
K
, by definition of the arrow marked “!”.

If we fix
p
in
K_{0}(\partial_{u}^{{\mathcal{Y}}}M)
, then the up-right-down composition is

(c\circ{\mathtt{pr}})^{*}p\cap^{\mathscr{X}}-:K^{\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(M)\to\Sigma K^{\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}({\mathcal{Y}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\Sigma K({\mathbf{C}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}({\mathcal{Y}})\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}^{\infty}(M)))\ .

By
Lemma

4.48
we get

{(c\circ{\mathtt{pr}})^{*}}p\cap^{\mathscr{X}}-\simeq\tilde{\partial}^{MV}:K_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\mathscr{X}}(M)\to\Sigma K_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\mathscr{X}}({\mathcal{Y}})\ ,

where
\tilde{\partial}_{MV}
is the Mayer-Vietoris boundary for
the decomposition of
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\infty}(M)
into
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\infty}({\mathcal{M}}_{+})
and
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\infty}({\mathcal{M}}_{-})
.
The down-right composition is

\partial p\cap^{\mathscr{X}\sigma}-:K^{\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(M)\to\Sigma K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\infty}(M))\to\Sigma K_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\mathscr{X}}({\mathcal{Y}})\ .
