which implies that

i^{\mathscr{X}}\circ\delta^{MV}\simeq\tilde{\partial}^{MV}
.
Combining these equivalences we get the assertion of the lemma.
∎

We now assume that
M
is represented by a complete Riemannian manifold, that
\tilde{f}:M\to{\mathbb{R}}
is smooth, and that
0
is a regular value. Then

N
is an embedded codimension-one submanifold of
M
with trivial normal bundle which separates
M
into the two components
M_{-}
and
M_{+}
.
We equip
N
with the induced Riemannian metric. Then the inclusion
i:N\to M

is a morphism of bornological coarse spaces.
We assume that
N
has a product tubular neighbourhod of uniform width.

If
\not{D}_{0}
is a generalized Dirac operator on
M
which is positive away from a big family
{\mathcal{Z}}
with symbol
\sigma_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(\not{D}_{0})
in
K^{\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(M)
, then by
Theorem

4.44
we know that

\delta^{MV}(\sigma_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(\not{D}_{0}))=\beta^{-1}\sigma_{N\cap{\mathcal{Z}}}({\not{D}_{0|N}})

in
K^{\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(N)
provided that
\not{D}_{0}
has a product structure on the product tubular neighbourhod of uniform width and

\not{D}_{0|N}
is also positive away from
N\cap{\mathcal{Z}}
. We can conclude a coarse version of Roe’s partitioned manifold index theorem
[
Roe88
]
.

Corollary 4.50
.

Under the assumption made above, we have an equality

i(\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D}_{0|N},\mathrm{on}\,N\cap{\mathcal{Z}}))\simeq\beta\cdot\partial^{MV}\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D}_{0},\mathrm{on}\,{\mathcal{Z}})

in
K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})
.

Proof.

Since the index map
Definition

2.40
is a natural transformation between
{\mathbf{Mod}}(KU)
-valued excisive functors it
commutes with Mayer-Vietoris maps. This is used in the marked equality below.
We calculate

\displaystyle i(\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D}_{0|N},\mathrm{on}\,N\cap{\mathcal{Z}}))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{jogergreffwfrf}}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle i(a_{N,{\mathcal{Z}}}(\sigma_{N\cap{\mathcal{Z}}}({\not{D}_{0|N}})))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{tkopherthtere9}}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\beta\cdot i(a_{N,{\mathcal{Z}}}(\delta^{MV}\sigma_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(\not{D}_{0})))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\beta\cdot\partial^{MV}a_{M,{\mathcal{Z}}}(\sigma_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(\not{D}_{0}))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{jogergreffwfrf}}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\beta\cdot\partial^{MV}\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D}_{0},\mathrm{on}\,{\mathcal{Z}})\ .

∎

Remark 4.51
.

Corollary

4.50
leads to the usual application to
obstructions against positive scalar curvature in the coarse equivalence class of
M
.
We assume that
i:N\to M
is a coarse embedding with a uniform tubular neighbourhood along
N
.
Then
i:K\mathscr{X}(N)\to K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Y}})
is an equivalence.
Assume that
M
is spin and let
\not{D}_{0}
be the spin Dirac operator.
Then
\not{D}_{0|N}
is again the spin Dirac operator on
N
.
If
\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D}_{0|N})\not=0
, then by
Corollary

4.50
and since
i
is an equivalence also
\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D}_{0})\not=0
.

We call two complete Riemannian metrics
g,g^{\prime}
on
M
coarsely equivalent if
(M,g)
and
(M,g^{\prime})
represent the same bornological coarse space.
If
g^{\prime}
is coarsely equivalent to
g
and
\not{D}^{\prime}_{0}
is the associated Dirac operator, then
\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D}_{0})=\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D}^{\prime}_{0})
.
We use here that two coarsely equivalent metrics induce the same index classes for the spin Dirac operators.
This can again be shown similarly as
Proposition

4.13
using the suspension theorem and the relative index theorem.

If
\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D}_{0|N})\not=0
, we can conclude that
M
can not have a metric
g^{\prime}
with uniformly positive scalar curvature in the coarse equivalence class of
M
.
It is important to note here that it is not required that
g^{\prime}
has a uniform tube at
N
, or even a product structure at
N
at all.
Otherwise the conclusion would be trivial since then the metric on
N
induced by
g^{\prime}
has uniformly positive scalar curvature and therefore
\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D}_{0|N})=0
.

Using the supports we obtain a new type of obstruction. We say that a class in
K\mathscr{X}(N)
comes from the big family
{\mathcal{Y}}
in
N
if it belongs to the image of

K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Y}})\to K\mathscr{X}(N)
.

Let
{\mathcal{Z}}
be a big family in
M
.

Corollary 4.52
.

If
\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D}_{0|N})
does not come from
N\cap{\mathcal{Z}}
, then there is no complete metric
g^{\prime}
in the coarse equivalence class of
g
of uniform positive scalar curvature on
M\setminus Z
for any member
Z
of
{\mathcal{Z}}
.
\blacksquare

Assume that
\not{D}_{0}
is a generalized Dirac operator of degree
k
on a bundle
E_{0}\to M
which is positive away from a big family
{\mathcal{Z}}
and that
\not{D}_{0|N}
is positive away from
N\cap{\mathcal{Z}}
. We further assume that
\smash{\tilde{f}}
is asymptotically constant away from
{\mathcal{Z}}
.
We define
\Psi_{0}:M\to{\mathtt{End}}_{{\mathtt{Cl}}^{1}}({\mathtt{Cl}}^{1})
by

\Psi_{0}(m):=\tilde{f}(m)i\sigma
, where
\sigma
in
{\mathtt{Cl}}^{1}
is the odd, anti-selfadjoint generator with
\sigma^{2}=-1
Then
\Psi_{0}
is very positive away from
{\mathcal{Y}}
and asymptotically constant away from
{\mathcal{Z}}
.
We define the bundle
E:=E_{0}\stimes{\mathtt{Cl}}^{1}
of
{\mathtt{Cl}}^{k+1}
-modules,
\not{D}:=\not{D}_{0}\stimes{\mathtt{id}}_{{\mathtt{Cl}}^{1}}
and
\Psi:={\mathtt{id}}_{E_{0}}\stimes\Psi_{0}
.

The function
\Psi_{0}
defines a class
[\Psi_{0}]
in
K^{1}({\mathcal{Y}})
. By
Theorem

4.32
we have

\sigma_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(\not{D}+\Psi,\mathrm{on}\,{\mathcal{Y}})=[\Psi_{0}]\cap^{\mathscr{X}\sigma}\sigma_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(\not{D}_{0})\ .

Combining (
2.27
), (
2.34
) and (
4.61
) we have the well-known formula for the
K
-theoretic boundary map

[\Psi_{0}]=\partial p\quad\mbox{in}\quad K^{1}({\mathcal{Y}})\ .

(4.69)

Proposition 4.53
.

In
K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})

we have

\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D}+\Psi,\mathrm{on}\,{\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})=\beta^{-1}i(\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D}_{0|N},\mathrm{on}\,N\cap{\mathcal{Z}}))

Proof.

We have the following chain of equalities:

\displaystyle\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D}+\Psi,\mathrm{on}\,{\mathcal{Y}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{qefewdad}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle a_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Z}}}(\sigma_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(\not{D}+\Psi,\mathrm{on}\,{\mathcal{Y}}))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{wegokwpergerfwefrwefwf}},\eqref{rgwergeggwgre}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle a_{{\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Z}}}(\partial p\cap^{\mathscr{X}\sigma}\sigma_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(\not{D}_{0}))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{erokgpwergrwefwerfwerfwer}}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle p\cap^{{\mathscr{X}}}a_{M,{\mathcal{Z}}}(\sigma_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(\not{D}_{0})))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{jogergreffwfrf}}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle p\cap^{\mathscr{X}}\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D}_{0},\mathrm{on}\,{\mathcal{Z}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{gojsekorpgergesrfrefs}}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\partial^{MV}\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D}_{0},\mathrm{on}\,{\mathcal{Z}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{gwergregwerferfw}}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\beta^{-1}i(\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D}_{0|N},\mathrm{on}\,N\cap{\mathcal{Z}}))\ .

∎

Remark 4.54
.

If
N
is compact, then
K({\mathcal{Y}})\simeq K\mathscr{X}(*)\simeq KU
and
K\mathscr{X}(N)\simeq KU
and under these indentifications

\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D}+\Psi,\mathrm{on}\,{\mathcal{Y}})
and

\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(D_{0|N})
are the usual Fredholm indices. If
k+1
is even, then the equality of indices

\mathrm{ind}(\not{D}+\Psi)=\mathrm{ind}(\not{D}_{0|N})
in
KU_{-k-1}
is a special case of Roe’s partitioned manifold index theorem
[
Roe88
]
, and also of the index theorem for Callias type operators shown in
[
Bun95
]
.

\blacksquare

4.7
The multi-partitioned index theorem

Let
M
be a uniform bornological coarse space with a big family
{\mathcal{Z}}
.
Assume that we have a finite family
\tilde{f}:=(\tilde{f}_{i})_{i=1,\dots,n}
of uniformly continuous and controlled functions

\tilde{f}_{i}:M\to{\mathbb{R}}
. Then we can iterate the constructions from
Section

4.6
. To
\tilde{f}_{i}
we associate the big families
{\mathcal{M}}_{i,\pm}
, as in (
4.62
),

{\mathcal{Y}}_{i}
, and the class and
p_{i}
in
K^{0}(\partial^{{\mathcal{Y}}_{i}}_{u}X)
. For a subset
I
of the poset
\langle n\rangle:=\{1,\dots,n\}
, we further define

{{\mathcal{Y}}_{I}:=\bigcap_{i\in I}{\mathcal{Y}}_{i}\ .}

If
i\notin I
, then the decomposition
({\mathcal{Y}}_{I}\cap{\mathcal{M}}_{i,-},{\mathcal{Y}}_{I}\cap{\mathcal{M}}_{i,+})
of
{\mathcal{Y}}_{I}

induces a Mayer-Vietoris boundary

{\partial^{MV}_{i}:\Sigma^{|I|}K_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\mathscr{X}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{I})\to\Sigma^{|I|+1}K_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\mathscr{X}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{I\cup\{i\}})\ .}

We avoid to use the embeddings (
4.67
) by considering excision with respect to the decomposition into two big families instead to the closed subspaces themselves, see e.g. the right triangle in (
4.19
).
We have
\partial p_{i}\in K^{1}({\mathcal{Y}}_{i})
and
can form the product

{\partial p_{i_{k}}\cup\dots\cup\partial p_{i_{1}}\quad\mbox{in}\quad K^{|I|}({\mathcal{Y}}_{I})\ ,\qquad\text{for}\qquad I=\{i_{1}<\dots<i_{k}\}\ .}

By an iterated application of
Lemma

3.46

and the version of
Lemma

4.49
for
M
replaced by a big family
we get:

Corollary 4.55
.

For
I=\{i_{1}<\dots<i_{k}\}\subseteq\langle n\rangle
,
we have an equivalence of functors

(\partial p_{i_{k}}\cup\dots\cup\partial p_{i_{1}})\cap^{\mathscr{X}\sigma}-\simeq{\partial}_{i_{k}}^{MV}\circ\dots\circ{\partial}^{MV}_{i_{1}}:K_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\mathscr{X}}(X)\to\Sigma^{|I|}K^{\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{I})\ .

Let
{{{\mathcal{W}}}}
be a big family on
M
with the property

\tilde{f}({{\mathcal{W}}})\subseteq{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}
so that
\tilde{f}
becomes a morphism

\tilde{f}:(M,{{\mathcal{W}}})\to({\mathbb{R}}^{n},{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}})
in
\mathbf{BC}^{(2)}
.
We then get an induced map

\partial\tilde{f}:\partial^{{{\mathcal{W}}}}M\to\partial_{h}{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\to S^{n-1}\ .

If we perform the constructions above with the coordinate functions
(x_{i})_{i=1,\dots,n}
of
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
instead of
(f_{i})_{i=1,\dots,n}
, then we get big families
\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{i}
on
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
and classes
\partial\tilde{p}_{i}\in K^{1}(\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{i})

such that
(\partial\tilde{p}_{n}\cup\dots\cup\partial\tilde{p}_{1})\in K^{n}(\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\langle n\rangle}})\cong K_{c}^{n}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})\cong{\mathbb{Z}}
is a generator.
In view of the well-understood long exact sequence in
K
-theory

K^{*}_{c}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})\to K^{*}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})\to K^{*}(S^{n-1})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\partial}}{{\to}}K^{*+1}_{c}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})

there exists a unique class
\tilde{u}
in
K^{n-1}(S^{n-1})
such that
\tilde{u}_{|*}=0
(i.e.,
\tilde{u}
is in the reduced summand) and
\partial\tilde{u}=(\partial\tilde{p}_{{n}}\cup\dots\cup\partial\tilde{p}_{1})
. We set
u:=(\partial\tilde{f})^{*}\tilde{u}
in
K^{n}({\mathcal{Y}}_{{\langle n\rangle}})
and conclude that
\partial u=(\partial p_{n}\cup\dots\cup\partial p_{1})
.

Corollary 4.56
.

The following square commutes:

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 26.77087pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-19.83337pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\raise-16.20029pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.52084pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{a_{{{\mathcal{W}}},{\mathcal{Z}}}}$}}}\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 50.77087pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Sigma^{n}K_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{\mathscr{X}}({{\mathcal{W}}}\cap{\mathcal{Y}}_{\langle n\rangle})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 127.56844pt\raise-16.20029pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 3.13194pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{a_{{{\mathcal{W}}}\cap{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\langle 53.0243pt\raise-26.19228pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.20831pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{u\cap^{\mathscr{X}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 80.77087pt\raise-32.40059pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 50.77087pt\raise-32.40059pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 80.77087pt\raise-32.40059pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Sigma^{n}K\mathscr{X}({{\mathcal{W}}}\cap{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\langle n\rangle}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ .

We assume that
M
comes from a complete Riemannian manifold.
We consider a Callias type generalized Dirac operator
\not{D}+\Psi
such that
\Psi
is very positive away from the big family
{{\mathcal{W}}}
and asymptotically constant away from the big family
{\mathcal{Z}}
, and
\not{D}
is positive away from
{\mathcal{Z}}
.
We assume that
\tilde{f}
is smooth and has
0
in
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
as a regular value, and we set
N:=\tilde{f}^{-1}(\{0\})
.

Corollary 4.57
.

If
N
has a tubular neighbourhood of uniform width with product structures, then in
K\mathscr{X}({{\mathcal{W}}}\cap{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\langle n\rangle}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})
we have the equality

u\cap^{\mathscr{X}}\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D}+\Psi,\mathrm{on}\,{{\mathcal{W}}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})\simeq\beta^{-n}i(\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}((\not{D}+\Psi)_{|N},\mbox{on $N\cap{{\mathcal{W}}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}}$ }))\ .

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle u\cap^{\mathscr{X}}\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D}+\Psi,\mathrm{on}\,{{\mathcal{W}}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{qefewdad}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle u\cap^{\mathscr{X}}a_{{{\mathcal{W}}},{\mathcal{Z}}}(\sigma_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(\not{D}+\Psi,\mathrm{on}\,{{\mathcal{W}}}))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{wrekopgpergrefwrefw}}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle a_{{{\mathcal{W}}}\cap{{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\langle n\rangle}}},{\mathcal{Z}}}({\partial}_{n}^{MV}\circ\dots\circ{\partial}^{MV}_{1}\sigma_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(\not{D}+\Psi,\mathrm{on}\,{{\mathcal{W}}}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{{{\mathcal{W}}}\cap{{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\langle n\rangle}}},{\mathcal{Z}}}({i^{\mathscr{X}}\delta}_{n}^{MV}\circ\dots\circ{\delta}^{MV}_{1}\sigma_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(\not{D}+\Psi,\mathrm{on}\,{{\mathcal{W}}}))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{tkopherthtere9}}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\beta^{-n}a_{{{\mathcal{W}}}\cap{\mathcal{Y}}_{1,\dots,n},{\mathcal{Z}}}(i^{\mathscr{X}}\sigma_{N\cap{\mathcal{Z}}}((\not{D}+\Psi)_{|N},\mathrm{on}\,N\cap{{\mathcal{W}}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}}))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{vfdsvervfdvsdfvsdvdf}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\beta^{-n}ia_{N\cap{{\mathcal{W}}},{\mathcal{Z}}}(\sigma_{N\cap{\mathcal{Z}}}((\not{D}+\Psi)_{|N},\mathrm{on}\,N\cap{{\mathcal{W}}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}}))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{qefewdad}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\beta^{-n}i(\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}((\not{D}+\Psi)_{|N},\mathrm{on}\,N\cap{{\mathcal{W}}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}}))\ .

∎

Example 4.58
.

We assume that
{{\mathcal{W}}}\cap{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\langle n\rangle}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}}\subseteq{\mathcal{B}}
. Then
for every member
W
in
{{\mathcal{W}}}\cap{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\langle n\rangle}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}}
the restriction
\tilde{f}_{|W}:W\to{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
is proper.
Consequently we get a map

t_{{\tilde{f}}}:K\mathscr{X}({{\mathcal{W}}}\cap{\mathcal{Y}}_{{\langle n\rangle}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\tilde{f}}_{*}}}{{\longrightarrow}}K\mathscr{X}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\tilde{u}\cap^{\mathscr{X}}-}}{{\simeq}}\Sigma^{n}K\mathscr{X}({\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}})\simeq\Sigma^{n}KU

(4.70)

If
\not{D}+\Psi
is a Callias type operator of degree
k
as above, then
following
[
SZ18
, Def. 1.3]
we define the multipartitioned index by

t_{{\tilde{f}}}(u\cap^{\mathscr{X}}\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D}+\Psi,\mathrm{on}\,{{\mathcal{W}}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}}))\in\pi_{-n-k}KU\ .

We furthermore have

N\cap{{\mathcal{W}}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}}\subseteq N\cap{\mathcal{B}}
and therefore

{\tilde{f}}_{|N,*}\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}((\not{D}+\Psi)_{|N},\mathrm{on}\,N\cap{{\mathcal{W}}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})\in K\mathscr{X}_{n-k}({\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}})\simeq\pi_{n-k}KU\ .

The following consequence of
Corollary

4.57
is a generalization of the multi-partition index theorem
[
Zei17
, Thm. 2.7]
,
[
SZ18
, Thm. 1.4]
.

Corollary 4.59
.

Under the assumptions of
Corollary

4.57
we have

t_{{\tilde{f}}}(u\cap^{\mathscr{X}}\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}(\not{D}+\Psi,\mathrm{on}\,{{\mathcal{W}}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}}))=\beta^{-n}{\tilde{f}}_{|N,*}\mathrm{ind}\mathscr{X}((\not{D}+\Psi)_{|N},\mathrm{on}\,N\cap{{\mathcal{W}}}\cap{\mathcal{Z}})\ .

4.8
Slant products

[
EWZ22
]
.

X,Y
be two uniform bornological coarse spaces.

be the bornology of

and consider the big family

on

. Pull-back of functions along the projection

)
